## Deferred from: code review of story 16.0 (2026-06-16)

Story 16.0 added the `--check` (no-write drift) / `--force` (gated write) modes + footgun guard to `scripts/gen-governance-baseline.mjs` (Epic 15 retro AI#3 / Rule #25). One HIGH-value robustness gap was auto-fixed inline during this review (see story Review Findings — `--check` now refuses a false "OK" if the committed `GOVERNANCE_BASELINE` import yields a non-iterable/empty export). The frozen baseline stayed git-clean (141 keys / `1e62c5ad5bf7`); the new test (4) and the authoritative `governance.test.ts` drift guard (41) both pass.

The following items are DEFERRED — none is a current correctness/security bug; all are tracked for a future generator/drift-test de-duplication or hardening pass:

- **[CR 16.0-1 / LOW — DEFERRED, known/out of scope (CR 15.0-5)]** Lock-step divergence between the generator's robust `unwrapActionOptions` (peels `ZodOptional`/`ZodDefault`/`ZodNullable`) on the `--check` path and `governance.test.ts:555`'s bare `tool.inputSchema?.shape?.action?.options` read. On today's all-bare action surface they AGREE (verified: `--check` and the drift test both green against the same surface). They would only diverge if a future tool wraps its `action` enum (`.optional()`/`.default()`), at which point `--check` would emit per-action keys while the test collapses to the bare tool key. This is the pre-existing CR 15.0-5 gap; Story 16.0 Dev Notes explicitly scoped closing it OUT and instructed not to regress the generator side (it did not). Suggested resolution: de-duplicate the two derivations into one shared helper (e.g. export the generator's `unwrapActionOptions` / `deriveLiveKeys` and have `governance.test.ts` import it) so the CLI `--check` and the in-suite drift guard can never disagree. `scripts/gen-governance-baseline.mjs:57` (unwrap) vs `packages/shared/src/__tests__/governance.test.ts:555` (bare read).
- **[CR 16.0-2 / LOW — DEFERRED, blocked by additive constraint]** The `--check` failure branch (a vanished frozen key → exit 1 + `VANISHED_HINT` regression guidance) has no direct CLI-level automated coverage. The new test (`governance-baseline-check.test.ts`) covers only the success + refusal paths and explicitly documents (lines 130-137) that reproducing the vanished-key scenario would require mutating the real built server dists OR stubbing the frozen `governance-baseline.ts` — both forbidden by Story 16.0's strictly-additive constraint (AC 16.0.7). The same one-directional logic IS exercised by the mirrored `governance.test.ts` drift guard, but against an in-process re-implementation (`deriveBaselineFromDists`), not the real CLI exit-code path. Suggested resolution: when the generator's derivation is extracted into an importable helper (CR 16.0-1), add a unit test that feeds it a synthetic live-key set missing a committed key and asserts the exit-1 / hint behavior — no dist/frozen-file perturbation needed. `packages/shared/src/__tests__/governance-baseline-check.test.ts:130-137`; `scripts/gen-governance-baseline.mjs:269-281`.
- **[CR 16.0-3 / LOW — DISMISSED]** `--check --force` passed together silently runs `--check` only (the safe verify path wins; `--force` write is suppressed) with no conflict diagnostic; and unknown/typo flags (`--chek`, case-sensitive `--Force`) fall through to the refusal path (benign, non-zero exit with guidance). The safe path always wins and the refusal is non-destructive, so this is correct-by-design for a build-time generator; adding flag-conflict / unknown-flag validation is gold-plating. No action. `scripts/gen-governance-baseline.mjs:56-58`.

## Deferred from: code review of story 16.1 (2026-06-16)

Story 16.1 added `iris_process_manage` (`get`/`terminate`/`suspend`/`resume`) on `@iris-mcp/ops` plus the `ProcessGet`/`ProcessManage` ObjectScript handlers on `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Monitor`. Three MED handler-correctness findings were auto-fixed inline during this review (see story Review Findings: exact-match action validation replacing the substring `[`-contains test that let comma-injected actions fall through the dispatch `Else` to `Resume()`; fail-CLOSED guard when `%SYS.ProcessQuery.%OpenId` fails in the safety check; one-time numeric `pid` coercion so the string-keyed `%ExistsId`/`%OpenId` and the numeric `$JOB` self-guard can never disagree). All three were verified live over HTTP on HSCUSTOM (non-numeric/negative pid rejected; `"suspend,resume"` rejected as invalid action; protected daemon 3736 terminate REFUSED and stayed RUNW). Bootstrap regenerated `3a395abc1eba` → `d4e197ef5ffc`; frozen `governance-baseline.ts` stayed git-clean (`1e62c5ad5bf7`, 141 keys); `--check` exit 0; ops 186 + shared bootstrap 47 green.

The following items are DEFERRED — none is a current correctness/security bug:

- **[CR 16.1-1 / LOW — DEFERRED, documented intentional]** The TS tool declares an optional `namespace` param and forwards it on the wire (`&namespace=` for `get`, `body.namespace` for control), but `ProcessGet`/`ProcessManage` never read it — both hard-switch to `%SYS` unconditionally, so the value is silently discarded. This is a deliberate design choice (process inspection/control is %SYS-scoped against the system-wide process table; the tool description states the param is "usually omitted") and AC 16.1.3 lists `namespace` as an optional input without mandating a behavioral effect, so it is AC-compliant. The residual gap is only that an accepted-but-ignored param is mildly misleading. Suggested resolution: either drop `namespace` from the schema, or tighten the description to state explicitly that the value has no effect (rather than "usually omitted"). `packages/iris-ops-mcp/src/tools/process.ts:61-67,101-103,160`; `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` ProcessGet/ProcessManage.
- **[CR 16.1-2 / LOW — DEFERRED, inherent to the domain]** TOCTOU window: `ProcessManage` queries live process state three separate times (`%SYS.ProcessQuery.%ExistsId`, then `%SYS.ProcessQuery.%OpenId` for the guard flags, then `SYS.Process.%OpenId` for control); a target can exit or transition between steps. The post-fix code now fails CLOSED if the guard-flag open fails, and a vanished process at the final `SYS.Process.%OpenId` yields a clean "Unable to open process … for control" error — so the worst case is a benign refusal/error, never an unguarded control action. True atomicity is not available in the `SYS.Process` API. No action; accepted as the standard pattern for live process control. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` ProcessManage.
- **[CR 16.1-3 / LOW — DEFERRED, perf only]** `ProcessGet` reads `MemoryUsed` (and could read other mailbox-requiring columns) via `%SYS.ProcessQuery.%OpenId`, which per the IRIS class doc sends a mailbox message to the target process and "may take several seconds" for some columns; the sibling `JobsList` avoids this by SELECTing only no-mailbox columns over SQL. This affects single-PID `get` latency only, not correctness. Suggested resolution: if `get` latency is ever reported as an issue, drop `memoryUsedKB` (or move the detail read to an SQL projection of the no-mailbox columns). No action now. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` ProcessGet.
- **[CR 16.1-4 / LOW — DISMISSED]** Self-PID guard protects `$JOB` (the worker serving the request) rather than the external MCP-client process or sibling web-app pool workers. `$JOB` is the only PID the handler can meaningfully self-protect and matches AC 16.1.4's intent ("the calling process"); protecting an arbitrary unrelated OS process is out of the tool's scope. No action.

One MED documentation discrepancy (story-doc test counts 17/2 vs actual 23/3) was fixed inline during review. ops 212 + shared bootstrap 47 green.

The following items are DEFERRED — none is a current correctness/security bug:

- **[CR 16.2-1 / LOW — DEFERRED, documented intentional (mirrors CR 16.1-1)]** The TS tool declares an optional `namespace` param and forwards it in the body (`body.namespace`, with a passthrough test), but `DatabaseAction` never reads it — the handler hard-switches to `%SYS` unconditionally, so the value is silently discarded. Database operations are genuinely %SYS-scoped: `SYS.Database` classmethods key on the directory path and run in `%SYS` regardless of the caller's namespace, so honoring a caller namespace would be meaningless. AC 16.2.3 lists `namespace` as an optional input without mandating a behavioral effect, and the description says "usually omitted" → AC-compliant. Identical pattern/resolution to CR 16.1-1. Suggested resolution: fold into the same future pass — either drop `namespace` from the schema across the ops %SYS-scoped tools, or tighten the descriptions to state the value has no effect. `packages/iris-ops-mcp/src/tools/database.ts:108-113,206`; `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` DatabaseAction.
- **[CR 16.2-2 / LOW — DEFERRED, clean-envelope path covers it]** No range validation on the numeric params: Zod uses bare `z.number()` (no `.min()`/`.max()`) for `percentFull`/`targetSize`/`initialSize`, and the handler coerces with bare `+tBody.%Get(...)`. So `percentFull:-50`/`150`, negative `targetSize`, or `initialSize:0` flow straight to the `SYS.Database` call. NOT a crash/corruption risk: each API returns a `%Status` that is propagated via `SanitizeError`, with the `Catch` block as backstop for any `<...>` runtime error → the caller always gets a clean error envelope (the HIGH-if-violated guard invariant is satisfied). Edge Case Hunter rated these HIGH assuming an unguarded crash; downgraded after confirming the clean-envelope path. Suggested resolution: add `.min()/.max()` (e.g. percentFull 1-100) and a non-negative guard in a future input-hardening pass for friendlier pre-flight rejection. `packages/iris-ops-mcp/src/tools/database.ts:90-107`; `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` DatabaseAction.
- **[CR 16.2-3 / LOW — DEFERRED, cosmetic]** The compact/truncate ByRef outputs (`tMbProcessed`/`tMbCompressed`/`tReturnSize`) are pre-initialized to 0 before the call, so a failure that leaves them untouched is indistinguishable from a genuine zero-result. In practice this is invisible to the caller: when `tActionSC` is non-OK the handler renders the SanitizeError envelope and the `success` result object (carrying the zeroed counters) is never rendered — the success path does not run on failure. Cosmetic only. No action. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` DatabaseAction.

## Deferred from: Story 16.3 (`iris_backup_manage`) — restore action (2026-06-16)

Story 16.3 added `iris_backup_manage` (`run`/`freeze`/`thaw`/`listHistory`) on `@iris-mcp/ops` plus the `BackupManage` ObjectScript handler on `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Monitor` (+ `POST /monitor/backup/manage` route). The following is DEFERRED by design (Rule #16, AC 16.3.3) — it is NOT a bug:

- **[Story 16.3 / restore — DEFERRED, verify-first (Rule #16)]** A `restore` action is intentionally NOT implemented. Pre-dev research against `irissys/Backup/General.cls` (doc comments L47–L65) confirmed IRIS restore is **interactive** (`^DBREST` database-restore utility / `CLUMENU^JRNRESTO` journal-restore routine) and has **no scriptable `RestoreDatabase()`-style classmethod** on `Backup.General` — the class explicitly directs callers to "restore the database files using your external restore mechanism." Shipping a half-working restore would be worse than none. **Decision:** restore is absent from the tool's action enum, AND the `BackupManage` handler defends against an `action="restore"` request with a clear "Restore is not supported via this tool — use the IRIS restore utility / Management Portal restore" message (no crash). The tool description states the same. **Suggested follow-up:** if scriptable restore is ever required, a dedicated handler would need to wrap the interactive utilities (driving `^DBREST` / `CLUMENU^JRNRESTO` non-interactively is non-trivial and risky), or expose the operator-facing Management Portal restore as the supported path. `packages/iris-ops-mcp/src/tools/backup.ts`; `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` BackupManage.

- **[Story 16.3 / run task-name reality — DOCUMENTED, Rule #16 live-probe outcome]** AC 16.3.4 originally specified `backupType` (full/incremental/cumulative) as the `run` selector. Live-probe (2026-06-16) found there are NO predefined/shipped backup task names on a stock IRIS instance (`^SYS("BUTASKS")` undefined; `%SYS.Task` has no backup-type rows). `Backup.General.StartTask` runs a USER-DEFINED task BY NAME, and the type is a property of that task definition — it cannot be auto-mapped to a guessed shipped name. The story spec + AC 16.3.4 were amended in place (Rule #5): `run` takes a required `taskName`; `backupType` is kept only as an optional informational field. The handler/tool reflect the amendment. Not a deferred bug — recorded for retrospective traceability of the spec-vs-reality correction.

- **[CR 16.3 / LOW — DEFERRED, mirrors CR 16.1-1 / CR 16.2-1]** As with the sibling %SYS-scoped ops tools, `iris_backup_manage` declares an optional `namespace` param and forwards it (`body.namespace`), but `BackupManage` hard-switches to `%SYS` and never reads it (backup operations are instance-wide / %SYS-scoped). AC-compliant (optional input, no mandated behavioral effect; description says "usually omitted"). Fold into the same future pass that resolves CR 16.1-1 / CR 16.2-1. `packages/iris-ops-mcp/src/tools/backup.ts`; `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` BackupManage.

- **[CR 16.3 / HIGH — RESOLVED in code review, 2026-06-16]** `BackupManage` listHistory decoded every `^SYS("BUHISTORY")` index node's timestamp via `$ZDateTime((tKey\1000000)_","_(tKey#1000000), 3)` with no per-entry guard. The decode is correct for well-formed `hindex` values (verified `days*1000000+seconds` against `irissys/Backup/General.cls:366-367` + `irissys/DBACKB.int:38`), but a single corrupt / foreign-written / out-of-$HOROLOG-range subscript (e.g. seconds ≥ 86400, or a non-canonical-numeric key passing the old `+tKey>0` skip) would throw `<ILLEGAL VALUE>` INSIDE the `While` loop and propagate to the outer `Catch`, failing the ENTIRE read instead of degrading one row. **Fix applied:** (a) the index-node filter is now `(tKey = +tKey) && (tKey = (tKey\1)) && (+tKey > 0)` — accepts only canonical positive integers, skipping the `0` metadata node AND any non-numeric subscript; (b) the `$ZDateTime` render is wrapped in a per-entry `Try/Catch` that degrades a bad node to `timestamp:""` rather than aborting. Redeployed + recompiled clean on HSCUSTOM; `gen:bootstrap` re-run (`04984d638f8d` → `fe972c4cb317`); `bootstrap.test.ts` green; frozen baseline git-clean. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` BackupManage (listHistory branch).

- **[CR 16.3 / MED — DEFERRED, "silently-ignored input"]** The `run` path reads `tDevice = tBody.%Get("device")` (and the TS tool forwards `body.device`), but `BackupManage` calls `Backup.General.StartTask(tTaskName, tJobBackup, 1)` WITHOUT the device argument — so a caller-supplied `device` override is silently dropped (the request still succeeds). This is the same class as the deferred `namespace`/`backupType`-informational pattern: the scriptable `StartTask` path runs a USER-DEFINED task BY NAME, and the backup destination is a property of that task definition, so a per-call device override is not part of the verified API path. AC-compliant (optional input). Suggested follow-up: either wire `device` into `StartTask`'s 4th positional `Device` arg (verify-first whether it overrides the task's configured destination), or drop `device` from the tool schema + body to avoid the false impression of control. Fold into the same ops-tool cleanup pass as the `namespace` deferral. `packages/iris-ops-mcp/src/tools/backup.ts`; `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` BackupManage.

- **[CR 16.3 / LOW — DEFERRED, theoretical, needs live confirmation]** `thaw` forwards a caller-supplied `password` to `Backup.General.ExternalThaw(tLogFile, tUsername, tPassword)`, and any non-OK `%Status` flows straight through `SanitizeError` to the client (Rule #9 passthrough). IF `ExternalThaw` ever embeds the submitted password in its auth-mismatch error text, that value would be echoed back. No evidence it does (the method body is kernel-generated; the doc only says the password is "checked against" the freeze password), so this is theoretical — and matches the narrow password-redaction concern that Epic 11's retro self-audit explicitly declined to codify as a rule (not a general pattern). Patching redaction blind risks corrupting legitimate diagnostic text (Rule #9 minimum-length-gate caution). Suggested follow-up: during a maintenance-window freeze/thaw round-trip smoke, capture the actual `ExternalThaw` mismatch error text; add field-specific password redaction ONLY if the password is observed in it. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` BackupManage (thaw branch).

## Deferred from: code review of story 17.1 (2026-06-16)

Story 17.1 added `iris_default_settings_manage` (`list`/`get`/`set`/`delete`) on `@iris-mcp/interop` plus the `DefaultSettingsList`/`DefaultSettingsManage` ObjectScript handlers on `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Interop` (+ GET/POST `/interop/defaultsettings` routes). Three-layer review (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Acceptance Auditor): all four in-scope ACs (17.1.1–17.1.4) PASS; the flagged `delete` → `"deleted"` output naming is correct; the governance test genuinely drives `McpServerBase.handleToolCall` with the real schema + `mutates` (writes default-disabled, reads enabled, opt-in flips per-action). TWO patches applied inline (see story Review Findings): (1) `||` IdKey-delimiter injection guard in `DefaultSettingsManage`; (2) clarified `list` filter `"*"` semantics in the tool description. Redeploy + `gen:bootstrap` re-roll `56f492db456d` → `8c748712e247` (Rule #24, bootstrap.test.ts green); frozen governance baseline `1e62c5ad5bf7` git-clean, `--check` exit 0 (Rule #23/#25); strictly additive (`iris_production_item`/`ItemManage` byte-for-byte unchanged, interop 19→20).

The following items are DEFERRED — none is a current correctness/security bug:

- **[CR 17.1-1 / MED — DEFERRED, needs live-HTTP test]** The `||` IdKey-delimiter injection guard added to `DefaultSettingsManage` (Interop.cls ~1951) has no automated test. The TS unit tests use mocked HTTP, so a TS-layer test would only assert the slot value is forwarded in the body — it cannot exercise the server-side guard. Genuine coverage requires the lead's live-HTTP smoke (Rule #26): send a get/set/delete with a slot containing `||` and assert the server REJECTS it ("Key slot values may not contain the '||' delimiter") changing nothing. Suggested resolution: add that rejection assertion to the Story 17.1 / Epic 17 live smoke. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Interop.cls` DefaultSettingsManage.
- **[CR 17.1-2 / MED — DISMISSED-AS-INTENTIONAL, recorded]** An explicit empty-string key slot (`production:""` etc.) is coerced to the wildcard `"*"` row (`Set:tProduction="" tProduction="*"`), identical to omitting the slot (the class InitialExpression is `"*"`). A caller who accidentally sends `""` writes/deletes the broad `*` catch-all row rather than erroring. Low practical risk: the TS schema is `z.string()` and the documented contract is "omit for the `*` default," so this is a deliberate-caller path. Suggested follow-up (optional): if stricter input hygiene is ever wanted, reject explicit `""` slots distinctly from omitted ones. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Interop.cls` DefaultSettingsManage:1943-1950.
- **[CR 17.1-3 / LOW — DISMISSED, matches existing handlers]** `deployable` is coerced at the REST layer via `+tBody.%Get("deployable")`, so a DIRECT REST caller (bypassing the Zod schema) sending the STRING `"true"` would store `Deployable=0` (opposite of intent). MCP callers are safe — the TS schema enforces `z.boolean()`. This mirrors the established `+`-coercion in `ItemManage`/`AutoStart` in the same class. Direct-REST input hardening is a cross-handler concern, not this story. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Interop.cls` DefaultSettingsManage:2023.

## Deferred from: code review of story 17.2 (2026-06-16)

Story 17.2 extended the EXISTING `iris_production_item` tool (`@iris-mcp/interop`) + `ItemManage` (`ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Interop`) with `add`/`remove` actions (`mutates:{add,remove:"write"}`, default-disabled) and arbitrary host/adapter settings routed to `Ens.Config.Setting` via a new `ApplyArbitrarySetting` helper. Three-layer review (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Acceptance Auditor against `17-0-api-probes.md` Area 2): **back-compat gate (Rule #19) PASS** — the 5 working property keys + the 4 existing actions' output shapes are byte-for-byte unchanged (the only existing-`set` delta is `adapterClassName` THROW→Setting, the AC-17.2.4-sanctioned additive error→success); full-object `toEqual` snapshots on request bodies AND `structuredContent` are non-vacuous. Bootstrap `8c748712e247`→`39dc932907cb` (bootstrap.test green); governance baseline frozen `1e62c5ad5bf7` git-clean, `--check` exit 0 (141 frozen/186 live/45 post-foundation); governance test genuinely drives `handleToolCall` (add/remove denied-by-default, enable/disable/get/set allowed, opt-in flip); interop suite 219. IRIS API verified against `irislib/Ens/Config/{Production,Item}.cls` (`LoadFromClass`/`SaveToClass`/`RemoveItem`/`FindItemByConfigName`/`FindSettingByName`); Rules #7/#9/#15/namespace all honored. No HIGH/MED blocking findings.

The following items are DEFERRED — all on the NEW, default-DISABLED, write-gated surface or pre-existing accepted IRIS limitations; none affects the back-compat gate or is a current correctness/security bug:

- **[CR 17.2-1 / LOW — DEFERRED]** `ApplyArbitrarySetting` returns a `%Status` that both call sites discard (`Do ..ApplyArbitrarySetting(...)`, Interop.cls:463,528); a failed setting application is swallowed yet the key is still pushed to `updatedSettings`. LOW impact — the helper only does in-memory `%New`/property-set/`Insert` (no save), so real persistence failures still surface at the checked `%Save()`/`SaveToClass`. Mirrors Story 5-3 "set save/update consistency" (closed won't-fix). Suggested resolution: capture + propagate the status before `%Push` (requires redeploy + bootstrap re-roll). `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Interop.cls` ApplyArbitrarySetting call sites.
- **[CR 17.2-2 / MED — DEFERRED, lead-smoke-must-cover]** `@Host`/`@Adapter` suffix parser edge cases (Interop.cls:606-615): `$Find` matches the FIRST `@` (`A@B@Host` mis-routes to name `A@B@Host`/Adapter); leading `@` (`@Host`) yields an empty setting Name; lowercase/typo/unknown suffix (`@host`,`@Foo`) silently folds into the name with default Adapter target — all silently reported as successful `updatedSettings`. NEW code; no ObjectScript-layer test coverage (TS tests mock HTTP). Suggested resolution: scan the LAST `@`, validate a non-empty name, and reject/log an unknown suffix; add the rejection assertions to the Epic 17 live-HTTP smoke. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Interop.cls` ApplyArbitrarySetting.
- **[CR 17.2-3 / MED — DEFERRED, lead-smoke-must-cover]** `add` does not validate that host `className` exists/compiles or that the item Name is unique in the production (Interop.cls:489-533). A non-existent/non-host `className` writes a broken item (`SaveToClass` swallows `OnConfigChange <METHOD DOES NOT EXIST>` per `Production.cls:148-157`); the non-unique `(Production,Name)` index lets a duplicate-named item persist (add not idempotent). Out-of-scope for AC 17.2.3 (pins the verified `Items.Insert`/`SaveToClass` recipe). Suggested resolution: pre-check `FindItemByConfigName` for duplicate + `%Dictionary.CompiledClass.%ExistsId(className)` for the host class; add a duplicate-name + bogus-className assertion to the live smoke. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Interop.cls` add branch.
- **[CR 17.2-4 / MED — DEFERRED, pre-existing/accepted IRIS limitation]** set-vs-add/remove persistence-model split + `LoadFromClass` side effects (Interop.cls `ItemManage` add/remove branches; the `add`-then-`get` visibility note is now documented in the tool description — see the Story 18.0 RE-AFFIRMED-DEFER entry below): `set` persists via the extent (`%Save()`+`UpdateProduction()`) while `add`/`remove` persist via class-XData (`SaveToClass`); `LoadFromClass` `%DeleteId`s + reloads the extent from XData, so a just-`add`ed item is invisible to a subsequent `get`/`set` (extent readers) until the next add/remove `LoadFromClass` syncs it, and a `set`-then-`add`/`remove` sequence can revert extent-only changes. This is the SAME issue closed won't-fix in Story 5-3 ("ItemManage set save/update consistency — pre-existing IRIS pattern limitation, no incidents"). The `LoadFromClass` sync is the dev's live-verified, required fix for the add→remove round-trip (DISCREPANCY #1b); its destructive nature is inherent to the IRIS API. Suggested resolution: document the extent/XData split in the tool description; confirm `add` observability in the Epic 17 live smoke (round-trip add→remove already live-verified by dev). `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Interop.cls` add/remove branches.
- **[CR 17.2-5 / LOW — RESOLVED in Story 18.0]** `LoadFromClass` return `%Status` was ignored (invoked with `Do`, no status check); a silent `LoadFromClass` failure proceeded to `%OpenId` on a stale/half-loaded extent. **Resolved** in `ItemManage` add and remove branches — both now capture and check the `%Status` and fail cleanly via `SanitizeError`. See the Story 18.0 RESOLVED entry below. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Interop.cls` add/remove branches.

Three-layer review (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Acceptance Auditor): all six ACs (17.3.1–17.3.6) PASS. `mutates:{explain,stats,indexUsage,running:"read"}` all-classified (required even for reads — `assertGovernanceClassification` throws on any non-baseline key lacking a class; the probe doc's "no mutates" note correctly superseded by the spec). Frozen governance baseline `1e62c5ad5bf7` git-clean, `--check` exit 0 (141 frozen / 190 live / 49 post-foundation). NO bootstrap/ObjectScript change — `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` stays `39dc932907cb`, `bootstrap.test.ts` green. Pattern fidelity to `sql.ts` confirmed (`atelierPath(…,"action/query")`, `result.content[]` parse, `IrisApiError`→`isError`, non-IrisApiError rethrow, structuredContent OBJECT never array). Dev tool count 24→25. TWO patches applied inline (see story Review Findings) — no items deferred. The `EXPLAIN <query>` direct interpolation is the intended pass-through (same trust model as `iris_sql_execute`, user owns their own query, read-only) and is NOT a finding.

No DEFERRED items for this story — both review findings were auto-resolved inline:

- **[CR 17.3-1 / MED — RESOLVED in code review, 2026-06-16]** The `stats`/`running` `filter` param doubled single-quotes (correct quote-breakout escape) but did NOT escape LIKE wildcards `%`/`_`, so a filter like `50%` or `a_b` silently broadened the match vs the advertised "substring filter" semantics (NOT an injection — quote escaping is sound). **Fix applied:** new `likeSubstringPredicate(filter)` helper escapes the LIKE escape char `\`, then `%`/`_`, then doubles the single-quote, and appends an explicit `ESCAPE '\'` clause — so the filter matches a LITERAL substring. Both call sites (`running` UserName, `stats` Statement) now use the helper. Two unit-test exact-SQL assertions updated for the `ESCAPE` clause; two new e2e tests added (`%`/`_` escaped; backslash escaped). `packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/sqlAnalyze.ts`.
- **[CR 17.3-2 / LOW — RESOLVED in code review, 2026-06-16]** The `explain`/`indexUsage` required-`query` guard rejected `undefined`/`""` but a whitespace-only `query` (`"   "`) fell through to a server-side SQL error (`EXPLAIN    `) instead of the clean client-side "requires a 'query'" message. **Fix applied:** guard now uses `query.trim() === ""`. Two e2e tests added (whitespace-only `query` rejected before any HTTP for both `explain` and `indexUsage`). `packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/sqlAnalyze.ts`.

Dismissed as noise / by-design (no action): `EXPLAIN <query>` interpolation (intended pass-through, read-only, user's own query — explicitly sanctioned); `parsePlanIndexes` bare-`map` precision trade-off (acceptable for machine-generated IRIS plan text; the char class stops cleanly at the `, looping` comma/space, no trailing-dot in real plan output); `String(...Plan ?? "")` non-string coercion (Plan is a live-verified string XML column); `maxRows` 0/negative (Zod `.int().min(1)` rejects pre-handler); `toTabular` heterogeneous keys (standard homogeneous-SQL-resultset assumption, mirrors `sql.ts`); optional `schema` filter param + `STATEMENT_DAILY_STATS`/`HOURLY_STATS` (spec marks both "e.g."/"optionally" — out of required scope).

## Triaged via Story 18.0 (2026-06-17)

Story 18.0 (Epic 18 retro-review gate — minimal cleanup epic, this one story) triaged the carried Epic 16–17 `deferred-work.md` items + Epic 17 retro Action Item #1 / AI#4 against Epic 18 scope. Strictly additive: the hardening rejects previously-silently-accepted BAD input on the NEW, opt-in, default-disabled `iris_production_item` add/arbitrary-settings surface and tightens read-only `namespace`-param descriptions — no existing enabled action's success output shape changed (the 17.2 back-compat snapshot tests stayed green). Governance baseline frozen `1e62c5ad5bf7` git-clean (141 keys; `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0 — the bare generator was NOT run, Rule #25). Interop.cls changed → bootstrap re-rolled `39dc932907cb` → `fd3f065bcd3c` (Rule #24; `bootstrap.test.ts` green; `governance-baseline.ts` unchanged).

### INCLUDE — resolved in Story 18.0

- **[CR 17.2-1 / LOW] `ApplyArbitrarySetting` `%Status` discarded at both call sites** — **RESOLVED.** Both call sites (`ItemManage` set + add branches, `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Interop.cls`) now capture the helper's `%Status` and propagate it via `SanitizeError` BEFORE pushing the key to `updatedSettings`, so a failed setting application surfaces as an error instead of being reported as updated.
- **[CR 17.2-2 / MED] `@Host`/`@Adapter` suffix parser edges** — **RESOLVED.** `ApplyArbitrarySetting` now scans the **LAST** `@` (not `$Find`'s first), rejects an empty setting Name (leading `@` like `@Host`), and rejects an **unknown** suffix (anything other than `Host`/`Adapter`, e.g. `@Foo`/`@host`) with a clear error instead of silently folding it into the name with the default Adapter target. Lead live-HTTP smoke (Rule #26) to assert the rejections.
- **[CR 17.2-3 / MED] `add` does not validate className exists/compiles or item-Name uniqueness** — **RESOLVED.** The `add` branch now pre-checks `%Dictionary.CompiledClass.%ExistsId(className)` (rejects a non-existent/non-compiled host class) and `tProd.FindItemByConfigName(itemName)` for a duplicate item Name (rejects the non-idempotent duplicate) BEFORE `Items.Insert`/`SaveToClass`. Lead live-HTTP smoke to assert both rejections change nothing.
- **[CR 17.2-5 / LOW] `LoadFromClass` `%Status` ignored** — **RESOLVED.** Both the add and remove branches now capture the `LoadFromClass` `%Status` and fail cleanly (via `SanitizeError`) on a load failure instead of proceeding to `%OpenId`/`FindItemByConfigName` on a stale/half-loaded extent.
- **[CR 16.1-1 / CR 16.2-1 / CR 16.3-namespace] ops %SYS-scoped tools accept-but-ignore `namespace`** — **RESOLVED (descriptions only).** The `namespace` param descriptions in `process.ts` / `database.ts` / `backup.ts` now state explicitly that the value has NO EFFECT (the operation is %SYS-scoped). The param is **retained** (dropping an accepted optional param is a back-compat break); a TS unit test per tool asserts the description text AND that the param is still accepted/forwarded without error.

### DEFER (re-affirmed, doc-only include)

> **⚠ Partial supersede by Story 22.1 (2026-07-04):** AI#4 (`iris_backup_manage restore`) is now CLOSED-WITH-EVIDENCE (won't-fix, re-verified) — see the Story 22.1 closure table at the end of this file. (CR 17.2-4 is a pre-existing/accepted IRIS limitation, not one of the 14 carried items.)

- **[CR 17.2-4 / MED] set-vs-add/remove extent/XData persistence split; just-added item invisible to immediate get/set** — **RE-AFFIRMED DEFER (doc-only include).** Pre-existing/accepted IRIS limitation (mirrors Story 5-3 closed won't-fix). A tool-description NOTE was added to `iris_production_item` (`packages/iris-interop-mcp/src/tools/item.ts`) documenting that an `add`-ed item is not visible to an immediate `get`/`set` until the next add/remove `LoadFromClass` sync (Rule #27), with a TS test pinning the note. The underlying split is NOT re-architected.

### Resolved in Epic 19 retrospective (2026-06-18) — both CR 19.0 LOW items addressed now

- **[CR 19.0-1 / LOW — RESOLVED 2026-06-18 (retro, user-requested)] Discovery now validates `profile` when `allProfiles:true`.** `computeServerDiscovery` (`packages/shared/src/server-discovery.ts`) now calls `resolveProfile(profiles, args.profile)` when an explicit non-empty `profile` is supplied alongside `allProfiles:true`, throwing `ProfileResolutionError` on an unknown name — the SAME structured error the single-profile branch raises — instead of silently ignoring the typo. The all-profiles map is still returned for a VALID `profile` (its single-policy output is superseded by the per-profile map; the `allProfiles` `.describe()` documents this precedence). Two regression tests added (`server-discovery.test.ts`: unknown-profile-under-allProfiles errors; valid-profile-under-allProfiles returns the full map). Verified live against the rebuilt dist. No deferral remaining.
- **[CR 19.0-2 / LOW — RESOLVED 2026-06-18 (retro, user-requested)] Invalid framework `server` arg no longer hard-fails discovery.** `handleToolCall` (`packages/shared/src/server-base.ts`) now catches the `server`→profile `ProfileResolutionError` for the connection-agnostic discovery tool ONLY and falls back to the reserved `default` profile (the tool never connects; its own `profile` arg selects the policy), so the "call discovery first to learn valid profile names" workflow stays usable even if the client guesses a bad `server`. NON-discovery tools still hard-fail on an unknown `server` (the uniform D2 gate is unchanged for them). Regression test added (`server-discovery.test.ts`: discovery ignores invalid `server` → roster returned; `iris_doc_get` with same bad `server` → structured error). Verified live against the rebuilt dist. No deferral remaining.

## Deferred from: code review of story 21.1 (2026-07-03)

> **⚠ SUPERSEDED by Story 22.1 (2026-07-04):** CR 21.1-1 is RESOLVED (stakeholder Option A — rule A extended to pairloops in `Compressor.cls`; symmetric + nest fixtures) and CR 21.1-2 is RESOLVED (unpaired Src=Dst self-call push-guard + boundary fixtures). See the **Story 22.1 closure table at the end of this file**. The "Deferral rationale" wording below is HISTORICAL.

- **[CR 21.1-1 / LOW]** Episode-tier rule-A unwind is gated on `Kind="arrow"` — a `pairloop` immediately following an abandoned sync frame does NOT unwind it (`Compressor.cls:EpisodeCore`, the `If tEv.Kind = "arrow" { While … }` guard before the request-shaped push). The same semantic traffic gets a different episode grouping depending on whether the pair tier collapsed it: an abandoned request + raw single pair unwinds and splits into two episodes, while abandoned request + `pairloop` glues into one composite episode. **Deferral rationale:** never renders untruthfully — Phase-2 pass-through preserves original event order, and merges happen only on identical composite signatures (in the analyzed repeated case `[reqLost, PL]×3` the composite grouping actually compresses BETTER and stays faithful); §6.4 item 4's core requirement ("must not swallow all later episodes") holds because the next request ARROW unwinds. Spec reads "a new request's source" over the raw-event depth stack "over sync calls" — pair-loops are synthetic units, so the choice is an interpretation, not a violation. **Suggested resolution:** in the future cleanup epic, either extend rule A to pairloops (unwind on `tEv.Req.Src`) with fixtures for both groupings, or codify the composite-episode interpretation in the proposal §6.3 text. Source: Blind Hunter (finding #2), Story 21.1 review.
- **[CR 21.1-2 / LOW]** Repeated unanswered sync SELF-calls (`Src = Dst`, no response rows) never trigger rule A (the frame's destination always equals the next request's source), so each occurrence pushes another frame — the depth stack grows linearly (bounded by maxRows ≤ 10000 local-array nodes, no crash) and every self-call lands in ONE episode, defeating episode compression for that pathological trace shape. **Deferral rationale:** degradation-not-corruption (full-length faithful diagram, unpaired warnings intact); requires a config item Inproc-calling itself repeatedly with no responses — not a shape any current fixture or live trace exhibits. **Suggested resolution:** in the future cleanup epic (alongside CR 21.0-2's correlator index maps), add a self-call guard (don't push when `Src=Dst` and unpaired, or cap stack growth) + a Src=Dst boundary fixture. Source: Blind Hunter (finding #5), Story 21.1 review.

## Deferred from: code review of story 21.0 (2026-07-02)

> **⚠ SUPERSEDED by Story 22.1 (2026-07-04):** CR 21.0-2 (correlator O(n²)) is CLOSED-WITH-EVIDENCE (live measurement: default maxRows=2000 → ~0.14 s; documented cap in `Correlator.cls`). See the **Story 22.1 closure table at the end of this file**. (CR 21.0-1 was already RESOLVED in Story 21.1.)

- ~~**[CR 21.0-1 / MED]** Queue fallback pairing fires on EMPTY `ReturnQueueName`~~ — **RESOLVED (Story 21.1, improvement I3, 2026-07-02)**: stakeholder decision amended proposal §6.2 with **[I3]** — the `ReturnQueueName` fallback fires only when BOTH values are non-empty and equal (`Correlator.cls` now guards `tReq.ReturnQueue '= ""`); a one-way async request + later CorrId-less reverse response-typed row stays UNPAIRED with both warnings emitted, and legitimate non-empty equal-queue fallback pairs still pair (fixture tests in `DiagramCorrelatorTest`). Deliberate deviation from reference behavior, recorded in §6.2 [I3]. *(Original finding: `"" = ""` trivially satisfied the equality, silently false-pairing one-way Queue requests. Source: Edge Case Hunter finding #1, Story 21.0 review.)*
- **[CR 21.0-2 / LOW]** Correlator pairing is O(n²) with no iteration bound — every Request forward-scans to end-of-list; an unmatched Queue request scans TWICE (CorrId primary + ReturnQueue fallback). At the advertised extremes (maxRows=10000 × 20 sessions, pathological all-unpaired trace — exactly the wedged-production shape users will diagram) a single blocking GET could burn ~10⁸ oref property accesses. Default maxRows=2000 keeps realistic traces fast. **Deferral rationale:** §6.2 prescribes the order-based forward-scan pairing; index structures (CorrId→position map, per-endpoint-pair response queues) preserve semantics but are an optimization outside review scope. **Suggested resolution:** add index maps in Story 21.1 or the future cleanup epic; alternatively cap fan-out cost (sessions × maxRows budget). Source: Blind Hunter (finding #4), Story 21.0 review.

## Deferred from: code review of story 20.0 (2026-07-02)

> **⚠ SUPERSEDED by Story 22.1 (2026-07-04):** CR 20.0-1 is RESOLVED (per-action documentation — `timeout`/`force` honored only by `stop`/`restart`, ignored for `start`/`update`/`recover`/`clean`; doc-only preserves the AC 20.0.7 back-compat gate byte-for-byte; +doc test). See the **Story 22.1 closure table at the end of this file**. The "Deferral rationale" wording below is HISTORICAL.

Story 20.0 correctly fixed `RecoverProduction()` to a no-arg call (AC 20.0.6), and the tool description was narrowed to "Force stop on timeout." But `force` remains a top-level optional param on `iris_production_control` and the handler still does `if (force !== undefined) body.force = force`; `tForce` is still parsed server-side. A client sending `{action:"recover", force:true}` gets a silent no-op on the force intent (advertised-vs-actual drift). **Deferral rationale:** `force` is a pre-existing tool-wide parameter (not introduced by this story); removing/rejecting it per-action is a schema-shape change outside Story 20.0's additive scope and risks the AC 20.0.7 back-compat gate. Same class as the `force`/`timeout`-ignored-for-`clean` consistency note. **Suggested resolution:** in a future interop-cleanup story, either (a) reject `force`/`timeout` for actions that don't consume them with a clear validation error, or (b) document per-action which optional params are honored. Source: Blind Hunter (finding #2), Story 20.0 review.

## Deferred from: code review of story 22.0 (2026-07-03)

- **[CR 22.0-D1 / LOW]** `Generate.Count` aborts the ENTIRE scan when one document's `ScanDoc` errors — the enumerate→retrieve window is a TOCTOU: a document deleted (or renamed) between `StudioOpenDialog` enumeration and `GetTextAsArray` retrieval kills a long whole-namespace run with a hard error instead of skipping the vanished doc. **Deferral rationale:** skip-and-report is a design change to the facade's all-or-nothing contract (the hard per-document error is also what enforces the bucket-sum invariant discipline); no incident is possible on a quiescent namespace and the error message names the offending document. **Suggested resolution:** in a future hardening pass, collect per-document retrieval failures into a `skippedDocuments` array on the result (keeping classification errors hard), or document the all-or-nothing contract in the tool description. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/Loc/Generate.cls` scan loop. Source: Blind Hunter (LOW-5), Story 22.0 review.
- **[CR 22.0-D2 / LOW]** StudioOpenDialog multi-part spec order sensitivity: an exact document name listed BEFORE an overlapping wildcard part silently drops documents (live-probed: `ExecuteMCPv2.Loc.Generate.cls,ExecuteMCPv2.Loc.*.cls` → 2 of 3 docs; reversed order → all 3; identical-part dedup works). This is native IRIS `%RoutineMgr:StudioOpenDialog` behavior (spec re-processing at `irislib/%Library/RoutineMgr.cls:1146-1163`), not a port defect — the scanner passes the caller's spec through verbatim per ratified decision D2. **Deferral rationale:** neutralizing it (enumerating each comma part separately and unioning names) would change the ratified "spec semantics = StudioOpenDialog semantics" model and needs a stakeholder decision; CR 22.0-4 documented the quirk in the tool description, dev README, and tool_support.md as the current mitigation. **Suggested resolution:** if AI callers hit the quirk in practice, amend D2 to per-part-union enumeration in `Scanner.EnumerateDocs` (semantics-preserving for non-overlapping specs) + a regression test on the overlap shape. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/Loc/Scanner.cls:EnumerateDocs`. Source: reviewer live probe + Edge Case Hunter (LOW-D adjacent), Story 22.0 review.

## Deferred from: code review of story 22.1 (2026-07-04)

Story 22.1's OWN code review (adversarial three-layer + live-IRIS verification) confirmed all 15 dispositions above are genuine and terminal (CR 18.0-1 re-verified live over HTTP against the real `ItemManage` handler — abstract/non-host rejected, concrete host passes; CR 16.0-1 `--check` exit 0 with the frozen 141-key baseline byte-identical; CR 20.0-1 docs confirmed truthful against the server `ProductionControl` handler; CR 21.1-1/-2 re-run per-class on HSCUSTOM). Two NEW LOW items were surfaced — both permitted to remain open per AC 22.1.7 (Epic-22-own findings, NOT carried items); neither is a live defect:

- **[CR 22.1-1 / LOW]** `EpisodeCore` rule-A pairloop unwind (the CR 21.1-1 fix) leaves `tReqSrc=""` when a `pairloop` render event has a non-object `.Req` (the `$IsObject(tEv.Req)` guard only gates the assignment, not the while-loop). Because the `While` runs for any `pairloop` and no frame destination equals `""`, an empty `tReqSrc` would over-unwind the ENTIRE abandoned-sync-frame stack (mis-grouping episodes — never a crash, never untruthful rendering since Phase-2 preserves event order). **Reachability: UNREACHABLE via the current pipeline** — the sole producer of `pairloop` events (`Compressor.cls:Compress`, line ~85 `Set tLoop.Req = tEv`) always sets `.Req` to a real request arrow with a non-empty `Src`, so `tReqSrc` is always non-empty for a real pairloop (confirmed independently by all three review layers + the pairloop-construction read). The `$IsObject` guard already prevents the only real risk (an `<INVALID OREF>` crash). **Deferral rationale:** latent defensive-only path in freshly-changed code; hardening requires an ObjectScript edit → bootstrap re-roll + redeploy immediately before the lead smoke, disproportionate for an unreachable path. **Suggested resolution (one line):** in a future `Compressor.cls` touch, add `(tReqSrc '= "")` to the rule-A `While` condition so an unknowable source conservatively does NOT unwind (matches the pre-CR-21.1-1 "pairloops never unwound" fallback) instead of over-popping. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/Diagram/Compressor.cls` EpisodeCore (~line 255). Source: Blind Hunter #1 / Edge Case Hunter #1 / Acceptance Auditor (INFO), Story 22.1 review.
- **[CR 22.1-2 / LOW]** CR 16.0-1's shared derivation introduces a dist/source split: `scripts/gen-governance-baseline.mjs` imports the helper from `packages/shared/dist/governance-baseline-derivation.js` (compiled DIST) while the in-suite drift guard (`governance.test.ts`) imports `../governance-baseline-derivation.js` (vitest resolves the TS SOURCE). If a developer edits the `.ts` derivation and runs `gen:governance-baseline` / `:check` WITHOUT first rebuilding `@iris-mcp/shared`, the generator would run STALE dist logic while the drift test runs the new source logic — reintroducing the exact lock-step divergence CR 16.0-1 set out to eliminate. **Reachability: LOW / maintenance-workflow** — no incident on today's all-bare-enum surface (both derivations coincide); a fully MISSING dist is a loud fail-fast (`ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, acceptable and pre-existing since the generator already imports server dists); the hazard only bites when the derivation logic is changed for a wrapped/edge action shape AND the dist is stale. The standard `pnpm test` flow rebuilds shared (turbo) before the lockstep test spawns the CLI, so CI is protected. **Suggested resolution:** in a future tooling pass, either add a `prebuild`/`turbo run build --filter @iris-mcp/shared` step to the `gen:governance-baseline*` npm scripts, or have `governance.test.ts` import the built dist (matching the generator), so both consumers read the same artifact. `scripts/gen-governance-baseline.mjs` + `packages/shared/src/__tests__/governance.test.ts`. Source: Edge Case Hunter #2, Story 22.1 review.

- **[CR 22.1-3 / LOW — pre-existing]** The `iris_backup_manage action="restore"` rejection message in `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Monitor:BackupManage` names the IRIS restore entry points `^DBREST` / `CLUMENU^JRNRESTO`; because those are caret-globals, `SanitizeError` strips them (Rule #33), so the client sees the message with those references blanked to `( / )`. **Pre-existing** (in `Monitor.cls`, NOT introduced by Story 22.1 — surfaced while re-verifying AI#4's won't-fix rejection) and **cosmetic** — the rejection itself functions correctly (restore is refused, no crash, no write); only the two entry-point names are lost from the guidance text. **Assessed for in-scope fix (per the code-review directive) → DEFER:** the fix is a one-line reword (drop the carets: "DBREST" / "CLUMENU^JRNRESTO" → name them without the leading `^`, per Rule #33), but it touches `Monitor.cls` → bootstrap re-roll + redeploy for a cosmetic message in a won't-fix action — disproportionate to bundle into this review immediately before the lead smoke. **Suggested resolution:** fold the caret-strip reword into the next `Monitor.cls` touch (mirrors the Epic 20 `Ens.AppData` de-caret fix pattern). `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Monitor.cls` BackupManage restore branch. Source: dev flag (Story 22.1) + code-review assessment.

**Dismissed (investigated, no action):** the Zod `.min/.max` on `database.ts` narrows only nonsensical inputs the server would reject anyway (intended CR 16.2-2 resolution — not a real back-compat break); `$classmethod(cls,"%Extends","Ens.Host")` cannot throw `<METHOD DOES NOT EXIST>` (live-confirmed `%Extends` is defined on `%Library.Base`, inherited by every compiled class — returns 0 cleanly for non-hosts) and always runs after the `%ExistsId` compiled-class gate; `deriveKeysForTool` is byte-behaviour-identical to the old inline generator logic (Edge Hunter verified + frozen hash `1e62c5ad5bf7` unchanged).

## Deferred from: code review of 24-0-baseline-classifications (2026-07-08)

Story 24.0's code review (adversarial three-layer — Blind Hunter / Edge Case Hunter / Acceptance Auditor — plus a live-source read of `irislib/%SYS/OAuth2/Registration.cls`, `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Security.cls`, and every flagged `read` key's tool definition) found and **fixed inline 2 HIGH safety misclassifications + 2 lower patches** (see the story's Review Findings section). The frozen governance baseline `1e62c5ad5bf7` is untouched (`git diff` clean; `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0). Shared suite green post-patch (607 tests, was 606). **1 LOW item deferred:**

- **[CR 24.0-1 / LOW] No mechanical guarantee that a `read` classification is truthfully a pure query.** `BASELINE_ACTION_CLASSIFICATIONS` is hand-curated; the completeness test (`baseline-classifications.test.ts`) only proves exact key-set parity with `GOVERNANCE_BASELINE` and that every value is literally `"read"`/`"write"` — it is BLIND to whether a given key landed on the correct side. `baseline-classifications-safety.test.ts` mitigates this with named value-pins on the well-known execution/mutation and pure-query keys plus a write-verb / read-verb sweep, but there is no generator or oracle that derives read/write from tool semantics, so a future edit (or a new baseline key, though the set is frozen) could reintroduce a false `read`. This review demonstrated the risk is real: it caught **`iris_oauth_manage:discover`** (the IRIS `%SYS.OAuth2.Registration.Discover()` method PERSISTS — deletes+refreshes issuer metadata in a committed transaction; the dev's justification comment verified only the REST wrapper, not the system method) and **`iris_transform_test`** (executes a caller-named compiled DTL `Transform()` — arbitrary code; the tool's own `readOnlyHint:false`) — both were reclassified `read → write`. **Suggested resolution:** when Story 24.1 wires the map into the `read-only` preset cascade, consider (a) a lint/test cross-checking each `read` classification against the tool's own `annotations.readOnlyHint` (flagging any `read` whose tool declares `readOnlyHint:false` for a justification comment or reclassification), and (b) documenting in the map header that read-correctness rests on human review + the value-pin safety test, not a generator. Building a full runtime oracle is out of scope / infeasible (would require executing every tool). Source: Edge Case Hunter (informational) + this review's two confirmed HIGH catches. **Epic 24 retro disposition (2026-07-08, lead decision):** the two misclassifications (`iris_oauth_manage:discover`, `iris_transform_test`) are ALREADY fixed + committed in Epic 24 (`826c2bc`, both `write`, live-smoke-confirmed blocked under read-only). The **preventive guard** — a test flagging any `read`-classified key whose tool declares `annotations.readOnlyHint:false` — is **ROUTED to Story 25.0** (Epic 25's first story). REMAINS OPEN until 25.0 adds the cross-check test.

- **[SPEC-DIVERGENCE FLAG — RATIFIED by lead 2026-07-08] `iris_transform_test` reclassified `read → write` diverges from story AC 24.0.2's "test (read-only dry-run)" example.** The review reclassified this key to `write` per the domain-critical "code execution MUST be write" rule + fail-safe principle (a DTL is not guaranteed side-effect-free — it can embed `<sql>`/`<code>` — and the tool's own `readOnlyHint` is `false`). This contradicted **story AC 24.0.2** (which named `iris_transform_test` as a canonical read example). **Lead ratification:** the `write` classification STANDS. The story AC 24.0.2 read-verb allowlist that included `test`/`find`/`search`/`adapters`/etc. was BROADER than the **binding feature spec** `02-governance-presets.md` §2.1, whose allowlist is only `list/get/view/status/check/history/listHistory/explain/stats` — the spec never blessed `test` as a no-comment read. So the applied fix aligns with the binding spec; the story AC was the outlier (over-broadened during story creation). No feature-spec change needed. Consistent with Rule #16/#42 (a story-spec claim verified against live semantics — IRIS DTL execution + `readOnlyHint:false` — and corrected in the safe direction). CLOSED — not carried.

## Deferred from: code review of story-23.0 (2026-07-07)

Story 23.0 is a research/spec-amendment story (pinned the `01-health-check.md` §3 probe table + §2 thresholds). Its own pin-correctness was verified CLEAN (every pinned API confirmed against handler source + live IRIS; `locks` formula verified byte-for-byte against `%Monitor.System.LockTable.GetSample`; `memory`-drop evidence confirmed via `SYS.Stats.Dashboard` having no memory field; Temp probe classes verified deleted). Nine spec clarifications were auto-fixed inline. The items below are **forward-looking implementation/design considerations surfaced by the three review layers** that belong to the *implementing* stories (23.1 ObjectScript endpoint / 23.2 TS verdict engine), not defects in 23.0's pins — deferred so 23.1/23.2 devs see them.

- **[CR 23.0-1 / MEDIUM — for 23.2]** `databases` space-health is INERT under the vendor-recommended common case. `dbFreePct=(maxSize-size)/maxSize*100` only fires when `maxSize>0`, but live-verified all 15 dev-instance DBs have `maxSize=0` (IRIS's own "recommended" unlimited setting) ⇒ every DB is `notApplicable` ⇒ the tool's headline "database space health" produces NO disk-exhaustion signal on a normally-configured instance. Unlike `journal` (which was engineered to read actual volume free space via `%Library.File.GetDirectorySpace`), `databases` offers zero volume-exhaustion protection. **Deferral rationale:** the pin (Config.Databases + SYS.Database Size/MaxSize) is correct and mirrors the existing handler; switching the metric is a design decision for the implementing story. **Suggested resolution:** 23.1/23.2 consider a per-DB volume-free-% via `%Library.File.GetDirectorySpace(<db directory>, .free, .total, 0)` (same call journal now uses) so unlimited-size DBs still get a disk-space signal, OR explicitly document that `databases` is unmounted/maxSize-only health in v1. Source: Blind Hunter #3.

- **[CR 23.0-2 / MEDIUM — for 23.1/23.2]** `license` numerator `CSPUsers()` may under-count license consumption. `licensePct=currentCSPUsers/userLimit*100` was cross-verified only vacuously (`0==0` on an idle instance) against `SYS.Stats.Dashboard.LicenseCurrentPct`. `CSPUsers()` counts CSP/web sessions; if non-CSP connections consume license units, the numerator under-counts and the license check silently fails to warn near exhaustion. **Deferral rationale:** cannot be distinguished on an idle instance; needs an under-load check 23.0 can't perform. **Suggested resolution:** 23.1/23.2 verify `CSPUsers()` vs `SYS.Stats.Dashboard.LicenseCurrent` under real load and prefer the IRIS-authoritative `LicenseCurrent`/`LicenseCurrentPct` (Management-Portal dashboard figure; also sidesteps the `userLimit=0` divide-by-zero). Spec §3 license row already carries this pointer. Source: Blind Hunter #4 (+ Edge Case Hunter #2 zero-denominator).

- **[CR 23.0-3 / MEDIUM — for 23.2]** No health-LEVEL (ok/warning) criteria for the applicable-but-unhealthy branch of `mirror`, `ecp`, `interop`, `system`. Only `journal`/`databases`/`license`/`locks` have thresholds and `alerts` has the state rule; for a mirror member whose backup is disconnected (`GetStatus` string), a configured-but-dead ECP, an interop production with a large queue backlog (no queue-depth threshold param exists), and `system` counters, the spec pins only the raw source / `notApplicable` gate, so those cases default to `ok` and hide real failures. **Deferral rationale:** verdict criteria are 23.2's job (thresholds live in TS by design) and partly a v1-scope product decision. **Suggested resolution:** 23.2 define ok/warning rules (e.g. an `interop` queue-depth threshold param; a `mirror` `GetStatus`-not-"Primary/Backup-normal" ⇒ warning) OR explicitly mark these areas informational (raw-only) in v1. Source: Edge Case Hunter #7, Blind Hunter #12.

- **[CR 23.0-4 / MEDIUM — for 23.2]** `databases` per-DB → single-area finding aggregation rule is unspecified. Raw is per-DB (`{size,maxSize,mounted}` × N; 15 on dev) but the §2 output example shows one finding per area with a scalar `value`/`threshold`. The spec never says how N per-DB results roll up into the `databases` area (worst-DB? one finding per DB? area `value` when DBs differ?). Never exercised because all 15 dev DBs are `notApplicable`. **Deferral rationale:** aggregation is verdict-engine (23.2) design. **Suggested resolution:** 23.2 pin an explicit rule — recommend worst-DB drives the area level with a per-DB breakdown in `raw`. Source: Edge Case Hunter #8.

- **[CR 23.0-5 / LOW — for 23.1]** `databases` `%OpenId` open-FAILURE is indistinguishable from `notApplicable`. The mirrored `Config.cls:DatabaseList` / `Monitor.cls:DatabaseCheck` wrap `SYS.Database.%OpenId(dir)` in Try/Catch and on failure leave `size/maxSize=0` (`mounted=0`); §3 maps `maxSize=0`→`notApplicable` and `mounted=0`→`notApplicable`, so a genuinely corrupt/locked DB whose open throws is silently reported `notApplicable` (healthy-ish) instead of `error`. **Deferral rationale:** 23.1 handler-robustness detail. **Suggested resolution:** 23.1 set an explicit error/open-failed flag in the per-DB raw so TS emits `level:"error"` for an open failure, distinct from the unlimited-size `notApplicable`. Source: Edge Case Hunter #10.

- **[CR 23.0-6 / LOW — for 23.2]** Tool-input validation gaps for the TS layer: (a) the dropped `memory` enum value — undefined whether `areas:["memory"]` is a Zod reject, silently ignored, or aliased to `system` (back-compat risk for a caller reading prior docs); (b) `areas:[]` empty-array semantics (check-nothing vs check-all) undefined ⇒ "All 0 areas healthy"; (c) threshold override has no range/ordering guard (`journalPctWarn=95, journalPctCrit=90` inverted; negative; >100) — AC 3 deliberately allows extreme values, but inverted/precedence behavior is unspecified. **Deferral rationale:** all three are 23.2 TS/Zod design. **Suggested resolution:** 23.2 decide + unit-test each (recommend: reject `memory` with a "renamed/dropped" message, treat `areas:[]` as all, document override precedence as per-threshold independent). Source: Edge Case Hunter #12/#13/#14.

- **Per-area fault isolation + single render (Rule #7)**: live-verified — malformed POST body → one clean `ERROR #5001` envelope at HTTP 200; the two forced-failure `%UnitTest` methods drive REAL exceptions (`SYS.Lock` outside `%SYS`; `Ens.Director` absent in `%SYS`) through the live Try/Catch → `errors[locks]` / `interopEnabled:false` while other areas stay intact. 15/15 %UnitTest (`total=15` matches — no Rule #35 partial-snapshot); bootstrap `13b4b5f003ab` regen idempotent; `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0 (frozen `1e62c5ad5bf7` untouched, no new key); `bootstrap.test.ts` 42/42.

Confirmed against `Utils.cls:SanitizeError` source — its backward-alnum-walk (lines 106-108) would strip a whole `CLUMENU^JRNRESTO` token (not just the leading `^`), so the caret-free reword is the correct fix. - **CR 23.0-5 → RESOLVED.** Story 23.1 implemented the per-DB `openFailed` boolean in `HealthCheckDatabases`, distinguishing a genuine `%OpenId` throw (`openFailed:true`) from legitimate `maxSize=0`/`mounted=0` `notApplicable`. Live-verified all 15 DBs return `openFailed:false`.
- **CR 23.0-2 → 23.1-half DONE; 23.2 retains the decision.** `HealthCheckLicense` now emits the IRIS-authoritative `licenseCurrent`/`licenseCurrentPct` (`SYS.Stats.Dashboard`, real properties confirmed) alongside `currentCSPUsers`/`userLimit`. The "prefer the authoritative figure under load" choice remains Story 23.2's (verdict engine).
- **CR 23.0-1 / -3 / -4 / -6 → remain Story 23.2-scope** (TS verdict-engine / request-contract items; already tracked in the story-23.0 section above — NOT re-deferred here).

**New Story-23.1 review findings (all LOW, non-blocking — routed as noted):**

- **[CR 23.1-1 / LOW — for 23.2]** Request-parsing permissiveness in the `HealthCheck` dispatcher: a POST body with `areas` as a JSON string (`{"areas":"journal"}`), a bare JSON array (`["journal"]`), or an empty array (`{"areas":[]}`) all silently fall back to "all 9 areas"; a GET/POST list of only mis-cased or unknown names (`?areas=Journal`, `?areas=cpu`) silently returns an empty `{"areas":{},"errors":{}}` at HTTP 200. **Consistent with the documented design** (`HealthCheckParseAreas` doc: "Unknown names are silently ignored — the TS-layer zod enum (Story 23.2) is the authoritative validator; this endpoint stays permissive") and **benign** (superset or empty, never wrong data; verified live — no crash, valid JSON, HTTP 200 throughout). The sole real consumer is the 23.2 TS tool, which always sends well-formed comma-separated (GET) / `{"areas":[enum,...]}` (POST) input. **Deferral rationale:** the request contract + its authoritative validation belong to Story 23.2. **Suggested resolution:** 23.2 confirm the zod enum fully guards these shapes (recommended — treat the OS endpoint as an internal producer), OR tighten the OS parser to hard-reject/normalize a non-array `areas`. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Health.cls` HealthCheck ~59-72 / HealthCheckParseAreas ~127-135. Source: Edge Case Hunter #1/#2/#3/#4, Blind Hunter #3.
- **[CR 23.1-2 / LOW — for 23.2]** Unit-test hardening for the health endpoint. The 9 per-area success tests assert field PRESENCE (`%IsDefined`), not values, so a value/logic regression that still populates a defined field of plausible type (e.g. an inverted `ecp.configured`, a per-process sampler in `system`) could pass; the interop success/queue branch (`interopEnabled=1`, `queues`/`queueCount`) is asserted only for presence; and the `HealthCheck` dispatcher's `%request` parsing (POST/GET branch, single-render, %SYS ordering) is structurally not `%UnitTest`-able (documented) and is covered only by the live HTTP smoke. **Coverage is GENUINE, not illusory (Rule 3 satisfied):** the tests drive the REAL handler methods against live IRIS and WOULD fail on a crash / wrong-namespace / missing-field / isolation-leak / gate-leak regression (the two forced-failure tests are real, not mocks), and the dispatcher IS exercised by the live HTTP smoke (Rule #26). This is hardening, not a gap. **Deferral rationale:** Story 23.2 adds the TS verdict-engine unit tests + live smokes over this same endpoint and is the natural place to strengthen. **Suggested resolution:** in 23.2, assert deterministic values where portable (e.g. `interopEnabled=1` + `queueCount` present in the interop-enabled test namespace; a mounted DB's `openFailed=0`) and/or add an HTTP-level dispatcher smoke to CI. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/Tests/HealthCheckTest.cls`. Source: Blind Hunter #2, Edge Case Hunter #5/#6.
- **[CR 23.1-3 / LOW]** Result-set close hygiene in `Health.cls`. `HealthCheckInterop`'s `Ens.Queue:Enumerate` `%ResultSet` is never `.Close()`d, and `HealthCheckDatabases`/`HealthCheckSystem` close (or never close) their result sets only on the success path — so an exception mid-loop skips the close. Impact is minimal: IRIS `%ResultSet`/`%SQL.StatementResult` orefs release their cursors on scope-exit (method return), so there is no cross-call leak — but it is inconsistent with the sibling `Do tDBRS.Close()` in `HealthCheckDatabases` and matches the pre-existing laxity already tracked for `Config.cls:DatabaseList` / `SystemConfig.cls` (CR 11.3). **Suggested resolution:** add `Do:$IsObject(tQRS) tQRS.Close()` after the interop loop and move the databases/system closes to run on the error path too, in a future `Health.cls`/handler-hardening touch. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Health.cls:246,468`. Source: Blind Hunter #1, Edge Case Hunter #8.
- **[CR 23.1-4 / LOW]** `HealthCheck` discards the `%Status` returned by `HealthCheckParseAreas` (line 73), and every subsequent `If tWant("<area>")` assumes all 9 subscripts exist. Safe today — the parse body is all non-throwing string ops over a static CSV after `Kill pWant`, so it cannot fail post-Kill — but fragile if the CSV/population is ever refactored to bail early (a missing subscript would raise `<UNDEFINED>` into the outer Catch and error the whole request). **Suggested resolution:** either check the returned status or default-read the flags (`If +$Get(tWant("system"))`). `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Health.cls:73`. Source: Blind Hunter #6, Edge Case Hunter #9.
- **[CR 23.1-5 / LOW]** GET repeated `areas` query params (`?areas=journal&areas=license`) read only the first value (`$Get(%request.Data("areas",1))`); the documented contract is comma-separated (`?areas=journal,license`, which works correctly), so a client using the repeated-param convention silently gets only the first area. Minor; no error, partial result. **Suggested resolution:** if leniency is wanted, loop `%request.Data("areas",n)` and join with commas before parsing. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Health.cls:70`. Source: Blind Hunter #3, Edge Case Hunter #7.

**Dismissed (investigated live, no action):**
- Blind Hunter "POST `areas` array with a non-string element throws `<INVALID OREF>`" — **empirically false**: verified live that `{"areas":[{"x":1}]}` and `{"areas":[123,"journal"]}` degrade gracefully (the element stringifies to a non-matching name and is ignored; `[123,"journal"]` → exactly `journal`), no throw, clean HTTP 200.
- Blind Hunter "`%Library.File.GetDirectorySpace` return treated as `%Status` may zero the volume values" — **verified non-issue**: live `journal.volumeTotalBytes=511067549696` / `volumeFreeBytes=349785391104` (non-zero, byte-identical to Story 23.0's probe), so `GetDirectorySpace` is status-returning and the `$$$ISERR` guard is correct.

## Deferred from: code review of story-23.2 (2026-07-08)

Story 23.2's code review (adversarial three-layer — Blind Hunter / Edge Case Hunter / Acceptance Auditor — plus a source read of the Story-23.1 endpoint `Health.cls` and the shared framework `ToolContext`/`server-base.ts`) closed at **0 HIGH, 0 MEDIUM (blocking)**. **2 LOW findings were auto-fixed inline** (patches below); **3 LOW deferred** (Story-23.2-own). The frozen governance baseline `1e62c5ad5bf7` is untouched; `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0 (141 frozen / 194 live / 53 post-foundation). Ops suite green post-patch (335 tests, was 329) + `type-check` clean.

**Note on the two hunters' MEDIUM (numerator-NaN):** Blind + Edge both flagged unguarded numerator coercion (`+raw.volumeFreeBytes`, `db.size`, `currentCSPUsers`) producing a `NaN`/wrong percentage that mis-levels a finding (false `ok` hides exhaustion; empty-string → false `critical`). **Downgraded to LOW after reading `Health.cls` (Rule #2/#16):** the co-designed Story-23.1 endpoint emits EVERY numeric field with an explicit `"number"` type hint + `+` coercion + 0-default, and defaults a failed volume probe to `free=0/total=0` (→ the guarded `total<=0` notApplicable path) — so a non-finite/empty-string numeric is **not live-reachable** through the tool→endpoint path. The hunters (Blind = no project access; Edge = noted "unlikely to emit") were appropriately cautious without the endpoint source. It remains a real robustness gap in the EXPORTED pure `evaluate()` (used directly in tests / by any future caller), so it was patched as cheap defensive hardening — see CR 23.2-P1.

**Patches applied inline this review (LOW, RESOLVED):**

- **[CR 23.2-P1 / LOW — PATCHED]** Non-finite hardening of the pure verdict engine. Added `Number.isFinite` guards so a non-finite computed percentage degrades to `notApplicable` (never a false `ok`/`critical`): `evaluateJournal` (pctFull), `evaluateOneDatabase` (freePct), `evaluateLicense` (extends the `pct===null` guard to catch a `NaN` `licenseCurrentPct`/fallback ratio); and `mergeThresholds` now ignores a non-finite override (`typeof===number && Number.isFinite`) so a direct `evaluate(raw,{},{journalPctCrit:NaN})` call can't silently downgrade a genuinely-critical area. The over-claiming comment ("`z.number()` already rejects them") was corrected to state the guard's actual scope. `packages/iris-ops-mcp/src/tools/health.ts`. +4 regression tests (`health.test.ts` "non-finite hardening" describe). Source: Blind Hunter MEDIUM #1, Edge Case Hunter MEDIUM #1 / LOW #5.

- **[CR 23.2-P2 / LOW — PATCHED]** Worst-DB tie-break determinism (CR 23.0-4 hardening). The worst-DB reducer used strict first-wins `rank > worstRank`, so an `openFailed` (`error`) DB listed AFTER an equal-rank `warning` DB was masked (area reported `warning`, the un-openable DB unnamed) — array-order-dependent, contradicting the changelog's "`openFailed` reported distinctly." Replaced with an `isWorseDb` comparator: at equal severity rank prefer `error` over `warning` (surface the openFailed DB), then prefer the lower `freePct` (name the genuinely-worst DB) — both order-independent; `notApplicable` still never selected. Verdict is unchanged (error==warning rank ⇒ still `warning`, never faked to `critical`). `packages/iris-ops-mcp/src/tools/health.ts`. +2 regression tests. Source: Blind Hunter LOW, Edge Case Hunter LOW #3.

**Ledger reconciliation — Story-23.2-owned CR items RESOLVED (closed, not carried):**

- **CR 23.0-1 → RESOLVED.** `databases` v1 decision folded in + DOCUMENTED (tool description + finding explanations): free-% health for `maxSize`-configured, mounted DBs only; unlimited-`maxSize`/unmounted DBs report `notApplicable` (no volume-level disk-exhaustion signal in v1 — pointer to `iris_database_check`). `evaluateOneDatabase`/`evaluateDatabases`.
- **CR 23.0-2 → RESOLVED.** `evaluateLicense` prefers the IRIS-authoritative `licenseCurrentPct` when present (needs no denominator), falls back to `currentCSPUsers/userLimit*100` only when absent, `notApplicable` when `userLimit=0` and no authoritative figure. Unit-tested (prefers-pct, fallback, NA guard, crit).
- **CR 23.0-3 → RESOLVED.** `system`/`mirror`/`ecp`/`interop` are informational-only in v1 (always `ok`, or `notApplicable` when not configured — no ok/warning threshold), DOCUMENTED in the tool description and each finding explanation. A queue-depth/mirror-status threshold is a documented future enhancement.
- **CR 23.0-4 → RESOLVED** (+ hardened by CR 23.2-P2). Worst-DB drives the `databases` area level; full per-DB breakdown stays in `raw.databases`. Tie-break now deterministic.
- **CR 23.0-6 → RESOLVED.** Zod `areas` enum excludes `memory` (rejected with the 9-value enumeration); `areas:[]` treated as all-9 (unit-tested end-to-end, "All 9 areas healthy"); threshold overrides per-field independent, extreme values allowed (`journalPctCrit:1`), `NaN`/non-number rejected by `z.number()` (+ direct-call finite guard from CR 23.2-P1).
- **CR 23.1-1 → RESOLVED.** The Zod enum is the authoritative validator (the endpoint stays permissive by design); `memory`/unknown/mis-cased inputs rejected before the HTTP call; `areas:[]`→all. The tool always sends well-formed comma-separated GET input.
- **CR 23.1-2 → RESOLVED.** Every fixture test asserts deterministic VALUES (exact `level`/`value`/`threshold`/`verdict` + the exact `GOVERNANCE_DISABLED` envelope), not presence-only (CR 23.1-2 intent). 76 `health.test.ts` + 3 `health-governance.test.ts`.

**New Story-23.2-own deferred items (all LOW, non-blocking — routed as noted):**

- **[CR 23.2-1 / LOW] `server` output field omitted from `structuredContent` — spec §2 reconciliation.** Spec §2's illustrative output shows `"server": "<profile name>"`; the tool emits `{verdict, checkedAt, findings, raw}` without it. **Verified acceptable (Rule #16):** the typed `ToolContext` (`packages/shared/src/tool-types.ts`) exposes only `resolveNamespace/http/atelierVersion/config/paginate`, and `config`'s type `IrisConnectionConfig` (`config.ts`) has NO profile-name field; the framework knows `profile.name` only at the `handleToolCall` layer (`server-base.ts:913`, used for the governance envelope) and the discovery-tool short-circuit explicitly notes "the standard ToolContext does not expose" profile internals. No other suite tool echoes a synthesized `server` field (grep-confirmed). Surfacing it needs a framework change, out of this TS-only story's scope, and no test asserts `server` so nothing regresses. **Suggested resolution:** a future framework touch either (a) amends spec §2 to drop `server` from the v1 output, OR (b) exposes the resolved profile name through `ToolContext` and echoes it here. Source: Acceptance Auditor #1.
- **[CR 23.2-2 / LOW] Unknown `errors` key (outside the 9-area enum) is silently dropped by `evaluate()`.** `evaluate(rawAreas, errors)` builds `checked` by filtering `AREA_VALUES`, so an `errors` key not in the 9 areas produces no finding and never contributes to the verdict — a server-reported error could vanish into a false `healthy`. **Not live-reachable:** `Health.cls` only ever sets `errors[<area>]` for the 9 canonical area names (`pErrors.%Set("<area>", ...)`), so the endpoint cannot emit an out-of-enum error key. A clean guard (surface unknown error keys as findings) would require widening the typed `Finding.area`/`Area` contract beyond the 9-area union — disproportionate for a near-unreachable case. **Suggested resolution:** if a future endpoint change can emit non-enum error keys, widen the contract and surface them as `error` findings; otherwise leave as a documented pure-function limitation. `packages/iris-ops-mcp/src/tools/health.ts` `evaluate` `checked` filter. Source: Edge Case Hunter MEDIUM #2.
- **[CR 23.2-3 / LOW] A 200 response missing the `result` object throws a raw `TypeError` (not the tool's error envelope).** The handler does `const result = response.result as {...}; evaluate(result.areas ?? {}, ...)` — if `response.result` is `undefined` (a valid-JSON, HTTP-200, no-`status.errors` body lacking `result`), `result.areas` throws a `TypeError` that is not an `IrisApiError` and is rethrown (the MCP framework converts it to an error, but not the tool's `{isError:true}` envelope). **Near-unreachable:** the Atelier envelope from `RenderResponseBody` always carries `result` on success, and the http-client throws `IrisApiError` on a non-JSON/malformed body. **Not blindly patched** because the correct fix direction is ambiguous — a `result ?? {}` guard would convert the malformed response into a false `healthy`/"All 0 areas healthy" (arguably worse than a loud failure); the better fix (explicit `isError` when `result` is absent) is a small design choice. **Suggested resolution:** guard `response.result` and return an explicit `isError` ("health endpoint returned no result payload") rather than either a crash or a false healthy. `packages/iris-ops-mcp/src/tools/health.ts` handler. Source: Blind Hunter LOW.

**Dismissed (by-design / cosmetic / not-reachable — no action):**
- `notApplicable` excluded from the text-content "non-ok" lines (Acceptance Auditor #2) — DISMISSED as by-design: listing informational `notApplicable` areas would break the spec's own "All N areas healthy" clean line; full detail stays in `structuredContent.findings`. Serves spec intent better than the literal reading.
- "All N areas healthy" counts `notApplicable` in N, and "All 0 areas healthy" for an empty payload (Blind/Edge LOW) — DISMISSED as cosmetic: the verdict is always correct; the empty-payload string is on the same near-unreachable path as CR 23.2-3.
- `NaN`/`undefined` leaking into `system`/`alerts` explanation TEXT when a raw field is absent (Edge LOW #6) — DISMISSED as cosmetic + not-reachable: informational areas (no verdict impact), and `Health.cls` always emits `state`/`processCount` as numbers.

**Carried, untouched by this TS-only story (remain open for a future ops/cleanup epic):**
- ObjectScript `Health.cls` hardening from Story 23.1: **CR 23.1-3** (result-set close hygiene), **CR 23.1-4** (`HealthCheckParseAreas` status discarded), **CR 23.1-5** (GET repeated `areas` params read first only) — all LOW, all ObjectScript, out of Story 23.2's TS-only scope.
- Epic-22-own LOWs still carried: **CR 22.0-D1**, **CR 22.0-D2**, **CR 22.1-1**, **CR 22.1-2** (CR 22.1-3 was RESOLVED by Story 23.1). Feature epic — Rule #37 burn-down not yet triggered.
