## Deferred from: code review of story-24.1 (2026-07-08)

Story 24.1's code review (adversarial three-layer — Blind Hunter / Edge Case Hunter / Acceptance Auditor) closed at **0 HIGH, 0 MEDIUM**. Acceptance Auditor confirmed all 5 ACs PASS. **1 LOW patched inline** (CR 24.1-P1 — prototype-safe classification lookup in `presetSeed`, applied + re-verified: `@iris-mcp/shared` build clean, 653/653 shared tests green, `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0, `governance-baseline.ts` git-clean). **1 LOW deferred** (below). 2 findings dismissed as spec-required (the discovery `preset` field being always-present and the `full`≠`null` self-report distinction — both mandated by AC 24.1.4a; the Rule #19 byte-for-byte contract governs enforcement, which is proven identical for unset vs `full`).

**New Story-24.1-own deferred item (LOW, non-blocking):**

- **[CR 24.1-1 / LOW] `presetApplied` attribution is not strictly but-for under read-only.** In the `GOVERNANCE_DISABLED` denial path (`server-base.ts` ~930), `presetCaused = this.preset !== undefined && !hasExplicitOverride(...) && presetSeed(...) === false`. For a NEW post-baseline `write` key that is NOT `defaultEnabled` and has no explicit override, under `IRIS_GOVERNANCE_PRESET=read-only` the denial carries `presetApplied: "read-only"` — but that same key is ALSO denied with NO preset (`defaultSeed` returns `false` for a non-`defaultEnabled` write), so the preset is not the *but-for* cause. `hasExplicitOverride` only inspects explicit overrides, never `defaultSeed`, so it cannot distinguish "preset blocked an otherwise-enabled action" (baseline writes + F2 `defaultEnabled` writes — correctly attributed) from "preset blocked an already-default-disabled new write". An operator who removes the preset expecting `presetApplied` to mean "this becomes available" would find such a new write still disabled. **In-spec, not a defect:** the implementation matches Task 3's explicit `presetCaused` definition verbatim ("the key is disabled, there is NO explicit `IRIS_GOVERNANCE` override for it at either layer, and `presetSeed(key)` returned `false`"), the Acceptance Auditor confirmed AC 24.1.4c PASS, and under read-only the preset IS the proximate cascade layer that fires (it short-circuits before `defaultSeed`). Confined to new default-disabled writes; correct for all baseline writes and F2 `defaultEnabled` writes. **Suggested resolution (future refinement, if desired):** if strict but-for attribution is wanted, compute `presetApplied` only when the key would resolve enabled with the preset omitted (i.e. `presetSeed===false` AND `defaultSeed(...)===true` under no preset), so `presetApplied` means precisely "removing the preset re-enables this call"; requires a spec amendment to Task 3's definition. `packages/shared/src/server-base.ts` ~930 (`presetCaused`). Source: Edge Case Hunter #1, corroborated by Blind Hunter attribution analysis. **CLOSED at Epic 24 retro (2026-07-08, lead decision):** in-spec, cosmetic edge case confined to new default-disabled writes; correct for all baseline + `defaultEnabled` writes. Not worth a spec amendment. No action — CLOSED, not carried.

## Deferred from: code review of story-24.2 (2026-07-08)

Story 24.2's code review (adversarial three-layer — Blind Hunter / Edge Case Hunter / Acceptance Auditor) closed at **0 HIGH**. **2 findings patched inline** (CR 24.2-P1 — `IRIS_SQL_TIMEOUT` non-finite rejection; CR 24.2-P2 — SQL caps propagation to non-default `IRIS_PROFILES` profiles). **1 LOW deferred** (below). 3 findings dismissed as noise/by-design. Verified after patches: `@iris-mcp/shared` build clean, 664/664 shared tests green, dev 365/365 green, `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0, `governance-baseline.ts` git-clean.

**Patched inline (recorded for provenance):**
- **CR 24.2-P1 (MED) — `IRIS_SQL_TIMEOUT` accepted non-finite `Infinity`.** The validator used `Number.isNaN(parsed)`, which lets `Number("Infinity")===Infinity` through (`> 0`, not `NaN`); `sqlTimeoutMs=Infinity` forwards `{ timeout: Infinity }` to `http.post`, and Node's `setTimeout` clamps `Infinity`/`NaN`/`>2147483647` to ~1ms — silently disabling SQL (opposite of the operator's "unlimited" intent). Fixed by switching to `!Number.isFinite(parsed)` (the pattern already used in `profiles.ts:200` for `timeout`; the `IRIS_SQL_MAX_ROWS` path was already safe via `Number.isInteger`). Added a config test asserting `IRIS_SQL_TIMEOUT="Infinity"` throws. `packages/shared/src/config.ts`. Source: Edge Case Hunter HIGH / Blind Hunter MED.
- **CR 24.2-P2 (MED) — SQL caps silently ignored on non-default `IRIS_PROFILES` profiles.** `mergeProfile` (`profiles.ts`) rebuilt each named profile from a field allowlist that omitted `sqlMaxRows`/`sqlTimeoutMs`, so a call with `server:"<other>"` bypassed the caps even when the operator set the env vars — undercutting AC 24.2.1's "HARD cap" and Epic 24's "point it at PRODUCTION" headline (production is commonly a named profile). Fixed by inheriting both fields from `base` (conditional spread, preserving the Rule #19 unset-absent shape) exactly like `timeout`. Flipped the QA-added "KNOWN GAP" `profiles.test.ts` case to assert propagation WORKS + added an unset-shape test. `packages/shared/src/profiles.ts`. Source: all three layers (MED).

**New Story-24.2-own deferred item (LOW, non-blocking):**

- **[CR 24.2-1 / LOW] `IRIS_SQL_MAX_ROWS` is a post-fetch display slice, not a wire/memory resource cap.** The handler builds `allRowObjects` from the ENTIRE Atelier `/action/query` `result.content`, then `slice(0, effectiveLimit)` — so a 5M-row result is fully transferred and materialized before the cap trims what is echoed back. The tool description and README market the env var as a "hard cap on the effective row limit"/"resource cap," but it gives no protection against unbounded transfer/memory; only the returned row list is bounded. **Pre-existing behavior of the tool's `maxRows`/Atelier query mechanism** (not introduced by this diff — the new env var only re-frames the existing post-fetch slice as a safety control), so left untouched in this scope. **Suggested resolution:** if a true resource cap is wanted, inject a server-side `TOP`/`FIRST`/`LIMIT` (or an Atelier row-limit parameter) so IRIS bounds the result set before transfer, and update the docs' "hard cap" framing accordingly; otherwise soften the docs to "caps the returned row count" to avoid overstating the guarantee. `packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/sql.ts` (`allRowObjects.slice`). Source: Edge Case Hunter MEDIUM. **RESOLVED at Epic 24 retro (2026-07-08, lead decision — docs softened):** reworded from "hard cap"/"hard ceiling" to "a ceiling on the number of rows returned … bounds the returned row count post-fetch, not the server-side result set or transfer" across all four surfaces (README env table + read-only section, `sql.ts` tool description, `iris-dev-mcp/README.md`). A true server-side `TOP`/`LIMIT` cap remains an optional future enhancement. CLOSED — not carried.

**Dismissed (by-design / consistent-with-existing-convention — no action):**
- `rowsCapped: true` fires when `cap < requested` even if fewer actual rows exist than the cap (so no row is withheld) — DISMISSED as by-design: AC 24.2.1 defines `rowsCapped` as "the cap actually reduced the caller's requested **limit**" (distinct from `truncated`, which means rows were withheld); the ceiling WAS reduced, and both fields are independently and explicitly tested. Blind/Edge/Auditor all noted it matches the AC wording.
- `Number()` coercion accepts hex/scientific/whitespace forms (`"0x10"`, `"1e3"`, `" 5 "`) and a whitespace-only value throws instead of no-op'ing — DISMISSED as consistent with the established codebase convention: the pre-existing `IRIS_PORT`/`IRIS_TIMEOUT` validators (`config.ts:64,89`) use the identical `Number()`-based lenient parse; the story explicitly instructed mirroring that pattern. Hardening it here alone would be inconsistent; a suite-wide strict-parse pass would be a separate cross-cutting item.
- Docs describe the caps as "a hard ceiling on any call" without the non-default-profile caveat (Auditor LOW) — DISMISSED as mooted by CR 24.2-P2: with the caps now propagated to every profile, the docs' "any call" statement is accurate.

## Epic 25 retro-review gate (2026-07-08) — triage of the inherited ledger

Source: `epic-24-retro-2026-07-08.md` + this ledger. Epic 25 (MCP Prompts Capability & Agent Skills Pack) is a **TS/content-only feature epic** (`packages/*/src/prompts/`, `scripts/gen-skills.mjs`, `scripts/validate-prompts.mjs`, docs) with three feature stories (25.0 framework plumbing, 25.1 content+generators, 25.2 docs+smoke). No ObjectScript, no bootstrap bump, no new tool/governance key.

| Item | Source | Triage Decision |
|---|---|---|
| CR 24.0-1 (preventive `readOnlyHint` cross-check test for `baseline-classifications.ts`) | Epic 24 retro (explicitly routed) | **INCLUDE — folded into Story 25.1 (Rule #16 correction of the retro's "Story 25.0" label).** The Epic 24 retro states "Story 25.0 carries a concrete action: add the `readOnlyHint` cross-check test." Empirical constraint discovered at this gate: the cross-check reads each tool's `annotations.readOnlyHint`, which lives in the **leaf packages** (`iris-dev/admin/interop/ops/data-mcp`). `baseline-classifications.ts` is in `@iris-mcp/shared`, and shared does NOT (and must not — circular) depend on the leaf packages (verified: leaf `package.json` declares `@iris-mcp/shared: workspace:*`; shared has no leaf deps). So the cross-check cannot live in shared's test suite. Its natural home is **Story 25.1's `validate-prompts.mjs` machinery**, which the spec explicitly builds to "import the five packages' tool arrays + framework tool names" — the exact aggregation the cross-check needs. The substance (readOnlyHint cross-check lands in Epic 25) is honored; only the host story moves 25.0→25.1 for a hard dependency-direction reason. Realizes Rule #44 mechanically. Added as an explicit AC/task to the Story 25.1 file at creation; Story 25.0 stays pure framework plumbing. |
| CR 22.0-D1 (loc-count scan-abort TOCTOU) | Epic 22 | **RE-DEFER.** ObjectScript/dev-tool hardening; Epic 25 (prompts/skills, TS/content) does not touch `ExecuteMCPv2.Loc.*` or `sql.ts`. |
| CR 22.0-D2 (StudioOpenDialog overlap-order) | Epic 22 | **RE-DEFER.** Same — out of prompts scope. |
| CR 22.1-1 (pairloop reqsrc unreachable) | Epic 22 | **RE-DEFER.** Message-diagram library; not touched by Epic 25. |
| CR 22.1-2 (dist-coupling) | Epic 22 | **RE-DEFER.** Not touched by Epic 25. |

The header's "fail on ANY drift" claim is thus mildly overstated for stray files. | Robustness-only. ## Deferred from: lead per-story smoke of story-25.1 (2026-07-08)

- **[CR 25.1-6 / LOW] All-optional-argument prompts reject a `prompts/get` that OMITS `arguments` entirely (must send `{}`).** The lead smoke (built dist, real MCP `Client`) found that the 4 prompts whose declared arguments are ALL optional (`check-system-health` `server?`, `run-external-backup` `server?`, `recover-stuck-production` `production?`+`namespace?`, `audit-security-posture` `server?`) return a clean `-32602 InvalidParams` when a client calls `getPrompt({name})` with no `arguments` key, instead of rendering with the optional args absent. **Root cause is the MCP SDK (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk@1.29.0`), not our code:** `_createRegisteredPrompt` stores `objectFromShape(argsSchema)` → a plain `z.object(shape)`, and the `GetPrompt` handler does `safeParseAsync(z.object(shape), undefined)` which fails ("expected object, received undefined") whenever ANY `argsSchema` is registered. The SDK offers exactly two mutually-exclusive modes: pass an `argsSchema` (args ADVERTISED in `prompts/list` + supplied args parsed, but omitted `arguments` rejected) OR pass none (omitted `arguments` tolerated, but NO arg advertisement + supplied args never reach the callback). CR 25.0-1 already took the "no schema" mode for genuinely zero-argument prompts (`objectscript-review`), which is why those tolerate omission. There is no SDK-supported schema that both advertises optional args AND accepts an omitted top-level `arguments`. **Impact: LOW.** All four prompts render correctly when called with `arguments: {}` (the normal path — MCP clients that build an arguments object from the advertised arg list send `{}` when the user supplies nothing); only the strict "arguments-key-entirely-absent for a prompt that declares optional args" case errors, and it errors cleanly (no crash, a spec-conformant JSON-RPC error). **Suggested resolution (future framework enhancement, Story 25.0 domain):** if full MCP-spec `arguments`-optional conformance for declared-optional-arg prompts is wanted, bypass the SDK's `registerPrompt` GetPrompt handler for these prompts — register `ListPrompts`/`GetPrompt` request handlers directly on the underlying `Server` (mirroring the D6 governance-resource wiring) so the framework controls arg parsing and can coerce an omitted `arguments` to `{}` while still advertising the arg list. Disproportionate to fold into the Story 25.1 content story; the deliverable (per-server prompts served + rendered) is sound. Source: lead smoke, Story 25.1.

## Deferred from: code review of story-25.2 (2026-07-08)

- **[CR 25.2-1 / LOW] Prose count numbers in the prompts docs are not mechanically asserted against the registered catalog.** The doc-rot guard `packages/iris-mcp-all/src/__tests__/docs-prompt-sync.test.ts` asserts every registered prompt name is present in the READMEs, that each owning server lists exactly its own prompts at the hardcoded `EXPECTED_COUNTS` (ops 2 / dev 3 / interop 2 / admin 2), that `iris-data` claims none, that no server claims a foreign server's prompt (added this review), and that "101 tools" is present — but it does NOT assert the human-readable NUMERIC prose: the root README's "**9 prompts**" heading and `tool_support.md`'s per-server tallies ("`ops` 2, `dev` 3, `interop` 2, `admin` 2; `data` none"). If a prompt is added/removed in a future epic, `EXPECTED_COUNTS` + the README name tables are enforced, but the "9 prompts" total and the `tool_support.md` per-server numbers could silently go stale with a green suite. **Impact: LOW** — the primary drift vectors (rename/add/remove/mis-attribution) are already caught; only the derived prose totals are unguarded, and they are visible/adjacent to the enforced tables. **Suggested resolution:** in the doc-rot guard, additionally assert the root README contains a "`<N> prompts`" string matching `prompts.length`, and parse the `tool_support.md` per-server tallies against the per-package registered counts. Fold into a future docs-touching story on the prompts pack (e.g. Epic 26/27 gated-prompt ship, which will move these totals anyway). Source: code review, Story 25.2 (Edge Case Hunter + Blind Hunter LOW).

## Deferred from: code review of story-26.1 (2026-07-09)

All six are Epic-26-own findings (not carried-ledger items — the Story 26.4 burn-down handles the inherited ledger). Each was assessed against the story's own stated threat model ("this handler's OWN guards are the real safety net for a direct-REST caller — make them airtight"). **None is a guard bypass, an AC violation, or able to cause an unsafe/unintended resend** — the primary double-gate invariant (execute requires an affirmative `confirm:true`, which coerces safe) and the "all six guards fire before any `ResendDuplicatedMessage` call" invariant both hold on every path (verified by two independent adversarial hunters + a manual control-flow trace). All six fail safe (under-resend / clean refusal / defense-in-depth), so they are LOW hardening/coverage items appropriate to defer. Each fix touches the bootstrapped, live-deployed `MessageResend.cls`, so applying any requires a coordinated redeploy + `gen:bootstrap` regen (hash change) + `%UnitTest` rerun — dev-story work, best folded into Story 26.2 (TS tool) or a hardening pass, not a mechanical review patch.

- **[CR 26.1-1 / LOW] `dryRun` coerces a non-boolean/truthy-string encoding to execute-eligible (safety flag defaults unsafe).** `MessageResend.cls` `resendFiltered` branch: `Set tDryRun = +tBody.%Get("dryRun")`. `+"true"` / `+"yes"` / `+null` all yield `0`, so `{"action":"resendFiltered","dryRun":"true","confirm":true,...}` treats `dryRun` as false and, combined with `confirm:true`, EXECUTES rather than previewing — the opposite of what a caller sending the string `"true"` intends. **Not a guard bypass:** `confirm` coerces the SAFE way (`+"true"=0` → not confirmed → refused), so no execution is possible without an affirmative real-boolean/numeric `confirm:true`; the double-gate's primary intent signal cannot be coercion-bypassed. Impact LOW: the omitted-default is safe (`tDryRun` defaults `1`), the TS consumer (Story 26.2, Zod) sends real JSON booleans, and to reach this a caller has already explicitly confirmed execution. **Suggested resolution:** default-safe — treat `dryRun` as true unless explicitly boolean/numeric false: `Set tDryRun = 1 If tBody.%IsDefined("dryRun") && (tBody.%Get("dryRun") = 0) { Set tDryRun = 0 }`. Source: Blind Hunter #1 + Edge Case Hunter (type-confusion), code review Story 26.1.

- **[CR 26.1-2 / LOW] Bare-date `to` silently excludes the entire final day.** `NormalizeTimestamp` appends `" 00:00:00"` to a bare date, and the filter uses `TimeCreated <= ?`, so `resendFiltered` with `"to":"2026-07-04"` excludes every message on 2026-07-04 after midnight from BOTH the dry-run count/sample AND the executed set. Impact LOW: fails safe (under-resends, never over-resends) and the omission is visible in the dry-run `matchCount`/`sample` the operator reviews before confirming — but a bare-date `to` is a natural human/agent input and the exclusion is non-obvious. **Suggested resolution:** when `to` is a bare date, append `" 23:59:59.999"` (or make the upper bound exclusive of next-day midnight). Source: Edge Case Hunter #2 + Blind Hunter #8, code review Story 26.1.

- **[CR 26.1-3 / LOW] `headerIds` as a non-empty JSON object (not array) is silently accepted.** `ValidateHeaderIds` guards `'$IsObject(pArr)` but both `%DynamicArray` and `%DynamicObject` answer `%Size()`/`%GetIterator()`, so `{"headerIds":{"x":"82546","y":"82557"}}` passes and its VALUES are used as header IDs. Impact LOW: the values are still validated numeric + bounded 1-100, the resend still requires a running production, an empty object is already rejected (count<1), and the TS layer (Story 26.2 Zod) enforces array type — this is defense-in-depth on a malformed body, not a bypass. **Suggested resolution:** additionally require `pArr.%IsA("%Library.DynamicArray")` in `ValidateHeaderIds`. Source: Edge Case Hunter #1, code review Story 26.1.

- **[CR 26.1-4 / LOW] The `resendFiltered` filtered-execute composition (TOP-N fetch → `ResendBatch`) is not driven by a single unit test.** `TestHttpResendFilteredMatchCountExceedsMaxMessagesGuard` short-circuits at guard (d) before the fetch; Section D exercises `ResendOne`/`ResendBatch` directly (not via the filtered enumeration). Impact LOW: every constituent IS tested independently — the filtered WHERE clause via the dry-run count+sample tests, and `ResendBatch` (with real resend + batch-continuation) via Section D — and the Story 26.3 live smoke (spec §7) mandates a resendFiltered-execute run against the scratch production before epic close, so the untested composition is downstream-covered. **Suggested resolution:** add a `%UnitTest` that inserts N (≤ cap) matching fixtures on a running scratch production, POSTs `dryRun:false,confirm:true`, and asserts N new headers + summary counts; or rely on the mandated 26.3 live smoke. Source: Edge Case Hunter #5, code review Story 26.1.

- **[CR 26.1-5 / LOW] Non-integer `maxMessages` passes bounds and flows into `SELECT TOP`.** `Set tMaxMessages = +tBody.%Get("maxMessages")` is not floored, so `maxMessages:2.5` passes the `<1`/`>500` checks and yields `SELECT TOP 2.5 ID ...`. Impact LOW: fails safe — IRIS either errors (caught + surfaced as a generic SQL error, no resend) or caps LOWER than intended; never over-resends. **Suggested resolution:** floor via `\1` (integer divide) or reject with `ValidateInteger`. Source: Blind Hunter #9 + Edge Case Hunter #3, code review Story 26.1.

- **[CR 26.1-6 / LOW] A fetch-query failure on the execute path is silently swallowed (reports success, resends nothing).** In the `resendFiltered` execute branch, if the TOP-N fetch RS is a non-object or `%SQLCODE < 0` (e.g. transient lock/timeout after the count query succeeded), `tIds(0)` stays `0`, `ResendBatch` loops zero times, and the handler returns a `$$$OK` envelope with `matchCount:N` but `summary.total:0` — no error surfaced (unlike the count query at line 527, which renders a clean SQL-failure envelope). Impact LOW: fails safe (no wrong resend) but is a misleading, unreported failure on a write path. **Suggested resolution:** mirror the count-query's `'$IsObject(tFetchRS) || (%SQLCODE<0)` → restore namespace + `SanitizeError` envelope. Source: Edge Case Hunter #4, code review Story 26.1.

## Deferred from: code review of story-26.2 (2026-07-09)

Epic-26-own finding (NOT a carried-ledger item — the Story 26.4 burn-down handles the inherited ledger). One finding was patched inline during this review (the `headerIdSchema` string-arm asymmetry — `/^\d+$/` accepted `"0"`/`"007"` while the numeric arm rejected `0`; tightened to `/^[1-9]\d*$/` + regression test). The item below is a LOW defensive-consistency gap that is **not reachable with the current Story 26.1 server** (which always emits `summary` and a JSON-boolean `dryRun` on a 200), so it is deferred rather than patched.

- **[CR 26.2-1 / LOW] `message-resend.ts` handler dereferences `result.summary` and branches on `result.dryRun` without the defensive guard its sibling `result.results`/`result.sample` get (`?? []`).** In the `resend` and executed-`resendFiltered` success paths, `renderResendSummary(result.summary)` reads `.succeeded/.total/.failed` unguarded; a malformed HTTP-200 body omitting `summary` (server contract drift) throws a `TypeError` INSIDE the `try` — but the `catch` only converts `IrisApiError`, so it re-throws as an opaque crash instead of the clean `isError` envelope the tool produces for every other failure. Relatedly, `if (result.dryRun)` is a bare-truthiness check on a field the discriminated-union type assumes is always a present boolean: an omitted `dryRun` (or `0`) on a 200 silently falls into the EXECUTED branch (then hits the unguarded `summary`), and a string `"false"` would be truthy and mis-route to the dry-run branch. Impact LOW: NOT reachable today — `MessageResend.cls` always sets `summary` and emits a real boolean `dryRun` on every 200; this is an asymmetric defense-in-depth gap, and it is untested (every mock includes `summary`/boolean `dryRun`). **Suggested resolution:** guard `result.summary` with a fallback (`const summary = result.summary ?? { total: 0, succeeded: 0, failed: 0 }`) mirroring the `?? []` on `results`, and normalize the discriminant (`if (result.dryRun === true)`), so a contract-drift 200 surfaces as a clean tool error rather than an uncaught throw. Source: Blind Hunter #2 + Edge Case Hunter #1/#2 (merged), code review Story 26.2.

## Deferred from: code review of story-26.4 (2026-07-09)

Story 26.4's code review (adversarial three-layer — Blind Hunter / Edge Case Hunter / Acceptance Auditor). The 16-item terminal-disposition audit PASSED (all 16 genuinely terminal; ledger at zero carried-open). **1 HIGH found and FIXED inline + live-verified** (a defective "resolved" fix, not a re-deferral). **3 LOW deferred** (Epic-26-own — permitted per AC 26.4.3 / AC 22.1.7 shape). 3 dismissed (pre-existing format drift with clean new files; a defensible no-test deferral; a positive completeness confirmation).

**Patched inline (recorded for provenance):**
- **[CR 26.4-R1 / HIGH] CR 23.1-5's "resolved" fix silently broke the `areas` GET filter on every call.** `Health.cls` used `Set tParamCount = +$Get(%request.Data("areas"))` to count `areas` query-param occurrences. Verified against `irislib/%CSP/Request.cls`: `Get`/`Set` store values only at `i%Data(name,index)` (subscripted), and `Count()` (:455) computes the count by `$order`-iterating subscripts — **the unsubscripted `i%Data(name)` node holds no count**. So `+$Get(%request.Data("areas"))` = `+""` = 0, the `For 1:1:0` loop never executed, `tRequestedCSV` stayed `""`, and `HealthCheckParseAreas` treated that as "all 9 areas." Because the production `iris_health_check` tool always sends `GET .../monitor/health?areas=<csv>` (`health.ts:836`), the caller's area filter was silently ignored on every call — running all 9 probes and computing the verdict over excluded areas (a regression vs the prior `$Get(%request.Data("areas",1))`, which correctly read the first value). Independently flagged by Blind + Edge hunters, corroborated by the Auditor's no-test note. **Fixed** → `Set tParamCount = %request.Count("areas")`. Redeployed + compiled clean on HSCUSTOM; live-HTTP verified: `?areas=journal`→`['journal']`, `?areas=journal,license`→`['journal','license']`, `?areas=journal&areas=license`→`['journal','license']` (the repeated-param goal now actually works), no-filter→all 9. `HealthCheckTest` 15/15; BOOTSTRAP_VERSION `f16c695aa9ef`→`e5c18edd00c0` (idempotent); `bootstrap.test.ts` 42/42; `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0; frozen baseline `1e62c5ad5bf7` git-clean. `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Health.cls:77`. Source: Blind Hunter #1 + Edge Case Hunter #1 (merged), Auditor corroboration.

**New Story-26.4-own deferred items (all LOW, Epic-26-own — not carried-ledger items):**
- **[CR 26.4-1 / LOW] Compressor `(tReqSrc '= "")` guard also gates the `arrow` branch.** The new `While` term (`Compressor.cls:265`) is in the shared `arrow`||`pairloop` loop, but the dev's UNREACHABLE proof covers only the pairloop producer. For arrows `tReqSrc=tEv.Src`; a hypothetical empty-`Src` arrow would now be denied an unwind it previously got, changing episode grouping. Conservative/safe direction, all episode tests green, arrows realistically always carry a `Src` → LOW. **Suggested resolution:** scope the guard to `tEv.Kind="pairloop"` only, or verify arrow producers always set `Src`. Source: Blind Hunter #2 + Edge Case Hunter #3.
- **[CR 26.4-2 / LOW] CR 23.2-2 closed-with-evidence lacks the in-code doc note the original finding requested.** The evidence (source read proving only canonical `errors` keys are emitted) is genuine and the disposition valid, but no comment was added at `health.ts`'s `evaluate` `checked` filter explaining that out-of-enum keys are dropped and why that's safe. **Suggested resolution:** add the one-line note. Source: Acceptance Auditor.
- **[CR 26.4-3 / LOW] `gen-skills.mjs --check` now flags/removes ANY non-generated file under `skills/`.** The recursive `listAllOnDiskFiles()` (the intended CR 25.1-3 fix) means an incidental `.gitkeep`/`.gitignore`/editor scratch under `skills/` breaks `--check` and is `rmSync`'d in write mode. By-design for a 100%-generated tree with DO-NOT-EDIT headers; scope contained to `skills/`. A mild CI-false-positive/surprising-deletion vector. **Suggested resolution:** an allow-list for intentional non-generated files if any are ever expected under `skills/`. Source: Blind Hunter #3 + Edge Case Hunter #2.

## Epic 27 retro-review gate (2026-07-10)

The `/epic-cycle 27` kickoff retro-review gate read the Epic 26 retrospective (`epic-26-retro-2026-07-09.md`) and this ledger. The inherited ledger stands at **ZERO carried-open** — the Story 26.4 burn-down (Rule #37) drove all previously-carried items terminal. The only open items are Epic-26's **own** review findings, all LOW:

- **CR 26.1-1..6** (Story 26.1 review) — `MessageResend.cls` guard hardening/coverage (dryRun coercion, bare-date `to`, JSON-object `headerIds`, filtered-execute composition test, non-integer `maxMessages`, swallowed fetch-failure).
- **CR 26.2-1** (Story 26.2 review) — `message-resend.ts` unguarded `result.summary`/`result.dryRun` defensive-consistency (not reachable with the current 26.1 server).
- **CR 26.4-1..3** (Story 26.4 review) — Compressor `(tReqSrc '= "")` guard also gates the `arrow` branch; CR 23.2-2 closed-with-evidence lacks the in-code doc note; `gen-skills.mjs --check` now flags/removes any non-generated file under `skills/`.

**Decision: RE-DEFER all 10.** Rationale:
- Epic 27 is a **feature epic** (`iris_env_diff`/`iris_env_promote` on `@iris-mcp/dev` + shared `ToolContext.resolveProfileClient` + a NEW OS `EnvSync.cls` `/dev/doc/hashes` endpoint). Its surface is **disjoint** from every carried item's file (`MessageResend.cls`, `message-resend.ts`, `Compressor.cls`, `health.ts`, `gen-skills.mjs`) — confirmed at kickoff.
- This is the **1st re-deferral** of this batch, **below Rule #37's ≥3-consecutive threshold** that would mandate a dedicated burn-down story. The Rule #37 burn-down for the prior (inherited) batch already ran in Epic 26 — a second burn-down is NOT created.
- **No colliding Story X.0** — the 27.0 slot is the feature story `27-0-profile-client-and-documents-diff`, mirroring the Epics 19/20/21/23/24 feature-epic pattern.

Carried into the Epic 28 retro-review gate as the (small, non-growing) Epic-26-own batch. **Re-deferral count for this batch: 1.**

**Operational note (not a dev story):** Epic 26 retro Action #1 — rebuild + reload the running MCP servers so `iris_message_resend` + the `resend-failed-messages` prompt go live at the MCP layer — is Project-Lead-owned operational work. It ALSO gates Epic 27's live MCP-client smoke of the new `iris_env_diff`/`iris_env_promote` tools and the `promote:execute` enable+reload dance (Stories 27.3/27.4); surfaced to the Project Lead as a Clarification/pause when reached (context item 5).

### Discovered during Story 27.0 dev (2026-07-10) — pre-existing, NOT an Epic-27 regression

- **[gen-skills --check line-ending sensitivity on Windows `autocrlf=true` / LOW] `packages/iris-mcp-all/src/__tests__/gen-skills-stray-file.test.ts` fails on a local Windows checkout.** Independently verified pre-existing (fails on the epic-27 base `4b56e66`; `git diff 4b56e66 -- skills/` empty; Story 27.0 made ZERO changes to `skills/`/`prompts/`/`gen-skills.mjs`). Root cause: the committed `skills/**` blobs are **LF** (correct), but `core.autocrlf=true` with no `.gitattributes` converts `skills/README.md` + `skills/resend-failed-messages/SKILL.md` to CRLF **on disk at checkout**, and `scripts/gen-skills.mjs --check` compares its LF output against the on-disk (CRLF) file → reports "DRIFT". The Epic-26 "green" was a Linux/CI or LF-on-disk state; **CI (Linux) and the committed content are correct** — this is a local-Windows-checkout artifact only, with nothing to commit (`git diff` empty; regenerating restores LF on disk and the test passes). **Real fix:** normalize CRLF→LF before the `--check` content comparison in `gen-skills.mjs`, mirroring `scripts/gen-bootstrap.mjs:72` (`.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n')`); OR add a `.gitattributes` entry (`skills/** text eol=lf`) so the tree stays LF on Windows. Pairs with **CR 26.4-3** (gen-skills `--check` robustness) — route to the same gen-skills hardening pass. For the DURATION of Epic 27 the local working tree is kept LF (regenerated once) so the default suite runs green; the regen is content-identical to the committed blobs, so it is never staged into any Epic-27 commit.

## Deferred from: code review of 27-0-profile-client-and-documents-diff (2026-07-09)

Opus 3-layer adversarial review (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor). Story 27.0 closed `done` — 0 HIGH, 0 patch, all four ACs + Integration AC + governance + bootstrap dual-roster + `onlyInTarget`-never-a-delete invariant CONFIRMED; SHA-256 live-verified against canonical values; `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0 (baseline git-clean); build 6/6, touched suites green. The following 4 LOW items were recorded for a future hardening pass (Epic-27-own; below the Rule #37 ≥3-consecutive re-deferral threshold). **Update (2026-07-10):** CR 27.0-3 was elevated and RESOLVED during the Story 27.0 Lead Smoke Rework (cycle 2) after the AC-27.0.3 live smoke reproduced it; the other 3 (CR 27.0-1, 27.0-2, 27.0-4) remain deferred. The **cycle-2 rework re-review** (Opus 3-layer, scoped to the TS-only namespace-routing delta) confirmed the fix correct + zero scope-creep and added ONE new LOW (**CR 27.0-5**, below):

- **[CR 27.0-1 / LOW (Blind+Edge, both initially MEDIUM) — Rule #38 wide-spec gate is partial] `CheckWideSpec` (`src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/EnvSync.cls:131`) refuses only a comma-part equal to a bare `*`; whole-TYPE scans (`*.cls`/`*.mac`/`*.int`/`*.inc`) bypass it and run a whole-namespace scan on BOTH profiles concurrently.** Live-confirmed: `CheckWideSpec("*.cls",0)`→OK(1), `CheckWideSpec("*",0)`→refused, `CheckWideSpec("MyPkg.*.cls",0)`→OK(1). **Downgraded to LOW (not auto-patched):** the user-facing `iris_env_diff` description accurately scopes its promise to a bare `*`; the docs INTENTIONALLY use `*.mac` as an example (`MyPkg.*.cls,*.mac`, in EnvSync.cls class-doc + env-diff.ts description + inputSchema), so per-type scans are an intended usage and a strengthening fix would break the documented example — the fix is therefore AMBIGUOUS (two defensible resolutions, per the Edge Hunter), not an unambiguous patch; the origin-precedent `iris_loc_count` (Rule #38's source) gates NOTHING and lets a wide spec 504 as documented, so EnvSync is already stricter than precedent; the failure is a recoverable ≤60s Web-Gateway 504 with an existing `allowWide` escape hatch. **Suggested resolution (design call for the lead / Story 27.1, which touches this endpoint):** EITHER (a) strengthen `CheckWideSpec` to also refuse a part whose stem before a trailing `cls/mac/int/inc` extension is `*` (i.e. `*.cls`/`*.mac`/…) AND update the three `*.mac` doc examples to a scoped form; OR (b) keep the bare-`*` gate and document that per-type scans rely on `allowWide` + the scoped-spec guidance. Source: Blind Hunter #1 + Edge Case Hunter (merged).
- **[CR 27.0-2 / LOW] `resolveProfileClient` establishment errors escape the tool's own error envelope.** In `env-diff.ts:333` the resolve-phase `try/catch` catches only `ProfileResolutionError` and rethrows everything else, so an `IrisApiError`/network failure establishing the `source`/`target` profile is rethrown to the framework's generic handler instead of the tool's friendly `Error diffing environments (source=…, target=…): …` envelope (which the later fetch-phase catch DOES produce). Consistent with the `loc.ts` rethrow convention and the framework still returns a clean error, so LOW. **Suggested resolution:** also catch `IrisApiError` in the resolve-phase `catch` and return the same `Error diffing environments …` envelope. Source: Blind Hunter #3.
- ~~**[CR 27.0-3 / LOW] Omitting `namespace` silently compares each profile's (possibly different) default namespace, and the report hides it.**~~ **RESOLVED (2026-07-10, Story 27.0 Lead Smoke Rework cycle 2).** Originally: `env-diff.ts:346` set `source.namespace`/`target.namespace` to `""` when the caller omitted `namespace`; if two profiles have different default namespaces the result was an all-drift diff with no signal that two DIFFERENT namespaces were compared. Elevated from deferred to fix-now by the Project Lead after the AC-27.0.3 live smoke reproduced it against real `default`(HSCUSTOM)/`sademo`(SADEMO) profiles. **Actual fix (TS-only — no ObjectScript/bootstrap/baseline change, `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` stayed `1e2008753853`):** rather than the originally-suggested OS-side echo (`/dev/doc/hashes` returning `$NAMESPACE`), the fix resolves each side's namespace on the TS side, which is simpler and needs no endpoint round-trip: added a public read-only `IrisHttpClient.namespace` getter (`packages/shared/src/http-client.ts`, `get namespace() { return this.config.namespace; }` — additive/back-compat, Rule #19), then `env-diff.ts` computes `sourceNamespace = input.namespace ?? sourceClient.namespace` / `targetNamespace = input.namespace ?? targetClient.namespace` independently per side (an explicit `namespace` still overrides both identically) and echoes the resolved value in `structuredContent.source.namespace`/`target.namespace` (never `""`). New coverage: 2 unit tests on the getter (`http-client.test.ts`), 2 rewritten `env-diff.test.ts` tests (two differently-namespaced mock profiles route their own namespace on the wire + in the echo when omitted; an explicit `namespace` overrides both). `pnpm turbo run test`: 13/13 tasks green, all 7 packages, 0 failures (`@iris-mcp/shared` 705/705 [+2], `@iris-mcp/dev` 401/401). `gen:bootstrap` idempotent; `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0; both baseline files git-clean. Source: Blind Hunter #4 + Acceptance Auditor (nit); fix verified by Amelia (dev agent) cycle-2 rework.
- **[CR 27.0-4 / LOW] A single unreadable/deleted document aborts the entire diff.** In `ComputeHashes` (`EnvSync.cls:171`) any per-document `HashDoc` `$$$ISERR` `Quit`s the loop and fails the whole request; a doc deleted between enumeration and hashing, or a locked/mapped doc `GetTextAsArray` cannot read, makes a many-document scan all-or-nothing. LOW (fail-loud is acceptable; no correctness impact). **Suggested resolution:** skip/annotate an unreadable document (e.g. a `skipped` bucket) rather than failing the batch. Source: Blind Hunter #5.
- **[CR 27.0-5 / LOW (cycle-2 rework re-review, 2026-07-10)] An explicit `namespace:""` (empty string) bypasses the "never blank" guarantee added by the CR 27.0-3 fix.** In `env-diff.ts:353-354`, `sourceNamespace = input.namespace ?? sourceClient.namespace` (and the target counterpart) uses NULLISH coalescing, so an empty string — which is not nullish — passes straight through: both sides send `body.namespace:""` on the wire AND echo `""` in `structuredContent.source/target.namespace`, contradicting the code's own `env-diff.ts:351-352` "always a resolved, non-empty namespace" comment. The `namespace` schema is `z.string().optional()` with NO `.min(1)` (unlike `source`/`target`, which both carry `.min(1)`). **LOW / not auto-patched:** degenerate input (no realistic LLM/operator caller sends an explicit `""` — the schema description says "Omit to use each profile's OWN configured default"), recoverable failure, and pre-existing-adjacent (cycle-1 also echoed `""` for this input). The fix is AMBIGUOUS (two defensible resolutions) → a design call, not an unambiguous patch: EITHER (a) reject an explicit empty via `namespace: z.string().min(1).optional()` (strict — surfaces a clear validation error), OR (b) coerce `""`→omitted (`const ns = input.namespace?.trim() || undefined` before the `??`, treating blank as "use default"). Route to the lead / Story 27.1 (which touches this endpoint). Source: cycle-2 re-review Blind + Edge (merged).

### Lead smoke findings — Story 27.0 cycle-2 re-smoke (2026-07-10, built dist + live IRIS)

The cycle-2 re-smoke PASSED (per-profile namespace routing proven live — output echoes `HSCUSTOM`/`SADEMO`, was `""`; endpoint namespace-switch proven live — a nonexistent `namespace` errors, a valid one enumerates that NS; both Rule #38 refusals hold). Two findings surfaced:

- **[ENV / Story 27.4 residual-risk CORRECTION — HIGH-priority for 27.4 planning, not a code defect] On THIS dev instance, HSCUSTOM and SADEMO SHARE their code databases — documents-domain drift CANNOT be seeded between them.** Authoritatively verified via `iris_doc_get metadataOnly` (bypassing `iris_env_diff`): `ZZZEnvSmoke.Probe.cls` (a scratch class deployed to HSCUSTOM's default code DB), `HS.Util.Trace.Request.cls`, and `SessionAgent.Sample.Production.cls` ALL exist in BOTH namespaces with IDENTICAL etags. This **contradicts the Project Lead's 2026-07-10 answer ("Separate code DBs — drift IS seedable")** — at least for the default user-code DB, the HS DB, and sample productions, everything HSCUSTOM sees SADEMO also sees, identically. **Consequence for Story 27.4 (AC 27.4.2 capstone — "seed one class edit … diff detects exactly the seeded drift"):** a documents-domain code edit changes BOTH namespaces identically (→ no drift), so the capstone CANNOT demonstrate a real documents-domain diff between these two namespaces on one instance. **Resolution options for 27.4 (Lead decision at 27.4 kickoff):** (a) seed the capstone drift via a genuinely per-NAMESPACE domain instead — `mappings`/`defaultSettings`/`config` are namespace-scoped (Story 27.1 delivers those); (b) if a class DB mapped to only ONE of the two namespaces exists, deploy the seeded class there; (c) record documents-domain live-drift as explicit residual risk (the stakeholder already accepted "no cross-instance coverage" — this extends it to "no cross-namespace documents drift on the shared-DB dev instance"). The tool itself is CORRECT: it reported `identical` because the docs ARE genuinely identical across the namespaces (not a routing bug — namespace switch is live-verified). Source: Story 27.0 lead cycle-2 re-smoke.
- **[CR 27.0-6 / LOW] `EnvSync.cls` SwitchNamespace-failure error text is truncated.** A `namespace` that fails to switch (e.g. a nonexistent namespace) surfaces as `#5001: ObjectScript error: ` with an EMPTY tail (observed live for `namespace:"ZZZNOPE"`) — the refusal correctly mutates nothing, but the message neither names the bad namespace nor carries the underlying `<NAMESPACE>`-style reason, so a caller can't tell WHY. LOW (the guard works; only the diagnostic is thin). **Suggested resolution:** in `EnvSync.cls`'s namespace-switch failure path (`:81-88`), include the offending namespace + the sanitized `SwitchNamespace` `%Status` text in the error (e.g. `"cannot switch to namespace 'X': <reason>"`). Route to the lead / Story 27.1 (which touches this endpoint). Source: Story 27.0 lead cycle-2 re-smoke.

## Deferred from: code review of 27-1-diff-domains-and-redaction (2026-07-10)

Opus 3-layer adversarial review (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor). Story 27.1 closed `done` — **1 HIGH found + FIXED inline + mutation-verified (CR 27.1-1, the config falsy-value bug); 2 patches applied (CR 27.1-1 code + CR 27.1-6 File-List doc); 5 deferred (below); 5 dismissed.** All three ACs + the Integration AC + the security-critical redaction (AC 27.1.2, airtight across all buckets + text render, verified by all three layers) + TS-only/governance/bootstrap constraints CONFIRMED; the Rule #47 "reuse targets" table re-verified against all four ObjectScript endpoints (`Config.cls:MappingList` raw array, `Interop.cls:DefaultSettingsList` `{settings,count}` object, `Security.cls:WebAppList` raw array w/ `resource` omitted, `SystemConfig.cls:GetConfig` `{section,properties:11-key}`; routes GET/GET/GET/POST). `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0 (141 frozen / 198 live / 57 post-foundation — unchanged; `iris_env_diff` stays `mutates:"read"`, no new key); `governance-baseline.ts` git-clean; `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` `1e2008753853` unchanged; no `src/ExecuteMCPv2/**`/`gen-bootstrap.mjs`/`bootstrap-classes.ts` change. Full `@iris-mcp/dev` suite 430/430 green (incl. +2 CR-27.1-1 regression tests), typecheck + lint clean. The 5 deferred items are Epic-27-own (below the Rule #37 ≥3-consecutive re-deferral threshold); the 2 MED items should be surfaced at Story 27.2/27.4 planning. **Update (2026-07-10):** CR 27.1-2 and CR 27.1-3 were both elevated and RESOLVED during the Story 27.1 Lead Smoke Rework (cycle 2) after the lead's live per-story smoke reproduced CR 27.1-3's failure mode against the real configured profiles ("a failed smoke is HIGH, never deferrable"); the other 3 (CR 27.1-4, 27.1-5, 27.1-7) remain deferred, below the Rule #37 threshold.

- ~~**[CR 27.1-2 / MED (security-hardening) — credential name list is narrower than the real interop credential surface] `isCredentialSetting` (`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/env-diff.ts:517`) matches only `/password|secret|key/i`.**~~ **RESOLVED (2026-07-10, Story 27.1 Lead Smoke Rework cycle 2).** Originally: SDS rows carried credentials under names containing NONE of those substrings — `Token`, `AccessToken`, `BearerToken`, `RefreshToken`, `OAuthToken`, `Passphrase`, `Pwd`, `Pass`, `Credential`/`Credentials`, `Salt`, `Auth` — so their plaintext `value` flowed UNREDACTED. The Project Lead elected the "expand the classifier" branch of the review's own suggested resolution (fail-safe direction, no spec-scope objection). **Actual fix (TS-only, no ObjectScript/bootstrap/baseline change):** `isCredentialSetting` broadened from `/password|secret|key/i` to `/password|secret|key|token|pwd|passphrase|credential|cert|private|salt/i` — a strict superset (every prior match still matches; the pre-existing non-matches `Timeout`/`RetryCount` remain unaffected), preserving the name-not-value contract unchanged (the pre-existing `ProxyURL`-credential-looking-VALUE test still passes untouched). New coverage: a dedicated `isCredentialSetting` unit test for the six new-term examples the Lead named (`AccessToken`/`Pwd`/`Passphrase`/`ClientCredential`/`PrivateKey`/`Salt`, all now redact), plus an end-to-end `diffDefaultSettings` test proving an `AccessToken` setting (matched ONLY by a new term) redacts correctly in the `differs` bucket with the plaintext absent from the full serialized result. Tool description's redaction note updated to list all ten terms. `pnpm turbo run test`: 13/13 tasks green, `@iris-mcp/dev` 438/438 (net +8 across both cycle-2 fixes combined — see CR 27.1-3 below for the isolation-side test count). Source: Blind Hunter #2 (Edge/Auditor concurred it was spec-conformant-but-narrow); fix verified by Amelia (dev agent) cycle-2 rework.
- ~~**[CR 27.1-3 / MED (robustness / UX) — no per-domain error isolation; the widened default fails the WHOLE diff on a subsystem-absent namespace] The five domain fetch/diff blocks share ONE `try/catch` (`env-diff.ts:973-1029`); any single domain's `IrisApiError` returns a single error envelope and discards all already-computed domains.**~~ **RESOLVED (2026-07-10, Story 27.1 Lead Smoke Rework cycle 2).** Originally assessed "latent on the two configured profiles" at cycle-1 review — the lead's live per-story smoke DISPROVED that assessment: a bare `iris_env_diff(source,target)` call aborted on `documents`' missing-`spec` guard (hiding the four no-spec domains), and `defaultSettings` against a non-interop namespace hard-errored and discarded every other domain's already-computed diff, both against the REAL configured profiles. Elevated from deferred to fix-now by the Project Lead. **Actual fix (TS-only, no ObjectScript/bootstrap/baseline change, `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` stayed `1e2008753853`):** each of the five domain blocks (`documents`/`mappings`/`defaultSettings`/`webapps`/`config`) now runs its OWN try/catch (mirrors `Health.cls`'s per-area isolation, Epic 23 / Rule #41) instead of one shared try/catch. An `IrisApiError` — or, for `documents`, the missing-`spec` guard, relocated from a whole-call early `validationError()` return into its own domain block — is isolated to THAT domain: a sanitized message lands in a new `domainErrors` map, surfaced as `structuredContent.errors: {<domain>: "<message>"}` (present only when ≥1 domain errored), and the loop continues with the remaining domains; `summary.driftCount`/`identicalCount` roll up only the domains that produced a diff. `isError` is computed from a canonical, de-duplicated requested-domains list: `true` only when EVERY requested domain errors, explicit `false` for a partial failure (mirrors the existing `compile.ts` "tool succeeded, part of the result is an error" convention), and omitted entirely (matching the pre-existing all-success contract) when nothing errored. `content[].text` renders a per-domain `ERROR:` line in place of the normal section for a failed domain, plus an aggregate "N of M requested domain(s) failed" callout. A non-`IrisApiError` throw (a genuine bug) is deliberately NOT isolated — it still propagates/rethrows out of the whole handler exactly as before this fix (proven by a dedicated regression test), so isolation never masks a real defect. This fix is paired with removing `documents` from the default domain set (a Lead-approved, intentional deviation from spec §3's literal "default all 5" — flagged for the Story 27.4 docs rollup and the spec owner) — together they resolve both live-smoke failure scenarios. New dedicated suite `packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/__tests__/env-diff-domain-isolation.test.ts` (5 tests): one domain hard-errors while the other three default domains succeed (`isError:false`, summary excludes the failed domain); every requested domain errors (`isError:true`, clean envelope); the `documents` missing-spec guard isolated alongside a succeeding domain; a non-`IrisApiError` still propagates and aborts domains not yet processed; an all-succeeding call has no `errors` key at all and `isError` stays `undefined`. `pnpm turbo run test`: 13/13 tasks green across all 7 packages, 2,415 tests total, 0 failures (`@iris-mcp/dev` 438/438 — was 430/430 post-cycle-1-review, net +8 combined with the CR 27.1-2 fix above; every other package unaffected). `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0 (141 frozen / 198 live / 57 post-foundation, unchanged — no new governance key; `iris_env_diff` stays scalar `mutates:"read"`); both baseline files git-clean; `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` confirmed unchanged. Source: Edge Case Hunter #1/#2 (rated HIGH by Edge, downgraded to MED at cycle-1 review — the smoke proved the Edge Hunter's original HIGH assessment was the more accurate one); fix verified by Amelia (dev agent) cycle-2 rework.
- **[CR 27.1-4 / LOW — unguarded `response.result` in two fetchers, inconsistent with their siblings] `fetchConfig` (`env-diff.ts:662`, `response.result.properties` → `diffConfig` `Object.entries`) and `fetchDefaultSettings` (`env-diff.ts:545`, `response.result.settings ?? []` — guards `.settings` but not `.result`) dereference `response.result` without a guard, whereas `fetchMappings`/`fetchWebapps` use `response.result ?? []`.** If an endpoint returns a non-throwing envelope whose `result` is null/absent, `Object.entries(undefined)` / `.settings of undefined` throws a `TypeError` — which is NOT an `IrisApiError`, so it escapes the `IrisApiError`-only catch and rethrows as an unhandled crash instead of the friendly `apiErrorEnvelope`. Shouldn't-happen malformed-envelope path (LOW). **Suggested resolution:** align config/SDS fetchers with the mappings/webapps guard pattern (optional-chain `response.result?.…` + a sensible empty default). Not auto-patched to avoid a subtly-wrong silent-empty defensive change on a shouldn't-happen path. Source: Blind Hunter #3 + Edge Case Hunter #3 (merged).
- **[CR 27.1-5 / LOW — `defaultSettings` compares only `value`; a `deployable`/`description` flip is invisible] `diffDefaultSettings` projects `(entry) => entry.value` (`env-diff.ts:563`), so an SDS row with an identical `value` but a differing `deployable` flag (a functional deployment attribute the server returns, `Interop.cls:2286`) or `description` reads as `identical`.** Spec Task 2 explicitly defines the compared value as `value` (conformant), but the drift is silently unsurfaced. **Suggested resolution:** compare `deployable` (and document `description` as intentionally excluded), or document the value-only comparison in the tool description. Source: Edge Case Hunter #4.
- **[CR 27.1-7 / LOW — a mapping row missing `database` yields `MappingValue.database === undefined` against a non-optional `string`] The server's `MappingList` (`Config.cls:493-500`) only emits `database` when the per-row `Config.Map*.Get` succeeds (`If $$$ISOK(tSC2)`); a row whose `Get` fails carries only `name`/`type`/`namespace`.** `mappingValue` (`env-diff.ts:448`) copies `entry.database` (undefined) into a field typed `database: string` (`:97`) — a type-lie, and a spurious `differs` (`{database: undefined}` serializes as `{}`) if only one side's row lacks it. Rare path (per-row `Get` failure), no crash. **Suggested resolution:** mark `database` optional and normalize a missing value in `mappingValuesEqual`, mirroring the `collation`/`lockDatabase` handling. Source: Edge Case Hunter #6.

**Cycle-2 re-review (2026-07-10) — additional LOW robustness edges (all shouldn't-happen paths; DEFERRED, no cycle-3 — the isolation directive is met for the REAL `IrisApiError`/subsystem-absent failure mode, re-smoke-confirmed live):**
- **[CR 27.1-8 / LOW] `Promise.all` mixed-rejection non-determinism.** Each domain block's `await Promise.all([fetchX(source), fetchX(target)])` rejects with whichever side settles first; if the two sides fail with DIFFERENT error classes (source `TypeError`, target `IrisApiError`), which wins is timing-dependent — a genuine-bug `TypeError` could be masked by an `IrisApiError` and treated as an isolated per-domain error. (`Promise.all` DOES attach handlers to all inputs → NO unhandledRejection; the Blind/Edge unhandledRejection concern is incorrect.) Very rare (simultaneous two-sided different-type failure). **Resolution:** `Promise.allSettled` / per-side `.catch`. Source: cycle-2 Blind #3 + Edge #2.
- **[CR 27.1-9 / LOW] Raw-args `domains:[]` → vacuous "0 drifted, 0 identical" all-clear.** Handler `input.domains ?? [...DEFAULT]` normalizes only `undefined`, not `[]`; a direct `handler({source,target,domains:[]})` (bypassing zod `.min(1)`) runs no domains + a misleading empty-diff success. Raw-args path only. **Resolution:** `domains?.length ? domains : DEFAULT` or reject. Source: cycle-2 Blind #1 + Edge #4.
- **[CR 27.1-10 / LOW] `config` `onlyInTarget` text render omits the "informational — NOT a deletion signal" annotation** the other four domains carry (`renderConfigSection`). Doc/description assert "every domain." Cosmetic. **Resolution:** add the annotation. Source: cycle-2 Blind #4.
- **[CR 27.1-11 / LOW] Domains run sequentially, not concurrently** — only the two sides within a domain are concurrent; the 5 domains await one after another (latency). Sequential order also makes the non-`IrisApiError`-abort ordering deterministic in tests. **Resolution:** run isolated domain blocks concurrently if latency matters. Source: cycle-2 Blind #5.
- **[CR 27.1-12 / LOW] Within-`mappings` partial failure discards succeeded types.** `fetchMappings`'s 3-way `Promise.all([global,routine,package])` rejects if any type errors → 2 succeeded types thrown away, whole `mappings` domain marked errored. **Resolution:** `Promise.allSettled` the 3 types + merge. Source: cycle-2 Edge #5.

These 5 + CR 27.1-4/-5/-7 are Epic-27-own deferrals (below the Rule #37 ≥3-consecutive threshold); CR 27.1-4 (malformed-`result`) + CR 27.1-8 (`Promise.all`) are the strongest hardening-pass candidates — surface at Story 27.2/27.4 planning.

Unreachable for the `plan` action over the JSON MCP transport (a `diff` deserialized from JSON contains neither), so NOT patched in 27.2. Relevant to Story 27.3, which will re-derive the hash from a possibly-in-process object: **Suggested resolution (27.3):** wrap the `execute` re-hash in try/catch (or guard `computePlanHash`) so a malformed diff yields a clean stale-plan/validation refusal rather than an unhandled rejection. But `execute` (27.3) WRITES to `target`: **Suggested resolution (27.3):** before any write, assert the caller's declared `target` (and `source`) equal the diff's own profile refs and refuse on mismatch, so a wrong-direction plan can't be executed against the wrong instance. Regression test added (`env-promote-execute.test.ts` — "a 'target' that does NOT match the diff's own target profile is refused (Gate 3c)").

Full `@iris-mcp/dev` suite green (503 tests, +5 review regression tests), type-check + lint clean.

**PATCHES APPLIED INLINE (not deferred — recorded for provenance):**
- **CR 27.3-P1 (Gate 3b plan/diff step consistency)** — the plan-hash gate bound `diff`↔`planHash` but NOT `plan.steps`↔`diff`; a hand-edited plan keeping a valid diff+hash could swap a step's `subject` to a source item OUTSIDE the reviewed diff and (given target governance + source existence) promote it. Added Gate 3b: re-derive `buildPlan(diff).steps` and require the supplied `plan.steps` to match exactly (identity fields + order); refuse-before-any-write on mismatch. No-op for a genuine caller; also subsumes the duplicate-step-index tamper vector. Raised by all 3 layers (Blind/Edge MED, Auditor LOW). Regression tests: subject-tamper refused + genuine-plan-passes.
- **CR 27.3-P2 (credential scrub on the SDS write FAILURE path)** — the success path never renders the re-fetched (possibly credential) value, but a target-POST error whose text echoed the value would reach the rendered per-step `error`. Added `scrubValueFromError` (Rule #9 min-length-gated literal scrub) around the SDS target POST. Regression test proves a value-echoing IRIS error is scrubbed to `[REDACTED]` while the diagnostic is preserved.
- **CR 27.3-P3 (fail-closed on non-array source doc content)** — an anomalous source `GET` returning 200 without a `content` array fell back to `[]`, PUTting empty content + recompiling (`flags=cuk`) → could blank a target document. Now throws (clean per-step failure) instead of writing empty content. Regression test proves no empty PUT / no compile reaches the target.
- **CR 27.3-P4 (accurate summary)** — the execute summary said "Nothing on the target was ever deleted." unconditionally, but an `updateMapping` step DOES issue a `delete`. Reworded to the true invariant: "No item that exists only on the target was ever removed."
- **CR 27.3-P5 (Gate 3c wrong-instance write guard)** — resolves the routed **CR 27.2-2**: `executeAction` now refuses before any write when the diff's own `source`/`target` profile ref mismatches the caller's args. Regression test added.
- **CR 27.3-P6 (test-title fix)** — a compile-failure test title claimed a later doc "is still reported 'completed'" while asserting "skipped"; corrected to match the asserted (documented) behavior.

**DEFERRED (Epic-27-own; all LOW; below the Rule #37 ≥3-consecutive threshold):**
- **[CR 27.3-1 / LOW — `documents` batch under-reports an applied doc as `skipped`]** In the compile-error branch, a doc whose PUT succeeded AND whose batched compile returned `errors:[]` but sits AFTER an earlier compile failure in `putOk` order is reported `skipped`, not `completed` — even though it was actually put AND compiled to the target. Same shape in the put-failure branch (earlier-put docs → `skipped`). A deliberate, documented, tested dev choice that errs CONSERVATIVE (never hides a failure; a re-run is idempotent), so not blocking. **Suggested resolution:** in the compile branch, report each `putOk` doc by ITS OWN per-doc compile result (`completed` when `errors:[]`) independent of sibling order, since the single batched compile call already ran for all of them. Source: Blind LOW / Edge MED / Auditor LOW (merged). [`env-promote.ts` `runDocumentsBatch`]
- **[CR 27.3-2 / LOW — `updateMapping` delete+create is non-atomic]** If the target `delete` succeeds and the following `create` fails (transient/validation), the pre-existing target mapping is gone with no rollback (the step reports `failed` and halts). Inherent to IRIS mappings (`Config.cls` has no update op; mirrors `iris_mapping_manage`'s documented delete+create) and gated by a source re-fetch (so only a mapping the source ALSO has is ever replaced — never a target-only item). **Suggested resolution:** create-then-delete order, or a compensating re-create on create-failure, if IRIS mapping semantics allow. Source: Blind MED (downgraded — inherent + mirrors the existing admin tool). [`env-promote.ts` `dispatchUpdateMapping`]
- **[CR 27.3-3 / LOW — `webapps` plan detail says "create" but the op is `modify`]** For an `onlyInSource` webapp the plan (27.2) labels the step "create web application (exists on source only)" but the dispatched op is `modify` — which is SPEC-FAITHFUL (feature-spec §ordering line 109 states "webapp modify") and DELIBERATELY tested (the dev/QA tests assert `action:"modify"` for an `onlyInSource` webapp). A source-only webapp that does NOT exist on target fails the `modify` cleanly (fails safe, halts). **Suggested resolution:** the LEAD confirms whether promoting a NEW webapp (target-side CREATE) is in v1 scope; if not, reword 27.2's `buildWebappsSteps` "create web application" detail to "modify web application" to remove the label/behavior mismatch. Source: Edge MED (downgraded to LOW — spec-faithful, fails safe). [`env-promote.ts` `dispatchModifyWebApp` / 27.2 `buildWebappsSteps`]
- **[CR 27.3-4 / LOW — empty configured namespace yields a misattributing error]** A misconfigured profile with `namespace === ""` (and no `namespace` override) resolves `srcNs`/`targetNs` to `""`; mapping/SDS/config steps then query `?namespace=` and surface a per-step "not found … may have changed since the plan was generated" error that misattributes the real cause (empty namespace). No crash; caught as clean per-step failures. **Suggested resolution:** guard `srcNs`/`targetNs` non-empty after resolution and refuse with a namespace-specific message. Source: Edge LOW. [`env-promote.ts` namespace resolution]

**DISMISSED (rationale recorded, no action):**
- **Uncaught `negotiateVersion` throw loses per-step statuses** (Blind MED) — FALSE: `negotiateVersion` (`atelier.ts`) wraps its `GET /api/atelier/` in a try/catch and returns `DEFAULT_VERSION` on ANY error; it cannot throw. Verified in source.
- **`iris_mapping_manage:delete` omitted from the local `EXECUTE_WRITE_FAMILY`** (Blind LOW) — correctly handled: `:delete` is a frozen-baseline member classified `write` in `BASELINE_ACTION_CLASSIFICATIONS`, so `defaultSeed` short-circuits on baseline membership (→ enabled) and `presetSeed`/read-only reads the baseline classification (→ blocked). The local map only needs to supply the ONE post-foundation key (`iris_default_settings_manage:set`). Verified against `governance.ts`.
- **`?ignoreConflict=1` disables target concurrency detection** (Blind LOW) — inherent to promote ("source wins"); documented design intent, not a defect.

## Epic 28 retro-review gate (2026-07-11)

The `/epic-cycle 28` kickoff retro-review gate read the Epic 27 retrospective (`epic-27-retro-2026-07-11.md`) and this ledger. Carried-open items entering the gate:

- **Epic-26-own batch (10 LOW):** CR 26.1-1..6, CR 26.2-1, CR 26.4-1..3 — **re-deferral count entering: 1** (Epic 27 re-deferred once).
- **Epic-27-own items (all LOW):** CR 27.0-1/-2/-4/-5/-6 (EnvSync.cls wide-spec gate, env-diff.ts resolve-phase envelope + empty-namespace edge + SwitchNamespace error text); CR 27.1-4/-5/-7/-8/-9/-10/-11/-12 (env-diff.ts fetcher guards, value-only SDS compare, optional mapping db, Promise.all edges, sequential domains); CR 27.3-1/-2/-3/-4 (env-promote.ts documents-batch skip-reporting, non-atomic updateMapping, webapps modify-vs-create label, empty-namespace misattribution); CR 27.4-1 (pre-existing `docs/migration-v1-v2.md` "100 tools" stale count). The gen-skills CRLF-on-Windows artifact (§27.0 discovery) — **RESOLVED at this gate** by the ride-along `.gitattributes` below.

**Decision: RE-DEFER all carried items.** Rationale:
- **Epic 28 is a FEATURE epic** — SQL Performance Advisor: a new `advise` action on the existing `iris_sql_analyze` (`@iris-mcp/dev`) + a NEW ObjectScript handler `POST /dev/sql/advise-data` (this tool's first OS handler; bootstrap bump in Story 28.1) + a TS heuristic engine with Rule #36 reference-captured fixtures. Its surface (`iris_sql_analyze` tool + a new advisor OS class + a new heuristic-engine TS module) is **disjoint** from every carried item's file (`MessageResend.cls`, `message-resend.ts`, `Compressor.cls`, `health.ts`, `gen-skills.mjs`, `EnvSync.cls`, `env-diff.ts`, `env-promote.ts`, `docs/migration-v1-v2.md`) — confirmed at kickoff.
- This is the **2nd re-deferral** of the Epic-26-own batch — **below Rule #37's ≥3-consecutive threshold**. Per the Epic 27 retro §8 Action #2, **a 3rd re-defer at Epic 29 trips the threshold → the Epic 29 plan MUST include a dedicated burn-down story.**
- **No colliding Story X.0** — the 28.0 slot is the feature story `28-0-advisor-probe-matrix` (a mandatory Rules #14/#16 probe), mirroring the Epics 19/20/21/23/24/27 feature-epic pattern.

**RESOLVED at this gate (Epic 27 retro §8 Action #1, ride-along hygiene):**
- **[gen-skills `--check` CRLF-on-Windows artifact / LOW → RESOLVED]** Added `.gitattributes` (`* text=auto eol=lf`) at the repo root. `core.autocrlf=true` with no `.gitattributes` was converting the LF-committed `skills/**` blobs to CRLF on a Windows checkout, so `scripts/gen-skills.mjs --check` compared its LF output against the on-disk CRLF file and reported phantom DRIFT. The `.gitattributes` pins LF on checkout, ending the noise repo-wide. Verified: adding it triggered **zero** renormalization churn (`git status` clean apart from the new file) — the repo already stores LF, so this only aligns Windows checkout behavior. Supersedes the narrower deferred fixes (the `skills/** text eol=lf` scope and the gen-skills.mjs CRLF-normalize-before-compare code change), which are now moot.

**Carried into the Epic 29 retro-review gate** as the (small, non-growing) Epic-26-own batch (**re-deferral count after Epic 28: 2**) + the Epic-27-own LOW items. **Rule #37 watch: a re-defer at Epic 29 is the 3rd consecutive for the Epic-26-own batch → plan the burn-down story at Epic 29.**

**Process notes for Epic 28 (from the Epic 27 retro §8):**
- Rules #49/#50 are in-scope review lenses for any plan-comparison logic in the advisor (the heuristic engine compares plan text against expected markers — Rule #50's item-identity-key discipline and Rule #49's oracle discipline apply).
- Rule #36 is BINDING: all advisor fixture expectations must be reference-captured from live IRIS plans (capture command cited in test comments), never hand-reasoned.
- Story 28.0 is a MANDATORY Rules #14/#16 live probe (plan markers VERBATIM, dictionary surface, tune-stats surface, statement-workload availability).

## Deferred from: code review of 28-0-advisor-probe-matrix (2026-07-11)

Story 28.0 is a probe/spec story shipping NO production code — its review confirmed the 3 Rule #47 corrections TRUE against source, the no-drift invariant, and (via independent live read-only spot-checks) the load-bearing plan-marker + `%Dictionary.CompiledIndex` + `stale-stats`-warning findings. Both items below are **forward hazards for Stories 28.2/28.3**, not defects in 28.0 (which has no code surface to patch). Epic-28-own deferrals — below Rule #37's ≥3 threshold.

- **[CR 28.0-1 / LOW — `parsePlanIndexes()` conflates `temp-file` tokens with real index names]** The existing `indexUsage` parser regex (`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/sqlAnalyze.ts:114`) matches `temp-file` as a map/index alternative, so a temp-file plan (`Read temp-file A, …`) yields `["A"]` in its "indexes" list. Spec §4 reuses `parsePlanIndexes()` output "as-is" for the `unused-index` heuristic, and `plan-anomaly` keys off temp-file markers — so Story 28.2's engine must NOT treat a temp-file label (e.g. `A`) as a real index name when it consumes this parser's output. **Suggested resolution:** in Story 28.2, either filter `temp-file`-derived tokens out of the index list fed to `unused-index`, or (cleaner) have `/dev/sql/advise-data` return the index dictionary rows (`%Dictionary.CompiledIndex`) as the authoritative index universe and use plan-text index tokens only for the touched-vs-declared cross-check. Source: Edge/forward review of the reused surface (Rule #47 lens). [`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/sqlAnalyze.ts:114`; spec §4 `unused-index`/`plan-anomaly` rows]
- **[CR 28.0-2 / LOW — AC 28.0.1 "subquery/join strategies": subquery not separately captured]** The probe captured a 2-table join and `GROUP BY`/`ORDER BY` temp-file builds, and reasoned that IRIS has no distinct join marker (joins share the temp-file vocabulary). A standalone correlated/`IN`-subquery plan (e.g. `WHERE x IN (SELECT …)`) was NOT separately captured, so the "same vocabulary generalizes to subqueries" claim is inferred, not reference-captured. Low risk — subqueries almost certainly flatten to the same temp-file/module phrasing — but per Rule #36 it should be observed, not assumed. **Suggested resolution:** in Story 28.2's fixture set, capture one subquery plan live and confirm its markers parse under the pinned §2-finding-1 vocabulary (add it as a reference-captured fixture); if it introduces a new marker, amend spec §2/§4. Source: Acceptance-auditor forward note against AC 28.0.1. [`28-0-advisor-probe-matrix.md` Probe Findings §4; spec §2 finding 1]

## Deferred from: code review of 28-1-advise-data-endpoint (2026-07-11)

Opus 3-layer adversarial review (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor) + direct lead live-verification of Story 28.1 (`/dev/sql/advise-data` — the first ObjectScript handler for `iris_sql_analyze`). Story 28.1 closed `done` — **0 HIGH; 1 patch applied inline (doc-only test-count 12→13 reconciliation in the Dev Agent Record — QA had added the 13th test, `TestHttpBadNamespaceCleanErrorAndRecovery`); 5 dismissed; 4 deferred (below, 1 MED + 3 LOW).** All ACs verified: response shape (`plan`/`tables`/`indexes` grouped-per-table with verbatim `Properties`), Rule #7 single-render (Blind Hunter explicitly confirmed correct on every path), SanitizeError, no caret-globals, `query` validated before namespace switch, explicit save/restore (never `New $NAMESPACE`), bootstrap dual-roster (Rule #39, 27→28), `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` `1e2008753853`→`6422caf6ec31` (idempotent, md5-stable), frozen governance baseline `1e62c5ad5bf7` untouched (`gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0, no new key). Live: `iris_execute_tests` **13/13 total:13** (Rule #35 genuine), `bootstrap.test.ts` 44/44. **No patch touched `SqlAdvisor.cls`** — on-disk `.cls` and embedded `bootstrap-classes.ts` stay in sync; no redeploy / `gen:bootstrap` re-run needed. All 4 deferrals are Epic-28-own (below Rule #37's ≥3 threshold).

**DEFERRED (Epic-28-own; robustness / forward hazards; graceful behavior today):**
- **[CR 28.1-1 / MED — `ResolveClassForTable` bare `Catch` masks a genuine dictionary-query failure as "table not found"]** `ResolveClassForTable` (`src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/SqlAdvisor.cls`) wraps the `%Dictionary.CompiledClass` lookup in `Try { ... } Catch { Set tClassName = "" }`, conflating (a) the intended "no compiled class maps to this schema/table" with (b) a genuine failure (wrong/renamed column, permissions, transient error) — both become `className=""`. Inconsistent with the sibling `IndexRowsForClass`, which treats an identical `%SQLCODE<0` dictionary failure as a hard `%Status` error. If the dictionary column names were ever wrong for an IRIS version, EVERY table would resolve to `""` → empty `indexes` → the Story 28.2 heuristic engine would recommend creating indexes that already exist, under an HTTP-200 "success" envelope. **Not a shipping defect on the pinned IRIS 2026.1 — live-verified the resolve works (`Ens_Config`/`Item` → `Ens.Config.Item`) and the raw query returns correct rows.** **Suggested resolution:** distinguish `%SQLCODE<0` (hard error) from no-match; either change `ResolveClassForTable` to return a `%Status` + output className (ripples into `BuildAdviceData`'s loop + new tests) or add an explicit `%SQLCODE<0` check that surfaces the error. Route to a hardening pass or Story 28.2/28.3. Source: Blind Hunter #1. [`src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/SqlAdvisor.cls` — `ResolveClassForTable`]
- **[CR 28.1-2 / LOW-MED — `ExtractTables` returns zero tables for a bitmap/bitslice-only plan]** `ExtractTables` recognizes only `"Read master map "` and `"Read index map "` marker lines. A plan whose table access uses only a bitmap/bitslice path with no master/index-map line (e.g. a low-cardinality-bitmap aggregate `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM T WHERE bitmapCol = 'x'`) yields `tables:[]`/`indexes:[]` for a query that genuinely reads a table — distinct from the documented "unresolvable token → empty className" case (here the table is entirely absent, not listed-with-empty-className). Forward hazard for Story 28.2's `missing-index` completeness (the heuristic can't fire on a table the endpoint never enumerated). **Graceful today** — the verbatim `plan` is still returned, so 28.2 has the raw material. **Suggested resolution:** in Story 28.2, capture a bitmap/bitslice plan LIVE (Rule #36 — never hand-reason plan vocab) and either extend `ExtractTables`' marker set or have 28.2 cross-check the plan's table tokens against the returned index-dictionary universe. Pairs with CR 28.0-1 (temp-file token conflation). Source: Edge Case Hunter #2 + Blind Hunter #2. [`src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/SqlAdvisor.cls` — `ExtractTables`]
- **[CR 28.1-3 / LOW — `ExtractTables` mis-splits/never-resolves delimited/quoted/schema-less plan tokens]** `ExtractTables` assumes every map token is `Schema.Table.IndexOrIDKEY` (≥3 dot pieces, `$Piece(...,".",1)`=schema, `$Piece(...,".",2)`=table) and strips a parenthetical alias but not surrounding double-quotes. A delimited identifier that embeds a dot (`"My.Schema"."Tbl"`), a quoted `"My Schema"."My Table".IDKEY`, or a 2-part schema-less token (`Item.IDKEY`) mis-splits → wrong schema/table → `ResolveClassForTable` returns `""` → table listed with empty `className`, no indexes. **Graceful (no crash), narrow/version-dependent — no live evidence IRIS 2026.1 emits these for normal persistent-class tables** (the live probe showed the standard 3-part `Ens_Config.Item.IDKEY`). **Suggested resolution:** in a hardening pass, strip surrounding quotes and, for delimited identifiers, split on the LAST dot before the index/IDKEY suffix using the known suffix vocabulary; add reference-captured fixtures if such plans are observed. Source: Edge Case Hunter #3/#4 + Blind Hunter #2. [`src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/SqlAdvisor.cls` — `ExtractTables`]
- **[CR 28.1-4 / LOW — whitespace-only `namespace` drives a switch failure instead of defaulting to current]** In `AdviseData`, `tQuery` is `$ZStrip`-ped but `tNamespace` is not, so a body `{"query":"...","namespace":"   "}` passes `If tNamespace '= ""` → `SwitchNamespace("   ")` → `Set $NAMESPACE="   "` throws → a namespace-error envelope for what is effectively an omitted namespace. **No crash (clean error), and it matches the EnvSync peer's identical behavior.** **Suggested resolution:** `Set tNamespace = $ZStrip(tNamespace, "<>W")` before the `If tNamespace '= ""` check — apply to EnvSync.cls AND SqlAdvisor.cls together in the same hardening pass to keep the sibling handlers consistent. Source: Edge Case Hunter #5. [`src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/SqlAdvisor.cls` — `AdviseData`; `src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/EnvSync.cls` — `DocHashes` (same pattern)]
