Story 28.2 closed `done` — **0 HIGH; 4 patches applied inline** (unused-index master/IDKEY + system-schema false-positive suppression [F1/F4, the dev-flagged known limitation — PATCHED not deferred, two hunters rated it MEDIUM and the "drop your primary key" recommendation is actively harmful]; per-group `rows`/null-group/null-row throw guards making AC 28.2.3 "never throw" hold on malformed `indexes` shapes [F2, live-confirmed the pre-fix throw]; single `findPredicates` call [F6]; **+ 2 new reference-captured fixtures/tests** — the LIKE-only predicate path [QA gap b] and the Fixture-5 aggregate finding-set lock [F9]). Rule #36 spot-verified live: Fixtures 4 and 5 re-captured against the live `/dev/sql/advise-data` endpoint match byte-for-byte, including both documented variances (`.Master` suffix, bare-column predicate). Frozen governance baseline `1e62c5ad5bf7` untouched (`gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0); `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` unchanged `6422caf6ec31` (`AdvisorFixture` fixture-only, absent from both bootstrap rosters); `sqlAnalyze.ts` four existing actions byte-for-byte untouched (Rule #19). `pnpm turbo run test` 13/13; dev suite 549 green (sqlAdvisor 39→46 tests). All 4 deferrals below are Epic-28-own (below Rule #37's ≥3 threshold).

**DEFERRED (Epic-28-own; latent limitations / coverage gaps; graceful behavior today):**
- **[CR 28.2-1 / MED — multi-table JOIN: plan-global predicate attribution + duplicate self-join findings]** `predicateExists` and `equalityRangePredicates` are computed once over the WHOLE plan (`sqlAdvisor.ts` ~`analyzeAdviceData`) and then applied inside the `for (const scan of fullScans)` loop to EVERY master-map scan. On a multi-table join where tables A and B are both full-scanned, (a) `full-scan` fires for a predicate-less table because ANY predicate exists in the plan, and (b) `missing-index` can attribute table A's predicate column to table B and emit `CREATE INDEX IdxX ON B (x)` for a column that may not exist on B. Additionally, a self-join that scans the same table under two aliases yields duplicate `full-scan`/`missing-index` findings (which would also break a `toEqual(findingTypes(...))` aggregate). **Not a shipping defect for the single-table queries the advisor targets today — all 8 fixtures are single-table, and the Rule #50 correlation KEY (schema+table) is item-identity-clean; the gap is the predicate→scan ASSOCIATION, which is not table-scoped.** **Suggested resolution:** in Story 28.3's live smokes, reference-capture a real 2-table join plan (Rule #36 — never hand-reason join plan text), then scope predicate collection per master-map module/scan (associate each `Test the … condition on` line with the scan whose module it falls under) and de-dupe fullScans by (schema,table,alias). Source: Blind Hunter #3 + Edge Case Hunter (multi-table) + Acceptance Auditor (plan-global note). [`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/sqlAdvisor.ts` — full-scan/missing-index loop] **Re-affirmed at the Story 28.3 review (2026-07-11):** the Edge Case Hunter re-flagged this and noted Story 28.3's `workload` mode advises real recent statements from `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.STATEMENTS`, which are join-heavy in any real app — so the engine will routinely hit multi-table plans, ELEVATING real-world exposure vs. the single-statement `query`-mode framing. Do NOT downgrade below MED. The 28.3 lead smoke §4 exercises a real disposable 2-table join live (capture per Rule #36 if a cross-attribution bug repros; else close verified-no-repro). Remains a Story-28.2-module deferral (out of scope for 28.3's action-wiring change).
- **[CR 28.2-2 / LOW — `missing-index` confidence is plan-order-dependent for a column carrying both `=` and a range op]** `isSingleColumnEquality = uniqueMissingCols.length === 1 && missingPredicates[0]?.op === "="` inspects only the FIRST missing predicate's operator. For `WHERE col = ? AND col > ?` on one unindexed column, the confidence is `high` or `medium` purely by which predicate the regex encountered first in the plan text — non-deterministic-feeling for logically equivalent queries. **No fixture exercises a mixed-op single column; behavior is always a defensible high/medium, never wrong-typed.** **Suggested resolution:** make it deterministic per spec intent — e.g. `high` iff exactly one unique missing column AND every predicate on it is `=` (else `medium`). Cheap, but defer to avoid over-touching without a live mixed-op fixture. Source: Blind Hunter #5 + Edge Case Hunter. [`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/sqlAdvisor.ts` — `isSingleColumnEquality`]
- **[CR 28.2-3 / LOW — `isSystemSchema`'s `INFORMATION_SCHEMA` branch is untested at the missing-index suppression point]** The `%*` branch is proven live (Fixture 5, `%Dictionary.CompiledClass`), but the `INFORMATION_SCHEMA` equality branch reaching the `missing-index` suppression is untested. **Live-investigated during review (Rule #36, not deferred blindly):** a full-scan on `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES` (`WHERE TABLE_TYPE = ?`) emits `Read master map INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES.Master, looping on SchemaExact and TableExact.` — the looping clause is NOT `looping on …ID.`, so `FULL_SCAN_RE` does not match it (see CR 28.2-4), meaning no full-scan/missing-index is reached on this instance's INFORMATION_SCHEMA tables at all. A matching full-scan plan could not be cheaply captured; the branch is a one-line equality sharing the exact code path as the proven `%*` branch. **Suggested resolution:** if a future live capture produces a full-scan (matching `FULL_SCAN_RE`) on an `INFORMATION_SCHEMA` table, add it as a reference-captured fixture asserting no `missing-index`. Do NOT fabricate the plan text. Source: QA-flagged residual coverage gap (a). [`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/sqlAdvisor.ts` — `isSystemSchema`]
- **[CR 28.2-4 / LOW — `FULL_SCAN_RE` under-detects master-map scans that loop on a composite/non-`ID` idkey]** `FULL_SCAN_RE` requires the master-map read to end `, looping on [\w.]*ID\.`. A table whose IDKEY is a composite string key loops on its key columns instead — e.g. `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES` emits `Read master map INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES.Master, looping on SchemaExact and TableExact.` (captured live during this review) — so its genuine full scan is NOT detected (a false negative, never a harmful false positive). Consistent with the spec §4 pinned marker `looping on ID.`. **Suggested resolution:** in a hardening pass (or Story 28.3 with live captures across composite-key tables), widen the looping clause to tolerate `looping on <col> [and <col>]…` while keeping the `Read master map` anchor and guarding against over-matching index-map reads. Only with reference-captured evidence (Rule #36). Source: reviewer live discovery while investigating CR 28.2-3. [`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/sqlAdvisor.ts` — `FULL_SCAN_RE`]

- **[CR 28.3-2 / LOW — workload total-outage where every per-statement call is an IrisApiError still renders a benign "no findings"]** After this review's fix (a non-IrisApiError per-statement failure now propagates), the residual is the all-`IrisApiError` case: if the `/dev/sql/advise-data` route is mis-deployed or 500s WITH an IRIS error envelope for every recent statement, each per-statement advise call is an `IrisApiError` → all skipped → the aggregate renders "No performance findings (mode: workload, analyzed: 0, skipped: N)" with `isError: undefined` rather than an error. The `skipped: N` / `analyzed: 0` counts ARE surfaced in both text and `structuredContent`, so it is not fully silent, but the headline reads as "no problems." **Suggested resolution:** track a per-statement `errorCount` distinct from blank-`Statement` client-side skips and surface `isError: true` (with a clear "the advisor could not analyze any of the N statements" message) when `rows.length > 0 && analyzedCount === 0 && errorCount > 0`. Deferred rather than fixed now to avoid a new contract branch in the closing story; the common single-statement `query` path and unexpected-error propagation are already correct. Source: Blind Hunter #1 (residual after the applied narrow-catch fix). [`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/sqlAnalyze.ts` — `handleAdvise` workload aggregation loop]

**DEFERRED (Epic-29-own; latent fidelity gap, no shipped trigger today):**
- **[CR 29.1-1 / LOW — `deriveAuditAction` reads pre-Zod `rawArgs.action`, so a future defaulted-action enum would log `action:null` while governance keys on the defaulted value]** `deriveAuditAction` (`server-base.ts:~1451`) reads `rawArgs.action` (the caller's un-validated args) and returns it only when it is a member of the tool's unwrapped `action` enum options. `computeGovernanceKey` (`:~965-985`), by contrast, reads `validatedArgs.action` (POST-Zod, defaults applied). For every SHIPPED tool this is equivalent — all action fields are required `z.enum([...]).describe(...)` (verified: no shipped tool uses `.default()`/`.optional()`/`.nullable()` on `action`), and `unwrapActionOptions` peels `.describe()` fine. But if a FUTURE tool declares `action: z.enum([...]).default("x")` and a caller OMITS `action`, the governance gate would key on `tool:x` (Zod fills the default) while the audit entry would log `action: null` (`rawArgs.action` is `undefined`) — an audit/governance divergence exactly of the shape AC 29.1.2 was written to prevent for stray keys, but in the under-reporting direction. The story's own Dev Notes considered pre-Zod vs post-Zod only for renaming, not defaults. **Suggested resolution:** thread the validated (server-stripped, default-applied) args used by `computeGovernanceKey` through to `recordAuditEntry` and derive `action` from those, single-sourcing the value the gate keyed on; OR add a registration-time assertion/test that pins the "no shipped tool defaults its `action` field" assumption so the divergence can't silently ship. Latent only — no current trigger. Source: Blind/Edge forward-review of the reused `unwrapActionOptions` surface (Rule #47 lens). [`packages/shared/src/server-base.ts` — `deriveAuditAction` vs `computeGovernanceKey`]

- **CR 30.2-1 (Edge Case Hunter, MEDIUM)** — `toolVisibility` visible/hidden COUNTS were computed once at construction and NOT refreshed by `addTools`/`removeTools`, so `iris_server_profiles`'s reported counts drifted from the real `tools/list` the instant the dynamic API changed the tool set (advisory-only; no governance bypass; no production caller today — latent). **RESOLVED**: added a private `recomputeToolVisibilityCounts()` (live `this.tools.size` for visible, tracked `hiddenToolNames.size` for hidden — identical to the construction formula at t=0) wired into BOTH dynamic paths, matching the governance-state sync those methods already perform; `addTools` now tracks a dynamically-added-but-hidden tool in `hiddenToolNames`, `removeTools` drops a removed name from it. +2 regression tests in `tool-visibility-surfacing.test.ts` (add-visible bumps `visibleTools` then remove restores; add-config-hidden bumps `hiddenTools` not `visibleTools`, name still never leaks). Full shared suite 825/825 green.
- **CR 30.2-2 (Blind Hunter, LOW)** — the "counts correctness" test in `tool-visibility-non-drift.test.ts` asserted only `visibleTools + hiddenTools === pkg.tools.length + 1`, algebraically tautological (guaranteed by the constructor: `visibleCount = visiblePackageToolCount + 1`, `hiddenCount = tools.length - visiblePackageToolCount`) — it could not detect a wrong visible/hidden SPLIT. **PATCHED**: now cross-checks `visibleTools` against the REAL advertised `tools/list` wire length (independent SDK-registry code path — Rule #36 reality oracle) AND `hiddenTools` against the package's own roster `exclude.length`, so a mis-split fails; the tautological sum is kept only as a documented secondary invariant.

| # | Finding | Location | Severity | Suggested resolution |
|---|---------|----------|----------|----------------------|
| 30-2-1 | The "no hidden name leaks" test guards use substring `expect(JSON.stringify(result)).not.toContain(hiddenName)`. Production filter (`visibleGovernedKeys`, exact `Set.has` on the pre-first-colon component) is correct; the TEST guard would false-FAIL if a future hidden tool name were ever a substring of a visible tool's name/key (e.g. a hidden `iris_task` beside a visible `iris_task_run`). Current real fixtures have no such collision, so it is inert today. | `packages/iris-mcp-all/src/__tests__/tool-visibility-non-drift.test.ts` (leak asserts); `packages/shared/src/__tests__/tool-visibility-surfacing.test.ts` (leak asserts) | LOW | Test-robustness only (no runtime defect). Tighten the leak assertions to token/field-precise checks (e.g. assert the hidden name is not a whole tool-name in `tools/list` and not a key/tool-component in the governance policy map) rather than a raw substring scan. Fold into a future Epic-30 test-hardening pass. |

## Epic 31 retro-review gate (2026-07-25)

The `/epic-cycle 31` kickoff retro-review gate read the Epic 30 retrospective (`epic-30-retro-2026-07-22.md`) and this ledger. Carried-open items entering the gate:

- **Epic-30-own batch (5 LOW):** 30-0-1..4 (Story 30.0 visibility-engine review) + 30-2-1 (Story 30.2 surfacing review) — **re-deferral count entering: 1** (first carry; Story 30.3 deferred nothing). No pre-Epic-30 item remains open (the Story 29.3 Rule #37 burn-down terminally disposed all 41 carried items).

**Decision: 1 CLOSED-WITH-EVIDENCE, 4 RE-DEFERRED.** Rationale:

- **Epic 31 is a FEATURE epic** — Server Manager Connection Integration (FR139): settings discovery + profile mapping, a credential chain, an `iris-mcp-credentials` CLI, registry integration, and the `iris-mcp-launcher` broker extension. Its surface is **disjoint** from every carried item's file (`packages/shared/src/tool-visibility.ts`, `server-base.ts`, `tool-visibility-backcompat.test.ts`, `tool-visibility-non-drift.test.ts`, `tool-visibility-surfacing.test.ts`) — confirmed at kickoff. Epic 31 adds NO tool and NO governance key, so the aggregate Rule #19 back-compat property the 30-0-3 item concerns is not placed at new risk.
- The 4 re-deferred items move to **re-deferral count 2 — below Rule #37's ≥3-consecutive threshold.** A 3rd re-defer at Epic 32 would trip it, requiring a dedicated burn-down story with terminal disposition in the Epic 32 plan.
- **No colliding Story X.0** — the 31.0 slot is the feature story `31-0-settings-discovery` (a shared-package foundation story), mirroring the Epics 19/20/21/23/24/27/28/30 feature-epic pattern. No cleanup story created (0 items included).

| # | Item | Severity | Disposition |
|---|------|----------|-------------|
| 30-0-1 | `addTools()` dynamic-add bypasses named-preset roster curation | LOW | **RE-DEFERRED** (count 2) — zero production impact; `addTools` still has no production caller (tests only), and `assertPresetCoverage` structurally forbids a roster naming a non-registered tool. Epic 31 does not touch `tool-visibility.ts`. |
| 30-0-2 | A named preset against a package with UNWIRED rosters is a silent no-op | LOW | **CLOSED WITH EVIDENCE** — the finding was explicitly scoped to "the 30.0→30.1 window". That window is closed: all 5 server packages now ship `src/tools/presets.ts` and wire `toolPresets` into `McpServerBase` via their `index.ts` (verified mechanically at this gate). The silent-no-op condition is unreachable for every shipped package. |
| 30-0-3 | AC 30.0.4 back-compat capstone snapshot transcribed from spec §2.5, not source-derived | LOW | **RE-DEFERRED** (count 2) — the aggregate Rule #19 property stays doubly gated (each leaf `index.test.ts` pins its real `tools[]` through the same filter under empty env). Epic 31 adds no tool, so the "package adds/renames a tool" scenario the item guards against cannot arise this epic. |
| 30-0-4 | Spec §2.2 "config hiding EVERY package tool ⇒ startup warning" not implemented | LOW | **RE-DEFERRED** (count 2) — diagnostic nicety, no correctness impact; not enumerated in any Epic 30 AC. |
| 30-2-1 | Hidden-name leak assertions use substring matching; would false-FAIL on a future substring-colliding tool name | LOW | **RE-DEFERRED** (count 2) — test-robustness only, inert today (no collision in real fixtures); the production filter (`visibleGovernedKeys`, exact `Set.has`) is correct. Epic 31 adds no tool name, so no new collision can be introduced. |

**Patched in-story (NOT carried) — the three HIGHs, for context on why the carried set is small:** (1) a per-entry `mergeProfile` validation throw escaped to `McpServerBase.start()`, so one stale entry in a VS Code settings file the suite does not own crashed all five servers — now contained per-entry exactly like a malformed file; (2) an entry omitting `webServer.host` inherited the LOCAL default host, aiming a remote-named profile and its inline password at localhost — `host` is now mandatory; (3) a UTF-8 BOM made a valid settings file look malformed and be discarded whole (verified live: `jsonc-parser` returns the correct object *and* an `InvalidSymbol` at offset 0) — BOM now stripped. Plus 10 MEDIUM (allow-list vs `required` counting order, case-sensitive `scheme`, Rule #43 README docs, Rule 5/#42 AC 31.0.3 amendment, the Story 31.1 return-contract seam, `process.cwd()` inside `discoverSettingsFiles`, a win32-host-only QA test, empty server names, EACCES/EISDIR swallow, zero-diagnostics discovery) and 6 LOW.

| # | Finding | Location | Severity | Deferral rationale | Suggested resolution |
|---|---------|----------|----------|--------------------|----------------------|
| 31-0-1 | An unresolved (password-less) higher-precedence entry is added to `seenNames` before the credential-completeness check, so it permanently shadows a *resolvable* same-named entry in a lower-precedence file — the name is absent from the registry entirely, with only the generic "N server profile(s) skipped" as a clue. | `packages/shared/src/server-manager-source.ts` `resolveServerManagerProfiles` (`seenNames`) | LOW (filed MEDIUM; re-severitied — the case needs two files where exactly one carries a *deprecated legacy inline* password, and Story 31.1's chain resolves per-name, making the shadow correct rather than lossy) | **Story 31.1 owns credential resolution** and structurally dissolves this case: once passwords resolve from keychain/helper, the higher-precedence entry is resolvable and winning is the intended precedence. Fixing it here would encode a shadowing rule 31.1 immediately invalidates. | In 31.1, decide the rule once with the chain in hand: either add to `seenNames` only on successful resolution, or track shadowed candidates so a lower-precedence resolvable definition can fill an unresolved slot. Add a two-file fixture test. |
| 31-0-2 | `smBase` clears only `password`; `username` is still inherited from the local default config. (2) Newly-exported `mergeProfile`/`ProfileOverride` widening the public API — verified NOT re-exported from `index.ts`. (3) `buildProfileRegistry`/`loadProfileRegistry` divergence — `buildProfileRegistry` has no runtime consumer but `loadProfileRegistry` and `server-base.ts`'s injected-config test path; roster and resolution both read the same `this.profiles`. (5) `IRIS_SERVER_MANAGER` not trimming/lowercasing — faithfully mirrors `parseGovernancePreset`'s suite-wide fail-fast convention, and the error names the valid set. (6) `Number()` coercion on a file-sourced `port` — AC 31.0.2 mandates reusing `mergeProfile`'s exact validation. (7) No cap on imported profile count — opt-in feature with an `IRIS_SM_SERVERS` allow-list. (8) AC 31.0.4's oracle being partly self-referential — the Acceptance Auditor's own analysis concluded the proof is genuine and mutation-sensitive (`buildProfileRegistry`'s body is byte-unchanged; the pre-existing matrix pins literal values; flipping the unset default `off`→`auto` fails it).

Patched to the only form the chain cannot undo: an entry declaring no `username` of its own is **not imported at all**, always with a warning naming the file and the fix. The keychain account shape is settled as `<serverName>` (not the spec's floated `<serverName>/<username>`) and documented in the README. Behavior change vs. Story 31.0 recorded in `.changeset/credential-chain-async-registry.md`. |

Add a fixture proving the chosen rule. |
| 31-1-3 | No aggregate startup budget for the credential helper: `spawnSync` is synchronous and profiles are completed sequentially, so a configured-but-unreachable helper blocks the event loop for up to 10s × (password-less profiles) inside `McpServerBase.start()` — nothing else in the process can run, stdout included. Measured: 3 profiles × 1.5s ⇒ 4744ms wall, **0** event-loop ticks. | `packages/shared/src/credential-chain.ts` `runCredentialHelperDefault` / `resolveServerManagerCredentials` | LOW | The per-link 10s timeout is **exactly what AC 31.1.1 specifies**; an aggregate budget, parallelism, or a profile cap is new scope beyond every AC. The cost is now documented in the `IRIS_CREDENTIAL_HELPER` README row and the module doc comment, with `IRIS_SM_SERVERS` named as the mitigation, and the helper link only runs when an operator has opted into it. | Story 31.2's `iris-mcp-credentials test` gives operators an interactive way to validate a helper before it reaches startup. If a real complaint appears, add an overall budget (e.g. 30s total, then skip the link for remaining profiles with one summary warn) or move the helper to `execFile` + bounded concurrency. |
| 31-1-4 | AC 31.1.1 says the helper receives the server name as **`argv[1]`**; the implementation appends it as the FINAL argv entry, so it is `argv[1]` only when `IRIS_CREDENTIAL_HELPER` is a single bare token (`op read op://vault/item <name>` puts it at `argv[3]`). The implementation is the safer superset — the name is always its own argv entry and never shell-interpolated, which is the AC's actual intent — but the AC wording is literally inaccurate. | `packages/shared/src/credential-chain.ts` `runCredentialHelperDefault`; `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md` AC 31.1.1 | LOW | Docs-only divergence with no behavioral risk: a single-token helper (the shape the AC assumes) *does* get the name at `argv[1]`, and the README was corrected in-story to describe the real contract ("appended as its final argument", with a wrapper-script recipe using `$1`). Amending an AC mid-epic is a Rule #42 action better batched with Story 31.3's docs rollup, which already revisits these rows. | In Story 31.3's docs rollup, amend AC 31.1.1 in `epics.md` in place — "as its final argv entry (`argv[1]` for a single-token command)" — with a dated parenthetical, matching the precedent set for AC 31.0.3 during the Story 31.0 review. |
| 31-1-5 | The debug line "N server profile(s) have no password yet; the credential chain will attempt resolution" is emitted inside `resolveServerManagerProfiles`, which cannot see `loadProfileRegistry`'s registry-shadow filter — so it counts entries that are discarded one call later and never reach the chain. Cosmetic over-count in a debug diagnostic. | `packages/shared/src/server-manager-source.ts` (`:671-674`) vs `packages/shared/src/profiles.ts` (shadow filter) | LOW | Purely a diagnostic count, and the shadowed names now each emit their own explicit collision warning (patched in-story), so an operator reading the log is not actually misled about which profiles were attempted. Moving the summary line into `loadProfileRegistry` crosses the Rule #52 seam this story deliberately holds. | In Story 31.3, when `loadProfileRegistry` gains the full merge/provenance layer, emit the pending-resolution summary from there (post-filter) and drop the source-module line, or reword it as "…found without a password" with no chain claim. |

**Dismissed (not carried):** (1) AC 31.1.1's "**error** log" for exhaustion vs. the implemented `logger.warn` — `logger.error` is reserved suite-wide for fatal startup failures immediately preceding `process.exit(1)`; a per-profile exclusion that `auto` mode deliberately survives is a warning, the message text is fully AC-compliant, and `logger` defaults to DEBUG so both are emitted. (2) The keychain-unavailable test throwing inside a `vi.mock` factory exercises vitest's own error rather than a native-load error — the `catch` branch and the "OS keychain link unavailable" assertion are genuine, which is what AC 31.1.1 requires. (3) `@napi-rs/keyring` pinned exact `1.3.0` while other deps use `^` — deliberate and correct for a native module with per-platform prebuilt binaries. (4) A password with significant leading/trailing whitespace is trimmed away — AC 31.1.1 mandates "trimmed stdout is the password"; changing it would violate the AC. (5) A server named `__proto__` is silently dropped — verified no prototype pollution (`jsonc-parser` drops the key; `Object.prototype` untouched); already dismissed identically in the Story 31.0 review. (6) `resolveCredential`/`CredentialLinkResult` being newly public API that returns a plaintext secret — intentional and required by Story 31.2's CLI; the type is documented and no serializing surface consumes it (`iris_server_profiles`'s allow-list never reads `password`, proven by test). (7) AC 31.1.5's "excluded" half not being independently mutation-sensitive — the `required`-throws half carries the sensitivity and the AC as a whole was mutation-verified red by the Acceptance Auditor.

**Disposition tally:** 5 deferred · 15 patched-in-story · 7 dismissed · 0 decision-needed. (27 findings after dedupe across three layers; mechanically recounted from the tables above, per Rule #51.)

## Deferred from: code review of 31-2-credentials-cli (2026-07-25)

Epic-31-own findings from the Story 31.2 3-layer adversarial review (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor) plus independent live verification. **3 HIGH / 15 MEDIUM / 9 LOW patched in-story · 6 deferred (all LOW) · 7 dismissed · 0 decision-needed.** All three HIGHs were reproduced live before being actioned; every patch is mutation-verified. Per Rule #37 these are **Epic-31-own new items at re-deferral count 1** (first carry). The inherited Epic-30-own batch (30-0-1, 30-0-3, 30-0-4, 30-2-1 — count 2) is untouched by this story; `31-0-3`/`31-0-4`/`31-0-5` and `31-1-1`…`31-1-5` remain open for Story 31.3 at count 1, unchanged (this story did not re-examine them — none was in its path, as its Dev Notes predicted). Verified at review (live): `pnpm turbo run build test lint type-check` 25/25 green (`@iris-mcp/shared` 59 files / **1067** tests, up from 977 at Story 31.1 close), `pnpm gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0 (frozen `1e62c5ad5bf7` / 141 / 201 / 60 unchanged), Rule #31 held (no tool count moved — this story adds no MCP tool), `bootstrap-classes.ts` / `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` / `src/ExecuteMCPv2/**` untouched, Story 31.0's Rule #19 gate green AND re-proven mutation-sensitive.

**Patched in-story (NOT carried) — the three HIGHs, for context on why the carried set is small:** (1) `set` **exited 0 having stored nothing** against a piped or closed stdin — the prompt registered only a `"data"` listener and no `"end"`, so its promise never settled, a pending promise does not hold the event loop open, and `process.exitCode` was never assigned (reproduced live: `outcome="HANG"`). (2) The prompt's `switch` compared the WHOLE `data` chunk against each terminator, so **a pasted password was stored with an embedded newline** — `set` printed success and every later authentication failed against a value unreadable from anywhere. Both fixed by code-point iteration + `"end"`/`"error"` handling; the prompt is now bound to the injected stdin/stderr seams and carries 9 tests where it previously had zero (every `set` test injected `promptPassword` and bypassed it entirely). (3) `test --connect` **printed a password in cleartext**: the catch redacted only against `profile.password`, `undefined` on exactly the branch that parses `IRIS_PROFILES`, and V8 embeds a ~20-character source excerpt in a JSON `SyntaxError` (verified live on Node v24). Plus 15 MEDIUM (no withhold branch for sub-4-character secrets; `--stdin` echoing on a TTY; no server-name validation — `test ""` silently probed the reserved `default` profile and a newline-bearing name broke line-oriented `list` consumers; `cmdList`'s unguarded `.map()/.sort()`; `list --json` emitting no JSON on failure; `test --json` dropping the remediation; unguarded `resolveCredentialFn`; the wrong `--connect` remediation; a UTF-8 BOM corrupting a `--stdin` password; stray NUL bytes making a test file a git BINARY blob; a service-blind Integration-AC proof with the reader's constant pinned nowhere; a packaging test that never ran the bin; the README-vs-client-config `npx` contradiction; the README's false "never read a password" claim; and the Rule #51 tally/File List drift) and 9 LOW.

| # | Finding | Location | Severity | Deferral rationale | Suggested resolution |
|---|---------|----------|----------|--------------------|----------------------|
| 31-2-1 | `test --connect` validates the profile REGISTRY's password, not the credential the chain just resolved. A stale `IRIS_PASSWORD` can resolve link 1 while the registry maps a different (keychain) password, so `resolved via env link` + `connect OK` can both print while the reported credential was never exercised — and the converse can report `connect FAILED` for a good keychain password. | `packages/shared/src/cli/credentials.ts` `cmdTest` (`--connect` branch) | LOW (filed MEDIUM; re-severitied — the divergence needs env and keychain to hold DIFFERENT passwords for the same name, and the two outcomes are reported as separate fields rather than conflated) | **AC 31.2.1 mandates this shape verbatim**: "optional `--connect` performs an Atelier HEAD against **the mapped profile**". Making `--connect` authenticate with `result.password` instead would exercise a credential no server ever uses, and changing it in code review would re-litigate the AC rather than fix a defect. | In Story 31.3 (which owns provenance): surface WHICH password the connect attempt used — e.g. a `connect.credentialSource` field derived from the registry entry — so the two halves are visibly distinct, or amend AC 31.2.1 to require the probe use the chain-resolved credential. Add a fixture where env and keychain differ. |
| 31-2-2 | `redactSecret` matches the exact plaintext substring only. `--connect` authenticates via HTTP Basic, where the credential travels as `Authorization: Basic base64(user:pass)`; an HTTP error that dumped request headers would leak the base64 form, which contains no plaintext substring to match. | `packages/shared/src/cli/credentials.ts` `redactSecret` / `redactKnownSecrets` | LOW (no live reproduction — `IrisConnectionError`/`IrisApiError` do not carry headers today; a hypothetical about a future error shape) | Defense-in-depth beyond every AC, on a leak path that does not exist in the current error types. The realistic vector (a plaintext source excerpt from a JSON parse error) WAS live and is patched, together with the sub-4-character withhold branch. Adding encoding variants with no reproducing case risks over-redacting unrelated text. | When `http-client.ts` next changes its error shape, add the encoded variants as redaction keys (`base64(user:pass)`, `encodeURIComponent(pass)`) in `redactKnownSecrets`, driven by a test whose thrown error carries a real `Authorization` header. |
| 31-2-3 | `test --connect` reports `connect: {attempted: false, ok: false}` when the credential was unresolved, and `attempted: true` when `resolveProfile` failed before any HTTP call. A machine consumer reading `connect.ok === false` cannot tell "the probe failed" from "the probe never ran". | `packages/shared/src/cli/credentials.ts` `cmdTest` (`ConnectOutcome`) | LOW | Shape-only: `attempted` and `error` together are unambiguous when read as a pair, the human branch now states `connect SKIPPED` explicitly (patched in-story), and `connect` is a JSON contract three tests already pin — making `ok` nullable is a breaking output change for a field that has no consumer yet. | Make `ok` nullable (`boolean \| null`, `null` when `attempted` is false) and set `attempted` from the actual stage reached, in the same change that next revises the `--json` shape. Update the three payload assertions together. |
| 31-2-4 | `set` prints the same `Stored a password for "<name>"` whether it created a new entry or silently replaced an existing one. For a tool whose worst failure mode is "the wrong password is stored", create-vs-replace is meaningful information. | `packages/shared/src/cli/credentials.ts` `cmdSet` | LOW | Purely an output nicety outside every AC. Distinguishing the two requires a `getPassword` read before the write, pulling an existing secret into memory for a cosmetic message — a trade this story's secret-discipline posture should not make casually. | Expose an `exists(account): boolean` on `KeyringPort` that does not return the value, and report "Replaced the existing password for …". |
| 31-2-5 | `readStdinPassword` accumulates the whole stream with no size cap, so a misdirected pipe (`cat bigfile \| … set x --stdin`) is an unbounded allocation rather than a clean error. | `packages/shared/src/cli/credentials.ts` `readStdinPassword` | LOW | Self-inflicted, immediately visible, and never silent-wrong: the operator watches the process consume memory on a command they typed. No AC covers input sizing, and any cap is an arbitrary number that could reject a legitimate long passphrase. | Cap at a generous bound (e.g. 64 KiB) and fail with exit 2 naming the cap and the likely cause (a file piped in by mistake). Add a fixture feeding a stream past the cap. |
| 31-2-6 | `--stdin` decodes bytes as UTF-8 unconditionally. A UTF-8 BOM is now stripped (patched in-story), but a UTF-16LE file — what PowerShell's `Out-File` produces on some hosts — is stored NUL-interleaved (`h`, U+0000, `u`, U+0000, …), and its trailing newline is not trimmed because the buffer ends with U+0000. `set` reports success; the failure surfaces later as an unexplained IRIS authentication failure. | `packages/shared/src/cli/credentials.ts` `readStdinPassword` | LOW (filed MEDIUM; re-severitied — the BOM case, by far the most common on this platform, is fixed, and the remainder needs a deliberately UTF-16-encoded input file) | Detecting and transcoding UTF-16 is encoding-guessing, which can misfire on a legitimate password containing the bytes a heuristic keys off. The safe half (BOM stripping) shipped; the rest needs a decision on whether to transcode or to REJECT — a product call, not a review fix. | Reject rather than guess: if the decoded password contains a U+0000, fail with exit 2 naming UTF-16 as the likely cause and pointing at `-Encoding utf8` / `printf`. Cheaper and safer than transcoding. Add fixtures for UTF-16LE with and without a BOM. |

**Dismissed (not carried):** (1) "The `bin` may be missing from the published tarball because of the `files` allow-list" — disproven live: `npm pack --dry-run` lists `dist/cli/credentials-cli.js` (the sibling half of that finding, that the packaging test never EXECUTED the bin, was real and is patched with a `spawnSync` assertion). (2) "Nothing pins the WRITER's keychain service" — disproven: `credentials-cli-real-keyring.test.ts` already recorded `Entry` constructor arguments through the production `loadRealKeyring()`; the READER half of that finding was real and is patched with a round-trip test. (3) Weak/self-fulfilling secret assertions — each cited test also carries a strong assertion in the same body (exact `toBe` output, exact JSON `toEqual`), so further hardening adds noise without adding sensitivity. (4) The global `-h`/`--help` scan making `delete "$NAME"` with `NAME=--help` a silent no-op — help short-circuits before any side effect and exits 0 having changed nothing, which is conventional CLI behavior; the genuinely dangerous name shapes are now rejected by server-name validation. (5) Duplicate/contradictory flags silently accepted (`list --json --json`) — `Set` semantics, conventional, harmless. (6) `set` rejecting a whitespace-only password while storing an untrimmed one — intentional and AC-mandated ("trimmed of a single trailing newline"), and now documented in the README. (7) "AC 31.2.3's live keychain smoke is unrun" — by design: the story's Dev Notes assign it to the lead AFTER code review; the statically verifiable half (bin runs from `dist`, shebang survives, path resolves, tarball contains it) is verified and now asserted in the suite.

## Deferred from: code review of 31-3-registry-integration (2026-07-25)

Epic-31-own findings from the Story 31.3 3-layer adversarial review (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor) plus independent reviewer verification. **This is the Epic 31 capstone story.** **1 spec contradiction (lead-found, treated as HIGH) / 5 MEDIUM / 4 LOW patched in-story · 9 deferred (2 MEDIUM, 7 LOW) · 6 dismissed · 0 decision-needed.** Per Rule #37 these are **Epic-31-own new items at re-deferral count 1** (first carry). The inherited Epic-30-own batch (30-0-1, 30-0-3, 30-0-4, 30-2-1 — count 2) is untouched by this story: a re-defer at Epic 32 would be its **3rd consecutive** and forces a burn-down story.

Verified live at review: `pnpm turbo run build test lint type-check` 25/25 green (`@iris-mcp/shared` 59 files / **1082** tests, up from 1067 at Story 31.2's close — +15 net new, mechanically recounted per Rule #51; the story's and sprint-status' hand-authored 1076/+9/+6 claims were all wrong and were corrected in place). `pnpm gen:governance-baseline:check` (the `:check` ONLY, Rule #25) exit 0, frozen `1e62c5ad5bf7` / 141 / 201 / 60 unchanged. Rule #31 held (no tool count moved — the discovery tool and audit entries gained FIELDS, not actions). No `bootstrap-classes.ts` / `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` / `src/ExecuteMCPv2/**` change; this repository's own `.vscode/settings.json` untouched. **Rule #19 gate independently mutation-verified at review** (three mutations, all restored): seeding an extra `sourceFile` on the default profile ⇒ **7** red (5 before this review's strengthening of the two AC 31.0.4 tests, 7 after); drifting `host`/`port`/`password`/`namespace` ⇒ **4** red; flipping the unset-mode default `off`→`auto` ⇒ **8** red, including both on-disk-fixture proofs. The strip removes `name`/`source` and nothing else, and `source === "env"` is asserted immediately before it — it cannot mask connection-field drift.

**ADR conformance (Rule #6): clean.** D5 — `profile.source` is read in exactly TWO non-test places (`server-discovery.ts` roster attribution, `server-base.ts` audit attribution), verified by exhaustive grep; the governance gate is `effective(governanceKey, profile.name, …)`, name-keyed, and runs in `dispatchToolCall` long before audit derivation. No authorization decision reads provenance. E1 — `buildRosterEntry` adds `source`/`sourceFile` by EXPLICIT naming with single-value conditional spread, never `{...profile}`; the spread-forbidding doc comment is preserved and extended; the redaction sweep was re-run over the widened shape with a password-bearing profile at three levels.

**Capstone verdict (AC 31.3.3 / Integration AC 31.3.6, Rule #21): CREDIBLE, and Run B is a genuine control — not a happy-path demo.** Attacked adversarially and it held: Run B's quoted error text matches `profiles.ts`' template byte-for-byte; Run B changed ONLY `IRIS_SERVER_MANAGER` while leaving `IRIS_SM_WORKSPACE` set, so the delta is the switch alone; and Run A's "the OS keychain resolved it" claim is structurally forced rather than asserted — `credential-chain.ts` reaches the `IRIS_PASSWORD` fallback only when the server name IS the reserved default, which `capstoneSm` cannot be, and a failed keychain link would have EXCLUDED the profile, so `iris_server_info` would have errored instead of returning a live Atelier payload. Residual weakness carried as `31-3-8`.

**Patched in-story (NOT carried):**

1. **The lead-found spec contradiction (top item).** AC 31.3.1 mandates `source: "env"` on env profiles, so `loadProfileRegistry`'s output is deliberately no longer byte-identical to pre-feature — yet **AC 31.0.4 still literally read "deep-equals its pre-feature output"** and was NOT amended, while the dev amended AC 31.0.3 and AC 31.1.1 and materially changed what that gate proves. The cycle log shows it was flagged and shipped anyway. **Resolved by amending AC 31.0.4 in place** (Rule 5/#42) to state the property that actually matters and that the tests actually assert — *with `IRIS_SERVER_MANAGER` unset, no Server-Manager data enters the registry, and every profile's CONNECTION fields deep-equal their pre-feature values* — naming `source` as the single permitted additive difference, requiring `source === "env"` to be asserted before the strip, and recording the mutation evidence. **Not** resolved by weakening a test.
2. **Docs contradicted the code they shipped with (raised independently by two layers).** The README and `tool_support.md` said the audit entry's `profileSource` is *"omitted, meaning `env`"* for an `IRIS_*`/`IRIS_PROFILES` profile. It is not: `buildProfileRegistry` sets `source: "env"` on the reserved default and `mergeProfile` defaults to `"env"`, so **every** entry from a stock deployment carries `profileSource: "env"` — the diff's own interceptor test asserts exactly that. Worse, the field IS absent in an undocumented case (an unknown `server` name), so a consumer following the published contract would read "no profile resolved" as "env". Both docs corrected to the real contract, and a **regression test added and mutation-verified** (`audit-interceptor.test.ts`: absent, not `"env"`, on an unknown-profile call; forcing a `?? "env"` fallback turns it red).
3. **A new test would go RED on any non-Windows host** — the QA `sourceFile` test built its expected path with the HOST `path.join` while pinning discovery to `"win32"` (which composes with `path.win32.join`), so the separators disagree off Windows. This is the **identical defect class the Story 31.0 review already caught once**. Fixed to `path.win32.join`.
4. **`sourceFile`'s information disclosure was documented only inside a test comment.** DECISION RECORDED (residual carried as `31-3-4`): **keep the full path, do not redact** — a basename is useless because every candidate file is literally named `settings.json`, so the directory IS the information the field exists to convey, and the disclosure is non-credential. But the docs called it *"never a secret"* / *"secret-free"* and said nothing about the fact that a user-scope hit embeds the **OS account name** and is returned to the connected MCP client (i.e. to the model). Made honest in all four places: the root README (a new bullet naming the exposure and the mitigations), the `IrisProfile.sourceFile` and `ProfileRosterEntry.sourceFile` doc comments, and the QA test comment (now a recorded decision with a standing pin, not an open question).
5. **The AC 31.0.4-labelled tests had become a self-referential oracle.** They compare `loadProfileRegistry` against `buildProfileRegistry`, which the Story 31.0 review dismissed as acceptable *on the explicit premise that `buildProfileRegistry`'s body was byte-unchanged* — a premise **this story invalidated** by adding `source: "env"` to it. Both tests gained an independent pre-feature anchor (`toEqual(loadConfig(env))` on the stripped default, plus `not.toHaveProperty` on `sourceFile`/`pathPrefix` across every profile). Mutation-verified: the oracle-drift mutation went from 5 red to **7** red.
6. **Rule #51 — every test tally in the record was wrong and mutually inconsistent.** Story Completion Notes claimed 1076 / +9 with a breakdown summing to 11; sprint-status claimed 1076 / +6. Mechanical truth: **1082 / +15** (`profiles.test.ts` +9, `server-manager-source.test.ts` +5, `audit.test.ts` +1, `audit-interceptor.test.ts` +1; 16 added `it(` blocks less 1 retitle). Both artifacts corrected in place with the derivation shown.
7. **CHANGELOG accuracy (user-facing).** *"Three new environment variables, all optional and default `off`"* introduced **five** bullets, four of which have no `off` default (`IRIS_SM_WORKSPACE` defaults to the process CWD — the documented untrusted-repo risk, the opposite of "off"). Also corrected: the self-negating *"byte-for-byte today's output **plus** the new field"* sentence, an internal monorepo `dist/` path published as the CLI's identity, and *"Delivered across four stories"* presenting Epic 31 as complete while Story 31.4 sits in `backlog` (now stated as the four **npm-suite** stories, with 31.4 named as a separately-shipped VS Code extension).
8. **AC 31.3.4's literal docs requirement was half-met.** It requires env-var rows *"with default states"* in the per-server READMEs; those stated a default for `IRIS_SERVER_MANAGER` only. All **6** package READMEs (5 servers + `iris-mcp-all`) now state the defaults for `IRIS_SM_SERVERS`, `IRIS_SM_SETTINGS_PATHS`, `IRIS_SM_WORKSPACE` and `IRIS_CREDENTIAL_HELPER` inline, matching the Epic 29 audit-subsection precedent directly above them.
9. **The collision notice prescribed an impossible remedy for the reserved name** (raised independently by two layers): the reserved `default` profile is unconditionally in the registry, so a Server Manager server named `default` collides every time — and the generic advice was "remove the `IRIS_PROFILES` entry", which such an operator may not have. The notice now calls that case out separately with the only remedy that works (rename it), with a regression test that also asserts the note does NOT appear on an ordinary collision.
10. **`profileSource` was typed as bare `string`** while the closed `ProfileSource` union was already exported and used for the roster's `source`. Now typed as the union (type-only import, so no runtime edge is added to the `profiles.ts` ↔ `server-manager-source.ts` cycle the Dev Notes warn about), so the two values every doc surface promises are compiler-enforced.

**Deferred-item dispositions carried into this story (Rule #37 mirror; Rule #48 higher bar applied — each verified in the code, not accepted from the Completion Notes):**

Pinned by a *discriminating* assertion (`toBe(workspace path)` **and** `.not.toBe(user-settings path)`), not a bare presence check. Docs-only; no behavior changed. |

**Dismissed (not carried):** (1) *"The `required` fixture was edited to accommodate the new behavior, hiding a startup-failure widening"* — the edit is legitimate: that test's subject is password-absence, and its fixture accidentally lacked `username`, so it was passing for the wrong reason (skipped by the 31-0-2 guard); declaring `username` isolates it correctly. The widening itself is the INTENDED resolution of `31-1-1`, affects only `required` (never the default `auto`), and is documented in the amended AC 31.0.3. (2) *"`consideredCount` may be pre-allow-list, so check 3's message could over-report"* — disproven by reading the code: `consideredCount` increments AFTER the `IRIS_SM_SERVERS` filter `continue`s, so it is post-allow-list and the message is accurate. (3) *"The governance-parity test is hollow because `effective()` only receives a name string"* — the observation is correct but the conclusion is not: `effective()` being structurally incapable of reading `source` is exactly the property D5 requires, and it is verified far more strongly by exhaustive grep (two read sites, neither in the gate) than any test could be. (4) *"`mergeProfile` emits `pathPrefix: ""` for an explicit empty string"* — reachable only by a direct call to the exported function with `""`; `normalizePathPrefix` never produces it from a settings file, and `baseUrl` is unaffected either way. (5) *"`expect(entry.sourceFile).not.toContain(smProfile.password)` would fail if the password were empty"* — true of `not.toContain("")` in general, but the fixture sets a real secret and the same test carries an exact `toBe(<path>)` assertion, so sensitivity does not rest on it. (6) *"CHANGELOG dated 2026-07-25 while cycle-log lines read 2026-07-26"* — UTC vs. local timestamps, not an inconsistency. Also dismissed: the `# prior last_updated:` / `# prior-prior last_updated:` comment chain in `sprint-status.yaml` reading ambiguously — YAML comments, no parser impact, the blocks belong to different sections, and the file parses clean.

**Disposition tally:** 9 deferred · 10 patched-in-story · 6 dismissed · 0 decision-needed. (25 findings after dedupe across three layers plus reviewer-originated verification; mechanically recounted from the tables above, per Rule #51.)

## Deferred from: code review of 31-4-broker-extension (2026-07-25)

Story 31.4 (Broker Extension MVP, `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/`) — the final story of Epic 31 and a
**standalone VS Code extension deliberately outside the pnpm workspace**. Nothing below touches `packages/**`;
these items live entirely in `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/`, so they do NOT interact with the frozen
governance baseline, tool counts, or `bootstrap-classes.ts`.

Verified live at review: extension `npx tsc --noEmit` clean and `npx vitest run` **107/107 across 10 files**
(dev 52/8 → QA 71/9 → review 107/10, counted mechanically from the runner output per Rule #51); VSIX
repackaged self-contained (15 files, 26.61 KB, `dist/*.js` only, zero npm runtime dependencies). Root workspace
isolation re-confirmed: `pnpm-workspace.yaml` still globs only `packages/*`, zero `packages/**` files modified,
`pnpm turbo run build test lint type-check` **25/25 with the same task count**, `pnpm gen:governance-baseline:check`
(the `:check` ONLY, Rule #25) exit 0 with `1e62c5ad5bf7` / 141 / 201 / 60 unchanged, no tool count moved, this
repository's own `.vscode/settings.json` untouched, and `git add -A -n` stages no `dist/`, `node_modules/` or
`*.vsix`.

**Two HIGH findings were raised INDEPENDENTLY by all three review layers and are PATCHED IN-STORY, not carried:**
the real `vscode.authentication.getSession({createIfNone:true})` **rejects** on user cancellation rather than
resolving `undefined` (verified against the installed `@types/vscode@1.125.0` declaration), and there was no
`try`/`catch` anywhere on the credential path — so AC 31.4.2's "definition unresolved with one clear message,
no error toast storm" was unreachable code and every cancellation test was green against a fake the real API
contradicts (Rule #36 wrong-oracle). Both fixed and mutation-verified (reverting the guard turns 4 tests red
across three files; files restored byte-identical). The reference implementation the story mandated reading
(`iris-table-editor`'s `ServerConnectionManager`) wraps the identical sequence in a `try`/`catch` — that
enclosing catch is precisely what made its `if (!session)` check safe, and it was dropped in the port (Rule
#14/#16). Full patch list in the story's `### Review Findings`.

| # | Finding | Location | Severity | Deferral rationale | Suggested resolution |
|---|---------|----------|----------|--------------------|----------------------|
| 31-4-1 | Concurrent `resolveEnvForLabel` calls are not coalesced. The default settings register 5 packages × N servers, so a cold start with no cached Server Manager session can fire several `getSession({createIfNone:true})` calls for the SAME server in parallel, stacking multiple modal credential prompts — while the README promises "never a repeated prompt". | `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/src/serverDefinitionProvider.ts` `resolveEnvForLabel` | MEDIUM | Requires real in-flight de-duplication design (a `Map<serverName, Promise<CredentialResult>>` keyed per server, with correct eviction on failure so a cancel is not cached as a permanent "no"), which is a behavior change no AC specifies and which no unit test can observe without a concurrency harness — every existing test awaits sequentially. It is also NOT the toast-storm the AC forbids: that was the per-call retry loop, which is fixed and pinned. Contained: the user gets N prompts once, then Server Manager caches the session and the problem disappears. | Add an in-flight promise cache keyed by server name inside `LauncherProvider`, evicted when the promise settles, so parallel definitions covering one server share a single `getSession` round-trip. Pin with a test that launches 5 `resolveEnvForLabel` calls under `Promise.all` and asserts `getSession` was called exactly twice. Confirm the real prompt count during the AC 31.4.4 smoke first — VS Code may already serialize modal auth prompts, which would downgrade this to cosmetic. |
| 31-4-2 | No refresh mechanism: the provider omits the optional `onDidChangeMcpServerDefinitions` event, subscribes to no `workspace.onDidChangeConfiguration`, ignores Server Manager's `onDidChangePassword()`, and passes no `version` to `McpStdioServerDefinition`. Changing `irisMcpLauncher.*`, adding a Server Manager server, or rotating an IRIS password has no effect until a window reload — and after a `combineProfiles` toggle the labels change, so a VS Code-cached definition resolves to "no configuration found for …". | `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/src/extension.ts` (provider registration) | MEDIUM | Feature-shaped rather than defect-shaped: no AC mentions refresh, and the extension is honest about it (its own warnings say "try reloading the window", and the limitation is now documented in the README and CHANGELOG at this review). Implementing it properly means an `EventEmitter`, a config-change listener filtered to this section, a `getServerSpec(..., flushCredentialCache)` story for the password case, and cache invalidation for `plansByLabel`/`scopesByServerName` — a coherent unit of work, not a review-day edit. | Add an `EventEmitter<void>` exposed as `onDidChangeMcpServerDefinitions`, fired from `workspace.onDidChangeConfiguration` (filtered on `irisMcpLauncher`) and from Server Manager's `onDidChangePassword`. For the password case pass `flushCredentialCache: true` to `getServerSpec` on the refresh path. Consider a `version` derived from the plan hash so the editor prompts to refresh tools. Remove the two "try reloading the window" hints once done. |
| 31-4-3 | An empty username is synthesized and passed straight through. The `spec.password` branch uses `spec.username ?? ""` with NO fallback chain at all, and the auth path's chain (spec username → `session.scopes[1]` → `session.account.id`) can end empty since `scopes[1]` is `""` by construction whenever `spec.username` is absent. `synthesizeIrisEnv` always emits the field, so `packages/shared/src/profiles.ts`'s `mergeProfile` (which rejects an empty `username` inside `IRIS_PROFILES`) aborts registry construction — under `combineProfiles` one bad server takes down every other, correctly-resolved profile in the same spawned process. | `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/src/credentials.ts` (both the `spec.password` branch and the fallback chain) | MEDIUM | A genuine product decision, not a mechanical fix: the extension can either (a) refuse to start the definition with a clear "no username could be resolved for X" message, or (b) keep passing it through so the downstream failure names the real constraint. Option (a) is friendlier but blocks a case that might work if the operator later fixes the spec; option (b) is today's behavior and is at least LOUD (the child fails to start). The QA pass deliberately DOCUMENTED this rather than guarding it, and reversing that at review would re-litigate a recorded decision. No live case has been observed — Server Manager specs normally carry a username. | Decide (a) vs (b) explicitly. If (a): return `{status:"unavailable"}` when the resolved username is empty, with a message naming the server and pointing at Server Manager's username field, pinned by a test per branch. Either way, add a no-username server to the AC 31.4.4 smoke, since that is the only way to see which failure the user actually experiences. |
| 31-4-4 | Under `combineProfiles: true`, a Server Manager server literally named `default` is emitted as an `IRIS_PROFILES` key, which `buildProfileRegistry` treats as an override of the RESERVED default profile — so every tool call that omits the `server` parameter silently targets that server with its credentials, while the first profile's values still populate the six top-level `IRIS_*` vars. The only signal is a `logger.warn` in the spawned child's stderr. | `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/src/env.ts` `synthesizeIrisEnv` (profile-key construction) | MEDIUM | Exactly the same reserved-name class as open ledger item `31-3-1`, and it should be decided ONCE for both rather than patched differently on the two sides of the process boundary — the suite already has a documented position on reserved-name collisions (`profiles.ts`'s collision notice, amended at the Story 31.3 review to tell the operator to rename). Requires an operator to have named a server `default`, and `combineProfiles` is opt-in and defaults off. | Decide alongside `31-3-1`. Cheapest honest option here: detect a `default`-named server in `planDefinitions`/`synthesizeIrisEnv` and surface one warning naming the shadowing, mirroring the suite-side notice's wording and remedy (rename it). Do NOT silently rename it in the extension — that would make the `server` parameter disagree with the Server Manager UI. |
| 31-4-5 | The authentication scope array is `[serverName, spec.username ?? ""]`. Scopes are the session identity key for `vscode.authentication`, so when a spec omits `username` the silent probe looks up `["myServer", ""]` and may miss a cached session that other InterSystems extensions stored under `["myServer", "_SYSTEM"]` — re-prompting on every start and defeating the "zero manual config" headline. | `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/src/credentials.ts` (scope construction) | MEDIUM | The extension matches the mandated reference implementation here (`iris-table-editor`'s `ServerConnectionManager`: `const username = smSpec.username || ''; const scopes = [serverName, username];`), and the story explicitly instructed following that precedent rather than inventing the flow. Whether Server Manager's provider actually keys sessions on the username scope — or ignores index 1 — cannot be determined from the type definitions and was NOT reproduced live; changing it speculatively risks BREAKING cache hits that work today. | Resolve empirically during the AC 31.4.4 smoke: configure a Server Manager server WITHOUT a username, authenticate once, restart the MCP server, and observe whether a second prompt appears. If it does, try `getAccount(spec)`'s `account.id` as the scope's second element (the account hint the API already provides) and re-measure. Only then change the scope array, and record the observation in `architecture.md`. |
| 31-4-6 | `CancellationToken` is ignored in both provider methods — neither `provideMcpServerDefinitions` nor `resolveMcpServerDefinition` declares or checks it. When the editor cancels a pending tool call mid-resolution of a multi-server `combineProfiles` plan, the extension keeps prompting for servers 3..N and still mutates `server.env` for work nobody is waiting on. | `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/src/extension.ts` (both provider callbacks) | LOW | Only observable under `combineProfiles` (opt-in, default off) with several servers AND an editor-side cancellation, and the worst outcome is a wasted prompt plus discarded work — never wrong data, never a leaked credential (the resolved env is dropped, and the containment tests cover the discard paths). Threading a token through `LauncherProvider` → `resolveServerCredentials` is a signature change across three modules for a cosmetic win. | Accept the `token` parameter in both callbacks, pass it into `resolveEnvForLabel`, and check `token.isCancellationRequested` at the top of each loop iteration in the multi-server path, returning `undefined` WITHOUT a warning (a cancellation is not a user error). Fold in with `31-4-1`, which touches the same call path. |
| 31-4-7 | The definition sets no `cwd` and spawns `npx -y @iris-mcp/<pkg>` unpinned. `npx` resolves against the extension host's working directory, so a checkout containing `node_modules/@iris-mcp/dev` shadows the published package — most likely to bite developers of THIS repository — and `-y` always resolves the latest published version, so a breaking `@iris-mcp/*` release changes behavior for every user with no version knob. | `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/src/serverDefinitionProvider.ts` (`command`/`args`) and `src/extension.ts` (no `server.cwd`) | LOW | Two entangled product decisions, neither specified by any AC: which directory the child should run in (the extension host's cwd is arbitrary; the workspace root is a supply-chain risk for an untrusted repo — the same "which directory do we trust?" question already open as ledger item `31-3-9`), and whether to pin the spawned package version (which couples the extension's release cadence to the npm packages', explicitly called out as INDEPENDENT in the README's publish checklist). | Take both decisions together at publish time. Minimum: set `server.cwd` to a neutral directory (e.g. the extension's own `extensionUri`) so a local `node_modules` cannot shadow the published package, and add an `irisMcpLauncher.packageVersion` setting (default `"latest"`) appended as `@iris-mcp/<pkg>@<version>`. Document both in the README settings table with their default states (Rules #30/#43). |
| 31-4-8 | `extension.exports` is cached without shape validation. If Server Manager activates but exports an object missing `getServerNames`/`getServerSpec`/`getAccount` (an API change or a partial activation), the truthy-but-wrong object is cached permanently — `getServerManagerApi`'s `if (cachedApi) return cachedApi` short-circuits forever — so the `!api` "extension is not available" branch can never fire for the rest of the session. | `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/src/extension.ts` `getServerManagerApi` | LOW | Largely mitigated at this review: `providePlannedDefinitions` now wraps `getServerNames()` and `resolveServerCredentials` wraps `getServerSpec`/`getAccount`, so a wrong-shaped API degrades to one clear warning and zero registered servers instead of a rejected provider promise. What remains is only that the message says "could not read the server list" rather than "Server Manager's API is not the shape this extension expects", and that the bad object stays cached. `extensionDependencies` makes the scenario unlikely. | Duck-type the exports before caching (`typeof exports?.getServerNames === "function" && typeof exports?.getServerSpec === "function"`), return `undefined` and do NOT cache when the check fails, and give that case its own message naming the version mismatch. Cheap; fold into the next touch of `extension.ts`. |
| 31-4-9 | **AC 31.4.4's hands-on Copilot smoke and cancel-path verification remain OPEN.** Requires a human in a real VS Code instance with GitHub Copilot Chat and configured Server Manager profiles: install the VSIX, confirm the MCP list shows one entry per (package, server) with zero manual config, invoke an `iris-dev-mcp` tool, and specifically exercise Cancel/Escape on the credential prompt. | `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/31-4-broker-extension.md` (Completion Notes, steps 1-6) | — (open AC, sanctioned) | **Not a defect and not a re-deferral** — the story pre-committed to leaving this open, and the record was audited at review and found honest and precise: nothing is claimed complete on the strength of unit tests, the six steps are unambiguous and executable, and the openness is propagated to the user-facing `CHANGELOG.md` "Known limitations".
