While there, opportunistically settle `31-4-1` (how many prompts actually appear on a cold multi-definition start), `31-4-3` (a server with no username) and `31-4-5` (whether a cached session is found when the spec omits `username`) — all three are questions only a live run can answer. |

**Dismissed (not carried):** (1) *"`activationEvents: []` means the extension never activates"* — disproven: VS Code's `mcpContributionPoint` declares an `activationEventsGenerator` that yields `onMcpCollection:<id>` for every contributed provider id, so the empty array is CORRECT (verified by the reviewer against `microsoft/vscode`'s `mcpConfiguration.ts`, and independently by the Edge Case layer). (2) *"`definitions.test.ts`'s 'plan labels are stable/deterministic' test cannot fail"* — true (it calls a pure template function twice with identical literals) but harmless and documentation-shaped; the label contract is really pinned by the resolve-time lookup tests. (3) *"`env.ts`'s `first.namespace || namespaceDefault` is a dead fallback because `readSettings` guarantees non-empty"* — `synthesizeIrisEnv` is an exported pure function tested directly, so the branch is reachable by design and is the function's own contract, not dead code. (4) *"`packaging.test.ts` skips itself exactly when it matters (no `dist`)"* — deliberate and documented; the sibling assertion in the same file checks `main` against `tsconfig`'s `rootDir`/`outDir` mapping deterministically without a build, so the unbuilt case IS covered. (5) *"`--allow-missing-repository` is a no-op since `repository` is declared"* — correct and inert; the flag costs nothing and `npm run package` was verified to work as documented. Folded rather than dismissed: *"`connection.ts`'s `host !== undefined` guard is contradicted by its own non-optional type"* — the guard was right and the TYPE was optimistic (`intersystems.servers` is hand-editable JSON), so the whole function was hardened to `typeof` checks instead.

**Disposition tally:** 9 deferred (8 findings + 1 sanctioned open AC) · **18** patched-in-story · 5 dismissed · 0 decision-needed = **31 findings** after dedupe across three layers plus reviewer-originated verification. Counted mechanically per Rule #51: `grep -c '\[Review\]\[Patch\]'` and `grep -c '\[Review\]\[Defer\]'` over the story's Review Findings section (18 and 9), and `grep -c '^| 31-4-'` over the table above (9).

**Epic-31 ledger state after this story:** Epic-30-own LOW items stay at re-deferral count 2 (untouched — nothing in this story is in `packages/**`). Epic-31-own items `31-0-5`, `31-1-2`…`31-1-5`, `31-2-1`…`31-2-6`, `31-3-1`…`31-3-9` remain at count 1; `31-4-1`…`31-4-9` enter at count 0. Per Rule #37, the Epic-30 batch reaches its **third consecutive re-deferral at Epic 32**, which would then require a dedicated burn-down story with a terminal disposition for every carried item — note that `31-4-4` should be burned down together with `31-3-1` (one reserved-name decision, two sides of the process boundary).

## Post-Epic-31 coverage-gap fix (2026-07-26) — `.code-workspace` discovery

Found by the Project Lead while preparing the AC 31.4.4 manual smoke, NOT by any review layer: `discoverSettingsFiles` enumerated the workspace `.vscode/settings.json` and the per-product user `settings.json` only. A VS Code **`.code-workspace`** file — a legitimate and, for multi-root workspaces, often the ONLY home for `intersystems.servers` — was never a discovery candidate, and `parseIntersystemsServers` looked only at the top-level key, so it could not have read one even if named in `IRIS_SM_SETTINGS_PATHS`. Because `auto` does not fail on zero definitions, such a user imported **nothing, silently**.

**Fixed directly on `feature/feature-wave-3-connection-config`** (Epic 31 was already closed and merged; the fix is small, well-scoped, and in the discovery path everything else depends on):
- `discoverSettingsFiles` now also emits every `*.code-workspace` file directly inside the workspace directory, sorted by filename for determinism, positioned **between** `.vscode/settings.json` and the user settings — mirroring VS Code's own folder > workspace > user scope ranking, which the existing first-file-wins rule then turns into exactly that precedence. The directory listing is fully guarded (missing dir / permission error / not-a-directory ⇒ contributes nothing, never throws) and is not reached when `IRIS_SERVER_MANAGER` is `off`, so the "off touches ZERO filesystem" proof (AC 31.0.3) still holds.
- `parseIntersystemsServers` accepts BOTH file shapes: top-level `intersystems.servers` (a `settings.json`) and the `settings`-nested form (a `.code-workspace`). Top level wins if a file somehow carries both. This also makes `IRIS_SM_SETTINGS_PATHS` work when it names a workspace file.

**Verification.** +11 tests (`@iris-mcp/shared` 1082 → **1093**); `pnpm turbo run build test lint type-check` 25/25; `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0, frozen `1e62c5ad5bf7` / 141 unchanged. **Mutation-verified both halves** (backed up by file COPY, never `git checkout --`, per the Epic 29 retro rule, since the diff was uncommitted): removing the discovery insertion ⇒ 4 red; removing the parser fallback ⇒ 5 red; both restored byte-identical with zero residue. **Rule #19 gate re-checked and unweakened** — flipping the unset default `off`→`auto` still turns 5 tests red, the same as before this change. **Live-proven end to end** against the Project Lead's real `iris-execute-mcp-v2.code-workspace`: a workspace directory containing only that file now yields the `local` profile with `sourceFile` naming the workspace file (it previously yielded nothing), and `HEAD /api/atelier/` through that profile succeeds against live IRIS; against the real repo — which has both files — `local` correctly resolves from `.vscode/settings.json`, proving the precedence order.

Docs updated at the point of use (Rules #30/#43): the root README's discovery-order bullet now states all three scopes and the two accepted file shapes.

## Post-Epic-31 coverage-gap fix (2026-07-26) — Linux user-scope completeness (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` + Flatpak)

Second gap in the same discovery surface, again raised by the Project Lead — this time by asking directly whether user scope was correct on all three platforms. **Windows and macOS were correct** (Windows empirically confirmed against the Project Lead's real `%APPDATA%` `\Code\User\settings.json`; macOS matches the documented `~/Library/Application Support/<Product>/User/settings.json`). **Linux was correct only for the default case**, missing two real install layouts:

1. **`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` was ignored.** VS Code is Electron, and `app.getPath("appData")` on Linux is documented as `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` OR `~/.config` — not hard-wired to `~/.config`. Discovery hard-coded `~/.config`, so a user who sets `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (NixOS, dotfile managers, some containers) imported nothing.
2. **Flatpak installs were unreachable.** A Flatpak app cannot write `~/.config`; Flatpak redirects the app's `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` into `~/.var/app/<app-id>/config`. Flatpak is a common install route on Fedora/Silverblue and increasingly on Ubuntu, so those users imported nothing — silently, since `auto` does not fail on zero.

**Evidence note (Rule #14/#16).** An initial research pass asserted Electron does NOT honor `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, contradicting Electron's own docs; it cited blog posts rather than the docs page and was NOT accepted. The question was settled from the other direction by the official Flatpak packaging docs, which state that Flatpak sandboxes an Electron app's config precisely BY redirecting `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` — a mechanism that only works if Electron honors it. The fix rests on that, not on the first answer.

**Fixed** on `feature/feature-wave-3-connection-config`:

- `userSettingsPathFor` → `userSettingsPathsFor` (now returns a list). On Linux it emits `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/<Product>/User/settings.json` first when set, then `~/.config/<Product>/User/settings.json`, de-duplicated for the common `XDG_CONFIG_HOME=$HOME/.config` case. Windows (`%APPDATA%`) and macOS are unchanged, and both deliberately ignore `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` — it is not their config mechanism (pinned by tests).
- New `flatpakSettingsPaths`, Linux-only, emitting the three **verified** Flathub app ids paired with their Electron product directory: `com.visualstudio.code` → `Code`, `com.visualstudio.code.insiders` → `Code - Insiders`, `com.vscodium.codium` → `VSCodium`. Native user paths are searched BEFORE Flatpak ones, so a native install wins a same-name collision. **Cursor is deliberately absent — it is not published on Flathub**, and a guessed app id would be worse than no entry; `IRIS_SM_SETTINGS_PATHS` is the documented escape hatch for unlisted layouts (including VS Code portable mode, which has no discoverable root on any platform and remains uncovered by design).

**Verification.** +12 tests (`@iris-mcp/shared` 1093 → **1103**, including the intentionally-updated linux exact-list pin); `pnpm turbo run build test lint type-check` 25/25; `gen:governance-baseline:check` exit 0, frozen `1e62c5ad5bf7` / 141 unchanged. **Mutation-verified both halves** (backed up by file COPY per the Epic 29 rule — the diff was uncommitted): dropping the XDG branch ⇒ 3 red; dropping the Flatpak candidates ⇒ 4 red; both restored byte-identical with zero residue. **Rule #19 gate re-checked and still unweakened** — `off`→`auto` still turns 5 tests red.

Docs updated at the point of use (Rules #30/#43): the root README's discovery-order bullet now lists all four scope tiers plus a Linux-specifics bullet naming the XDG/Flatpak rationale, the Cursor-not-on-Flathub exclusion, and portable mode as the escape-hatch case.

**Process note:** the first attempt to append this very entry failed silently-ish — a Python heredoc parsed `\User` in a Windows path as a `\U` unicode escape and aborted, while the `git commit` in the same command chain still succeeded. The code fix shipped in `7b3f1a3` with its ledger entry missing until this follow-up. Third instance this epic of shell/Python quoting mangling content (cf. the NUL-byte binary file and the Story 31.2 review's heredoc trouble); prefer writing content to a file and appending it, over inlining it in a shell heredoc.

## Deferred from: code review of 31-5-launcher-selection-ui (2026-07-26)

Story 31.5 (Launcher Server-Selection UI, `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/`) — the post-retro story Epic 31 was
re-opened for. Nothing below touches `packages/**`; these items live entirely in
`extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/`, so they do NOT interact with the frozen governance baseline, tool counts, or
`bootstrap-classes.ts`.

Verified live at review: extension `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vitest run` **162/162 across 11 files**
(baseline 107/10 → dev 140/11 → QA 153/11 → review 162/11, counted mechanically from the runner output per
Rule #51), `npm run build` clean, zero `packages/**` files modified, no binary/NUL-byte files in the diff.
**Thirteen findings were PATCHED IN-STORY and are not carried** — including the HIGH: the `workspaceFolder`
write target was a branch the real VS Code API can never reach (and is documented to reject if it were),
"proven correct" only by fakes returning an impossible `inspect()` shape. AC 31.5.2 was amended in `epics.md`
rather than worked around in code (skill Rule 5). The six items below are what remains.

- **`31-5-1` — HIGH (skill Rule 3) — no real-runtime (Extension-Host) test evidence for a user-facing UI
  surface.** `src/extension.ts` is the only file with a value-level `import * as vscode` and is imported by
  **zero** tests; `package.json` has no `@vscode/test-electron` / `@vscode/test-cli`, so there is no
  Extension-Host tier at all. Everything that constitutes the shipped UX — status bar creation and `show()`,
  the click wiring, the `onDidChangeConfiguration` filter, `showQuickPick`'s real options object,
  `toConfigurationTarget`, `updateServersConfig` — runs only in a real VS Code process.
  *Why deferred, not fixed:* standing up an Extension-Host tier means a new dev dependency, a downloaded VS
  Code build, and a second `vitest`-independent runner — its own story, and not runnable in the headless
  environment this review ran in. *What WAS done instead:* the mechanically-closable part is now pinned from
  disk in `packaging.test.ts`'s new "extension.ts wiring" describe — the `ConfigWriteTarget` →
  `vscode.ConfigurationTarget` mapping (a swap type-checks cleanly and is exactly AC 31.5.2's named failure
  mode), the declared setting `scope`, the command/status-bar-before-MCP activation order, the status bar
  `command` constant, and the `affectsConfiguration(CONFIG_SECTION)` filter. Two of those were
  mutation-verified red→green. *Suggested resolution:* a dedicated story adding `@vscode/test-electron` with a
  smoke suite that activates the extension, asserts the status bar item exists with the zero-state text, and
  drives the command end-to-end against a real `WorkspaceConfiguration`. *Compensating control until then:*
  the Project Lead's per-story manual smoke, which Rule 3 itself names as a separate later gate — AC 31.5.4's
  empirical step is part of it.

- **`31-5-2` — LOW — the status bar count is the raw `settings.servers.length`, which can diverge from the
  number of MCP definitions actually registered.** `buildStatusBarState` counts the literal
  `irisMcpLauncher.servers` array; `planDefinitions()` de-duplicates AND intersects with the Server Manager
  roster. So a hand-edited `["prod","prod"]` shows `IRIS MCP: 2` while one server is registered, and a
  mistyped name shows `1` while zero are. *Why deferred:* this is the open design question the QA stage
  explicitly flagged for a **product decision**, and AC 31.5.3 does not specify raw vs effective — its wording
  ("when the roster is empty") matches the current raw reading. Current behavior is pinned by a test, so it
  cannot drift unnoticed. The blast radius is small: the command itself de-dupes on write and the setting
  declares `uniqueItems: true`, so only a hand-edit produces it. *Suggested resolution:* Project Lead decides
  whether the status bar should report the raw setting or the effective registered count; if effective, feed
  the resolved roster into `buildStatusBarState` (it is pure, so this stays unit-testable) and update the
  pinning test.

- **`31-5-3` — LOW — unchecking every item writes `[]`, which means "expose ALL servers" — the inverse of the
  gesture.** A user who opens the picker to stop exposing anything and unchecks everything ends up exposing
  strictly more. *Why deferred:* the `[]`-means-all semantic is inherited from Story 31.4's setting contract,
  not introduced by this story, and both the post-write confirmation and the zero-state tooltip explain it in
  words. A confirm-before-writing-empty prompt is a new UI interaction no AC asks for. *Suggested resolution:*
  either a modal confirmation on an empty selection, or a separate explicit "expose none" representation
  distinct from `[]` — a settings-contract change that should be decided alongside `31-5-2`.

- **`31-5-4` — LOW — `onDidChangeMcpServerDefinitions` is never supplied or fired**, so a saved selection has
  no effect until the user reloads. `McpServerDefinitionProvider` declares the optional event
  (`@types/vscode@1.125.0` `index.d.ts` ~20518) and the object literal in `extension.ts` supplies only
  `provideMcpServerDefinitions` / `resolveMcpServerDefinition`. *Why deferred:* AC 31.5.6 **deliberately**
  specifies reload semantics ("writing the setting does not retroactively change already-registered MCP
  definitions; the confirmation therefore states plainly that a reload ... is required"), so wiring the event
  now would contradict the AC as written rather than fix a defect. *Suggested resolution:* a follow-up story
  that amends AC 31.5.6 first, then adds a `vscode.EventEmitter<void>` pushed to `context.subscriptions`,
  fired from the same `onDidChangeConfiguration` handler that refreshes the status bar, and softens the
  confirmation wording.

- **`31-5-5` — LOW — `$(…)` in Server Manager metadata renders as a theme icon in QuickPick rows.**
  `QuickPickItem.label`/`.description`/`.detail` all document "Supports rendering of theme icons via the
  `$(<name>)`-syntax", so a server literally named `$(zap)prod` renders an icon instead of its text; the value
  written back is still the raw name, so settings stay correct but the user picked partly blind. *Why
  deferred:* `@types/vscode` documents no escape for `QuickPickItem` fields (unlike `MarkdownString`), so a
  blind `$(` → `$\(` substitution risks corrupting legitimately-displayed names — that would be a worse
  outcome than the cosmetic issue. Server names come from the user's own Server Manager config, not from an
  untrusted source. *Suggested resolution:* verify empirically in a real Extension Host whether an escape is
  honored (natural companion to `31-5-1`), and only then escape.

- **`31-5-6` — LOW — both source-file rosters are non-recursive.** `containment.test.ts`'s `SOURCE_FILES` and
  `packaging.test.ts`'s `sourceFiles` each use a flat `readdirSync(SRC_DIR)`, so a module added under a future
  `src/commands/` or `src/views/` subdirectory would be invisible to the credential-leak grep, the
  `.update(` write pin, and the `contributes.commands` ↔ `registerCommand` cross-check — a hand-rostered
  blind spot of exactly the kind Rule #51 exists to prevent. *Why deferred:* `src/` is flat today (10 files),
  so nothing is currently uncovered, and making both recursive is a behavior change to two guards better done
  in one deliberate pass than as review drive-by. *Suggested resolution:* switch both to
  `readdirSync(SRC_DIR, { recursive: true, withFileTypes: true })` (Node 20+, already the declared
  `@types/node` floor), excluding `__tests__`, and add a test asserting the roster picks up a nested file.

## Deferred from: code review of 31-6-local-package-path (2026-07-26)

Three-layer adversarial review; 17 findings were patched in-story (including three HIGHs: the
`PACKAGE_DIR_NAME` correspondence gap, the fail-OPEN relative `developmentRepoPath`, and the missing
`"scope": "machine"` on a setting that names an executable). The six below were deferred.

- **`31-6-1` — MEDIUM — the two new warnings re-fire on every `providePlannedDefinitions()` call.**
  `serverDefinitionProvider.ts`'s stale-`"all"` warning and the aggregated `developmentRepoPath` warning both
  call `showWarning` unconditionally; verified live, three provide calls on one provider instance produced six
  warnings. The `warnedPathPrefixes` set immediately above them exists precisely "so the docs' 'one-time
  warning' is literally true", and neither new warning has an equivalent. Blast radius is currently bounded:
  `extension.ts` supplies no `onDidChangeMcpServerDefinitions` emitter, so VS Code re-enumerates only on
  activation / MCP refresh. *Why deferred:* the pre-existing "none of the configured servers match" warning
  behaves identically, so this is a consistency gap across all three warnings rather than a regression this
  story introduced, and fixing one in isolation would leave the surface inconsistent. *Suggested resolution:*
  one dedupe set keyed by warning identity covering all three, applied in a single pass, with a test driving
  `providePlannedDefinitions()` repeatedly and asserting one warning total. Becomes user-visible the moment an
  `onDidChangeMcpServerDefinitions` emitter lands.

- **`31-6-2` — MEDIUM — synchronous `fs` validation blocks the VS Code extension host on a network path.**
  `isExistingDirectory`/`isExistingFile` (`serverDefinitionProvider.ts:63-77`) call `existsSync`/`statSync`
  inside `provideMcpServerDefinitions`, which VS Code invokes on the single-threaded extension host — every
  extension in the window stalls for the duration. Measured on this machine: `existsSync` on a nonexistent UNC
  host blocked **1281 ms** (a fast DNS failure; a reachable-but-hung SMB share is far worse). The story
  explicitly enumerates UNC paths as a supported input shape, and the one UNC test mocks `existsSync` away, so
  the suite gives zero signal here. *Why deferred:* `providePlannedDefinitions` is already `async`, so moving
  to `fs/promises` is mechanically feasible, but it changes the shape of `resolveSpawnTargets` and its ten
  tests — a deliberate change, not a review drive-by. *Suggested resolution:* switch both helpers to
  `fs/promises` with `Promise.all` over the per-package checks, and add a test asserting the validation does
  not run serially per plan.

- **`31-6-3` — MEDIUM — `command: "node"` is resolved from the extension host's PATH and never validated.**
  `resolveSpawnTargets` proves `dist/index.js` exists but assumes an interpreter. A Windows user with
  nvm-windows/Volta shims that only exist in an interactive shell, or VS Code launched from a Start Menu
  shortcut with a stale PATH, passes every check and then dies at spawn with an opaque ENOENT inside VS Code's
  MCP output — bypassing the legible failure path that is the entire point of this story's fail-closed work.
  *Why deferred:* AC 31.6.1 pins the spawn as `node <repoPath>/packages/<dir>/dist/index.js`, so switching to
  the standard extension-host pattern (`process.execPath` with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`) would contradict the
  AC as written and needs a spec amendment first (skill Rule 5). *Suggested resolution:* amend AC 31.6.1 to
  name the interpreter as an implementation detail, then use `process.execPath`, which removes the PATH
  dependency entirely; alternatively keep `node` and add an explicit resolvability probe with its own reason
  string.

- **`31-6-4` — LOW — the status bar tooltip claims development mode is spawning local builds even when it
  registered nothing.** `buildStatusBarState` (`selectServers.ts:376-383`) branches only on
  `developmentRepoPath !== ""`; it never sees `resolveSpawnTargets`'s verdict. With a typo'd path the status
  bar reads `$(server) IRIS MCP: 2` plus "Development mode: spawning from local build at C:\typo" while zero
  definitions exist — AC 31.6.7's stated purpose ("never ambiguous whether the extension is running local
  builds or published packages") is inverted in exactly the state where it matters most. Note the count was
  already `settings.servers.length` rather than a registered-definition count (pre-existing), so this is a
  sharper instance of an existing divergence. *Why deferred:* `buildStatusBarState` is a pure function of
  `LauncherSettings` by design and is pinned by AC 31.5.3's tests; giving it the provider's verdict means
  plumbing new state through `extension.ts`'s refresh path. *Suggested resolution:* pass an optional
  "definitions actually registered" count into `buildStatusBarState` and word the dev-mode line from it.

- **`31-6-5` — LOW — live IRIS credentials hardcoded in a committed test file.**
  `localSpawnIntegration.test.ts` hardcodes `_SYSTEM`/`SYS` and asserts
  `expect(env?.IRIS_PASSWORD).toBe(IRIS_PASSWORD)`, so a failure prints both values to stdout/CI logs — in an
  extension whose headline invariant is "no password ever written to a log/output channel" and which maintains
  a dedicated `containment.test.ts` for it. `containment.test.ts`'s `SOURCE_FILES` roster deliberately excludes
  `__tests__/`, so no guard covers this file. *Why deferred:* these are the documented local dev defaults used
  throughout this repo's docs and rules, and changing the convention in one test file is not the right scope.
  *Suggested resolution:* read them from `IRIS_TEST_*` env vars with the current values as fallbacks, and
  decide repo-wide whether `__tests__/` should join the containment roster.

- **`31-6-6` — LOW — the extension's unit tests now hard-require the monorepo `packages/` tree on disk.**
  `definitions.test.ts` reads `<repoRoot>/packages` for the AC 31.6.2 cross-check and
  `localSpawnIntegration.test.ts` probes `<repoRoot>/node_modules/.pnpm`, so the extension folder is no longer
  testable in isolation (standalone `vsce` packaging checks, sparse checkouts). This is in tension with the
  README's "nothing in `packages/**` depends on it, and it depends on nothing in `packages/**`" claim, which is
  about build-time dependencies but reads more broadly. *Partially mitigated in review:* the `readdirSync` is
  now guarded so a missing `packages/` produces a named diagnostic instead of an ENOENT stack trace.
  *Why deferred:* the cross-check's value comes precisely from reading the real tree (Rule #51), so removing
  the dependency would weaken the HIGH fix it implements. *Suggested resolution:* keep the disk oracle, and
  narrow the README claim to build-time/runtime dependency rather than the test tier.

## Epic 31 GUI smoke (2026-07-26) — AC 31.4.4 + AC 31.5.4 closed; `31-5-1` compensating control satisfied

The Project Lead ran the full six-step GUI smoke in real VS Code 1.128.0. **All six steps confirmed working.** Full record in the Completion Notes of both `31-4-broker-extension.md` and `31-5-launcher-selection-ui.md`.

**What this closes:**

- **AC 31.5.4 (empirical half)** — the status bar item IS present after a plain window reload with MCP never exercised, proving `onStartupFinished` activation works. The dev agent correctly declined to claim this headlessly rather than asserting it; the lead performed it.
- **AC 31.4.4 (except the Copilot hop)** — registration, both credential paths, and the cancel path all confirmed against real VS Code.
- **The Story 31.4 headline HIGH is now confirmed fixed on real infrastructure.** `authentication.getSession({createIfNone:true})` REJECTS on cancel rather than resolving `undefined`; there was no `try`/`catch` on the path and every test faked the wrong shape — a green suite over an impossible path. Code review fixed it, mutation-verified it (4 tests red on revert), and the lead has now exercised the real cancel branch in a real session.
- **`31-5-1` (Rule 3 real-runtime gap) — compensating control SATISFIED, item narrowed but NOT closed.** Story 31.6's `localSpawnIntegration.test.ts` added genuine automated real-runtime evidence (real child process, real MCP handshake, live IRIS, runs rather than skips), and this manual smoke covers the VS Code-host surface that no automated tier can reach. What remains open is only the absent `@vscode/test-electron` Extension-Host tier, which would let `src/extension.ts` be exercised automatically instead of by hand. Keep `31-5-1` open at reduced severity.

**Residual risk, recorded not hidden — the Copilot consumer hop.** AC 31.4.4 as written says "Copilot chat lists an iris-dev-mcp tool set". The Project Lead does not use Copilot (no `github.copilot-chat` installed; their clients are Claude Code, Cline and Kimi Code, none of which consume VS Code's MCP registry). Registration into the registry IS confirmed (step 3, `MCP: List Servers`), so the only unverified link is the final hop inside Copilot's own UI. Deferred to an external Copilot user. This is the one AC in Epic 31 closed on partial evidence, and it is deliberate: the extension's whole audience is Copilot-family users, and nobody on this project is one.

**New observation, unrelated to Epic 31 — `31-7-1` (LOW, pre-existing).** Every MCP server start logs `[WARN] CSRF preflight completed but no X-CSRF-Token header was returned. Mutating requests may be rejected by IRIS.` twice. It predates this epic, occurs regardless of launch path (extension, Claude Code, or a direct spawn), and blocked nothing during the smoke — every start/stop cycle was clean and all tool calls succeeded. Suggested resolution: determine whether the Atelier CSRF preflight is expected to return `X-CSRF-Token` on this IRIS configuration, and either stop emitting the warning when the token is legitimately absent, or explain the condition in the message. Not urgent; do not fix blind.

## Story 32.3 — Rule #37 BURN-DOWN: terminal dispositions (2026-07-26)

The Epic 32 burn-down story (Story 32.3) drove every carried-open item to a TERMINAL disposition. No re-deferrals. Rule #48 bar applied: every "resolved" code fix was proven by a revert→red→restore cycle (test written/updated against the pre-fix code went red, green after the fix — or an explicit mutation injected and caught) unless the entry names LIVE evidence instead. Rule #16 probe-first applied to 31-4-1/31-4-5/31-5-5/31-7-1. Paired decisions taken ONCE (AC 32.3.4): **PD-1** (31-1-2 + 31-3-3) = FIRST-FILE-WINS, always — a name's fate is decided at its first sighting across the precedence-ordered settings files and is never reconsidered (the Story-31.1 "rescue" is removed; a skipped password-bearing lower-precedence definition warns naming both hosts and the `iris-mcp-credentials set <name>` remedy); **PD-2** (31-3-1 + 31-4-4) = a reserved-name/collision is never silent on EITHER side of the process boundary — suite side: aggregate warning under `auto`, startup failure under `required` when every SM definition collides; extension side: one warning naming the shadowing and the rename remedy; never auto-rename. **PD-3** (31-4-2 + 31-5-4) = keep the AC-31.5.6-documented reload semantics (no `onDidChangeMcpServerDefinitions` in v1; the limitation is documented in the extension README/CHANGELOG and every stale-state message already says to reload).

## Deferred from: code review of 32-0-governance-file-loader (2026-07-26)

Epic-32-own findings from the Story 32.0 review. **All three spawned review layers failed to return; their mandates were executed reviewer-direct** (mirroring the Story 32.3 review). **0 HIGH / 0 MEDIUM / 1 LOW deferred · 4 dismissed · 0 decision-needed · 0 patched.** Re-deferral count 1 (first carry). Verified live at review: `@iris-mcp/shared` 60 files/1186 tests green, `@iris-mcp/dev` 37/608, `iris-mcp-all` 15/97 (incl. QA's 5-test process gate re-run by the reviewer against the built dist + live IRIS, 5/5 executed not skipped); `pnpm gen:governance-baseline:check` (`:check` only) exit 0 (frozen `1e62c5ad5bf7` / 141 / 201 / 60 unchanged); cascade ordering mutation-verified (rejected interleaved ordering ⇒ 3 red, restored byte-identical); NUL-byte scan clean; tallies mechanically recounted (Rule #51).

| # | Finding | Location | Severity | Deferral rationale | Suggested resolution |
|---|---------|----------|----------|--------------------|----------------------|
| 32-0-1 | `iris_env_promote`'s Gate 4 calls `loadGovernanceFile()` on EVERY `execute` call, while the D5 gate, the `iris-governance://` resource, and `iris_server_profiles` all use the startup snapshot (`this.governanceFileConfig`). A mid-session edit to the governance file is honored at Gate 4 but nowhere else — contradicting the documented "read once at startup; restart to apply (no hot-reload in v1)" contract at exactly one enforcement point, in both directions (a file edit that ENABLES a write-family key takes effect without restart; one that DISABLES is enforced at Gate 4 while the reporting surfaces still show the old value). No security degradation (the operator controls the file; enforcement follows the operator's own edit), and the precondition (editing the file while a server runs, contra docs) is operator-error territory. | `packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/env-promote.ts` (`executeAction`, Gate 4: `const governanceFileConfig = loadGovernanceFile();`) | LOW | The correct fix is a DESIGN choice, not an unambiguous patch: either (a) thread the startup-loaded snapshot through `ToolContext` so Gate 4 reads the same frozen view as D5 (touches the shared context contract — every server constructs it), or (b) a shared memoized accessor in `governance.ts` (process-lifetime cache, mirroring the startup read-once semantics). Both exceed a code-review patch's scope and interact with Story 32.1's CLI (which must NOT memoize — a CLI process should read the file fresh per invocation, so the accessor needs a no-cache mode or the CLI keeps calling `loadGovernanceFile` directly). | Decide in Story 32.1 (which designs the CLI's own file access and will make the memoization contract explicit): preferred — a shared `getProcessGovernanceFileConfig()` accessor with load-once semantics for long-lived servers and a documented fresh-read path for one-shot CLI invocations; env-promote Gate 4 switches to the accessor. Add a regression test: a file edited after startup does NOT change Gate 4's verdict within the same process. |

**Dismissed (not carried):** (1) `iris-governance://` resource payload grew from a bare policy map to `{ policy, configSource }` — spec-sanctioned wire-shape change (AC 32.0.1's 2026-07-26 clarification explicitly excludes the serialized report from the back-compat deep-equal; all 10 in-repo parse sites updated mechanically, downstream assertions untouched). (2) Set-but-empty `IRIS_GOVERNANCE_FILE` treated as unset — matches the `IRIS_AUDIT_LOG` unset/empty convention; zero-fs spy test pins it. (3) BOM'd JSON governance file fails fast — the fail-safe direction for an explicit operator-supplied path; consistent with the env channel's strictness. (4) Unit-level Rule #19 explicit-`undefined`-vs-omitted-arg proof near-tautological in isolation — compensated by the unchanged-and-green 1139-test pre-existing matrix, the hand-derived seed oracle, the server-level Rule #19 test, and process-gate Case E at the wire.

**Disposition tally:** 1 deferred · 0 patched · 4 dismissed · 0 decision-needed (5 findings after dedupe; layers' mandates covered reviewer-direct — the three spawned layers returned nothing).

**Resolution of 32-0-1 (Story 32.1, 2026-07-27): RESOLVED — design option (a), ToolContext snapshot threading (NOT the ledger's "preferred" memoized accessor).** Rationale for choosing (a) over (b), recorded per the story's either-option sanction: (1) hermetic — no module-level mutable cache, no reset-for-tests API surface, no cross-test poisoning risk (a process-lifetime memo would leak across `start()`s in one test process whenever two servers use different `IRIS_GOVERNANCE_FILE` values); (2) the restart-only snapshot semantics become part of the TYPE contract (`ToolContext.governanceFileConfig?`, optional/absent-by-default) rather than a hidden memo; (3) the regression test is structural and strong — a re-introduced per-call `loadGovernanceFile()` in Gate 4 flips the verdict on a mid-session file rewrite (mutation-verified red during dev); (4) the CLI needs no "no-cache mode" at all — a one-shot process simply calls `loadGovernanceFile` directly, which IS the correct fresh-read-per-invocation semantics. Implementation: `ToolContext` gained optional `governanceFileConfig` (type-only import — no runtime cycle with governance.ts's type-only `ToolDefinition` import); `buildToolContext` gained a 7th optional param (conditional spread, so direct-caller shape is byte-for-byte pre-32.1); `McpServerBase.handleToolCall` passes the startup snapshot (`this.governanceFileConfig`, loaded once in `start()`); `env-promote.ts` Gate 4 reads `ctx.governanceFileConfig` (the `loadGovernanceFile` import removed). Regression test in `env-promote-execute.test.ts`: snapshot disables `iris_config_manage:set`, the on-disk file is then rewritten to re-enable it, the verdict STILL refuses with zero client resolution — mutation-verified (restoring the per-call read ⇒ red; reverted ⇒ 37/37 green). The pre-existing file-channel test was updated to build its snapshot via the REAL loader, keeping file → snapshot → Gate 4 wiring in the loop. Re-deferral count stays 0 — terminal at the first gate.

The three review layers for Story 32.3 (blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, acceptance-auditor) returned their findings ~2 hours AFTER that story's review closed and committed (`c4ff9ae`), having been recorded as failed layers whose mandates were covered reviewer-direct. The lead triaged both delivered lists (blind: 12, edge: 9 after exclusions; acceptance-auditor sent no findings list, only idle notifications) against the committed tree. Deduped across layers: **15 Epic-32-own items, re-deferral count 0.** All require verification-before-patch per Rules #16/#48 (blind-layer findings have produced false positives before); terminal handling is assigned to a dedicated cleanup story (Story 32.4) before Epic 32 closes — these are Epic-32-own, so the AC-22.1.7 shape permits the carry, but the epic should not close on unexamined MEDIUMs.

No fixture covers drop-first-then-valid-lower in either direction. | `packages/shared/src/server-manager-source.ts` (parserDropped loop) | MEDIUM | blind #2 + edge M3 (independent) |

## Project Lead deferral decisions (2026-07-27, post-Epic-32 merge)

Recorded at the Epic 32 close-out so the Epic 33 retro-review gate does not re-triage these as undecided:

1. **npm publish of `@iris-mcp/*` — DEFERRED by Project Lead decision.** Nothing on npm; `npx -y @iris-mcp/<pkg>` fails for external users and the extension's published-mode paths (31.4 spawn, 32.2 governance engine npx mode) remain dev-mode-only. Consequences accepted: Epic 33's client-config docs snippets that assume published packages must be written against the local-path/dev-mode reality, and the post-publish smoke (win32 `cmd.exe` wrap, six-package sibling-dist derivation) stays unexercised. Re-surface when the publish checklist (Epic 9 Story 9.3 lineage + 31-4-7) is picked up.
2. **Copilot consumer-hop verification (AC 31.4.4 residual) — DEFERRED by Project Lead decision.** Registration into VS Code's MCP registry is confirmed; only the final hop inside Copilot's own UI is unverified, and no project member uses Copilot. Remains a recorded residual risk, not an open defect.
3. **32-4-R1 triage — deferred until AFTER the AC 32.2.4 GUI smoke** (Project Lead sequencing, 2026-07-27). The item (LOW: `helpRequested` valued-option mirror needs a mechanical pin against parseArgs) stays open in the ledger; triage happens alongside the smoke's findings rather than at a separate pass. **→ RESOLVED 2026-07-27 (post-smoke triage, smoke PASS).** `VALUED_OPTIONS` single-sourced and exported in `cli/governance.ts`; `helpRequested` consumes it (the second hand-maintained literal removed); a mechanical source-scan pin in `governance-cli.test.ts` asserts every `parseArgs` call site's options ⊆ `VALUED_OPTIONS` and that no new hand-maintained `valuedOptions = new Set(...)` literal exists. **Mutation-verified** (adding `--bogus` to one call site ⇒ pin red; restored ⇒ 63/63 green). Full gates green: turbo 25/25, extension 329/329, baseline `1e62c5ad5bf7` byte-unchanged. **The Epic 32 ledger is now at ZERO open items.**

| 32-1-R6 | Atomic write severs symlinks | **RESOLVED** | `resolveWriteTarget`: when the governance path IS a symlink (lstat-gated, so ordinary paths never get Windows `\\?\` rewrites), the temp+rename lands beside the link's REAL target — the link survives `set`/`unset` (the nix/etckeeper case). Rollback uses the same target. Symlink fixture (skips where symlink creation is unavailable; it runs and passes on this Windows dev-mode host). Mutation-verified (write-through removed ⇒ red ⇒ restored). |
| 32-1-R7 | Rollback pre-image read in a second, later operation | **RESOLVED** | `set`/`unset` read the pre-image ONCE; the SAME text is validated (`parseGovernanceFileText` — NEW export factoring `loadGovernanceFile`'s parse half, so validation stays single-sourced with identical error text) and kept for rollback. Read failures re-run through the real loader for the server's exact error text (the DIRECTORY/EISDIR clean-text contract preserved). Mechanical pin: fs-spy counts exactly one pre-image read per write command (`governance-cli-preimage.test.ts`). Mutation-verified (second read ⇒ count red ⇒ restored). |
| 32-2-R1 | Sync `statSync` in the governance engine/panel path | **RESOLVED** | `resolveGovernanceCli` + `FileExistsProbe` + the panel's `fileExists` dep + `GovernanceEngineHost.describe` are async end-to-end (`fs/promises.stat`, the 31-6-2 discipline): the empty-state render consumes the describe result the most recent async refresh computed (no sync probe on the render path). All call sites and test fakes converted; extension `npx tsc --noEmit` clean + 327/327 tests green (incl. the real-wiring activation flows). |

## Deferred from: code review of 33-1-write-engine (2026-07-27)

Epic-33 findings from the Story 33.1 review. **All three spawned review layers failed to return; their mandates were executed reviewer-direct** (the 32.3/32.0 precedent). **1 HIGH + 2 MEDIUM + 1 LOW patched in-story · 5 LOW deferred · 1 dismissed · 0 decision-needed.** Re-deferral count 0 (first carry). Verified live at review: AC 33.1-I1 spy proof mutation-verified (re-derivation => exactly the I1 test red, restored); Codex `enabled`-flag probe evidence independently re-verified against the live official docs; golden cross-stage oracles byte-compared at file level; the HIGH and both MEDIUM patches re-verified LIVE against the rebuilt dist; package 215 tests/13 files (runner count, Rule #51); full uncached `pnpm turbo run build test lint type-check` 29/29; `pnpm gen:governance-baseline:check` (`:check` only) exit 0 (frozen `1e62c5ad5bf7` / 141 / 201 / 60 unchanged); NUL scan clean.

| # | Item | Severity | Issue | Deferral rationale | Suggested resolution |
|---|------|----------|-------|--------------------|----------------------|
| 33-1-R1 | Stale stash record survives an already-in-state enable no-op | LOW | When a stash client's entry is present in the file AND a stash record exists (reachable via enable's config-write-succeeded-but-writeState-failed warning path), a re-run enable renders already-in-state and the no-op early return in `runEdit` skips `updateState` — the stale stash is never dropped. Self-healing on the next disable (`addStash` replaces), and the harmful cousin (remove-while-stashed) WAS fixed in-story. Cosmetic ledger hygiene only. | In `engine.ts`, drop the stash record when an enable no-ops with the entry present (mirrors the remove fix's purge-on-absent shape); add a MemFs regression. |
| 33-1-R2 | CRLF TOML `set-flag` splices LF-only lines | LOW | `executeTomlSplice`/`tomlEdit` insert or replace the flag line without a `\r`, diluting a CRLF `config.toml` to mixed line endings around the toggled line. Parseable (TOML tolerates mixed endings); the 33.0 CRLF tests assert parseability not byte-identity, and all golden families are LF. Codex configs on Windows are typically LF (Codex writes its own file LF), so real exposure is hand-edited CRLF files. | Carry the matched line's terminator through the splice (`\r\n` when the region line ends `\r`); extend the CRLF TOML test with a byte-identity assertion on the flag round-trip. |
| 33-1-R3 | Comments INSIDE a manager-owned JSONC entry are not stash-round-tripped | LOW | The stash holds the PARSED entry and enable re-inserts via jsonc `modify` determinism (proven byte-exact for manager-rendered entries by the vscode golden) — but a user's hand-added comment INSIDE the owned entry is lost across disable → enable. Comments anywhere ELSE in the file are always preserved. Same documented limitation class as the raw-text TOML stash variant (dev Completion Notes). Owned entries are manager-rendered in v1 flows, so user-in-entry comments are an unusual state. | If in-entry comment preservation is ever required, a raw-text stash variant (store the entry's source span, splice it back verbatim) — the same design noted for a future TOML stash adapter. |
| 33-1-R4 | Backup names collide within the same millisecond | LOW | `stamp()` is millisecond-precision ISO; two writes to the same file landing in the same ms produce one backup path, the second silently overwriting the first. Real-fs engine chains perform several fs ops between backup writes, so sub-ms collision requires pathological timing (or an injected fixed clock — which tests use deliberately). | Append a short disambiguator (counter or nanoseconds) when `fs.exists(backupPath)` is already true at backup time; pin with a two-writes-same-tick test. |
| 33-1-R5 | state.json loss orphans non-canonical manager-created entries | LOW | A present non-canonical entry whose manager-created record is gone (state.json deleted/corrupt-restored) fails ownership for BOTH update and remove — it becomes unmanageable until the file or the record is repaired manually. Canonical entries are unaffected (always owned by name). The corruption path itself is a typed refusal (QA-pinned), so this is the recovery-UX tail. | The 33.2 `doctor` command (spec §3.6) is the natural home: detect present-non-canonical-unrecorded entries and offer to re-record them manager-created with a typed confirmation. **RESOLVED 2026-07-27 (Story 33.2, AC 33.2-I2):** `iris-mcp-clients doctor` reports present `iris-`-namespaced non-canonical entries failing ownership (the `iris-` prefix heuristic — foreign third-party entries fail ownership by design and are never flagged) and re-records them manager-created behind `doctor --repair --yes-i-mean-it` (containsSecret conservatively true, unknown provenance); pinned by the doctor suite in `clients-cli.test.ts` incl. a repair-then-clean round-trip. |

**Dismissed (not carried):** restore being blocked by a corrupt state.json (`resolveTarget` fails before `restoreBackup` runs) — conservative-by-design: the manager never guesses at its own ledger, restore's target is unrelated to state content, and refusal matches the QA-pinned corrupted-state contract. The restore itself needs no ledger.

**Disposition tally:** 4 patched · 5 deferred · 1 dismissed · 0 decision-needed (10 findings; layers' mandates covered reviewer-direct — the three spawned layers returned nothing; any late return goes to lead triage per the 32.3/32.1 late-findings precedent).

## Epic 34 retro-review gate (2026-08-14)

The `/epic-cycle 34` kickoff retro-review gate read the Epic 33 retrospective (`epic-33-retro-2026-07-28.md`) and this ledger, via Story 34.0's triage table (`34-0-epic-33-deferred-cleanup.md`). Carried-open items entering the gate: the full Epic-33 ledger, **17 LOW items, all in `@iris-mcp/client-config`** — 33-1-R1..R4 (engine-level, from the 33-1-write-engine review) and 33-5-L1..L11 + 33-5-R1..R2 (from the 33-5 late-findings-cleanup batch). Every one of these 17 is on its **first** deferral entering this gate (per the 2026-07-28 ledger notes above, each carried batch was recorded "re-deferral count 0 (first carry)").

**Decision: all 17 RE-DEFERRED — first re-deferral, Rule #37 count = 1.** Rationale:

- **Epic 34 is disjoint in scope.** Its two stories touch `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Command` (an ObjectScript REST handler) and the `iris_execute_classmethod` TS tool in `@iris-mcp/dev`, plus Story 34.0's own review-skill/doc changes (`.claude/skills/bmad-code-review/steps/*.md`, this file, `.claude/rules/project-rules.md`). None of these overlaps `packages/client-config/**`, the package every one of the 17 items lives in.
- Per Rule #37, re-deferral is valid at count 1 (only a 3rd CONSECUTIVE re-deferral mandates a dedicated burn-down story); this is the first re-deferral for all 17, well below that threshold.
- **No colliding Story X.0** for the 17-item ledger — Story 34.0 is itself a review-skill/doc cleanup story (Rule #57 implementation, retro action item #5), not a `@iris-mcp/client-config` burn-down. The Epic-34 slot for that burn-down, if the count reaches 3, would be seeded at a future gate.

## Deferred from: code review of 34-0-epic-33-deferred-cleanup (2026-08-14)

Epic-34-own findings from the Story 34.0 review. **All three review layers DELIVERED within the 20-minute hard window** (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Acceptance Auditor) — the first non-degraded 3-layer close since Epic 32, and live evidence that this story's own bounded-close gate works. **2 HIGH / 6 MEDIUM / 3 LOW raised · 2 HIGH + 6 MEDIUM + 1 LOW patched in-review · 2 LOW deferred · 0 decision-needed · 0 dismissed.** Re-deferral count 0 (first carry). Both carried items are in the review skill itself (`.claude/skills/bmad-code-review/steps/`), not in `packages/**`.

| # | Finding | Location | Severity | Deferral rationale | Suggested resolution |
|---|---------|----------|----------|--------------------|----------------------|
| 34-0-R1 | `{review_degraded}` and `{failed_layers}` have no durable persistence during the wait window | `.claude/skills/bmad-code-review/steps/step-02-review.md` (frontmatter + instruction 3) | LOW | Raised by the Edge Case Hunter. These are in-context pseudo-variables that exist only in the runner's working state between step-02 instruction 2 and step-04's write. If the orchestrating session is interrupted, compacted, or restarted during the (potentially 20-minute) wait, the degraded/failed-layer state is lost with no recovery instruction — the review would silently restart from a clean slate. Deferred because the mitigation is architectural (the whole skill is LLM-executed prompt state with no checkpointing anywhere), the blast radius is a re-run rather than a wrong close, and a partial fix for this one variable would imply a durability guarantee the surrounding design does not provide. | Have step-02 instruction 3 write the accounting to a small sidecar (e.g. `review-state-{key}-{run}.json` beside the diff snapshot, same `.gitignore` pattern) as each layer is classified, and have step-04 read it back rather than relying on carried context. Decide it as one piece with any future checkpointing of the skill's other runtime variables. |
| 34-0-R2 | `{story_key}` is never discovered on the invocation-text branch of step-01 instruction 1 | `.claude/skills/bmad-code-review/steps/step-01-gather-context.md` instruction 1 | LOW | Raised by the Blind Hunter, confirmed live. When the review mode is matched from the invocation text (the common case), instruction 1 announces the mode and skips ahead to instruction 4 — never running the sprint-status scan that is the only place `{story_key}` is set. Consequence: step-04's sprint-status sync self-skips ("If `{story_key}` is not set, skip this subsection"), so the story's status is updated in the story file but never in `sprint-status.yaml` unless the caller supplies the key explicitly (as this review's caller did). **Pre-existing** — not introduced by Story 34.0; the story only added a second consumer of the variable, and that consumer now has a well-defined `unkeyed` fallback. Deferred as out of this story's scope. | In instruction 1's invocation-text branch, still consult the sprint status file to resolve `{story_key}` before skipping to instruction 4 — match on the detected story or on a single `review`-status story — so sprint sync works on the common path rather than only when a key is passed in by hand. |

They are recorded so Stories 34.2/34.3 treat them as open decisions rather than settled ones. Most are owned by the immediately-following stories, so "deferred" here means "routed to 34.2/34.3", not "postponed indefinitely". Full narrative lives in Story 34.1's `### Residual Risk` section; the two constraints that were NOT deferred (mnemonic package location C-1, `$ZNAME` same-`.int` C-2) were amended into `epics.md` AC 34.2.1 in-review per Rule #42.

**Tally (27 findings after dedup across three delivered layers + lead verification, counted mechanically per Rule #51):**
- **12 patched in-review** — 4 HIGH · 8 MEDIUM

All three review layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Acceptance Auditor) delivered explicit findings payloads within the 20-minute hard timeout — `failed_layers` empty, review **not** degraded (Rule #57).

Re-deferral count 0 at the time this batch was recorded. **Updated 2026-08-14 (Story 34.3):** the surviving seven items (`R2, R3, R4, R5, R6, R7, R10`) are now at Rule #37 re-deferral count **1** — see "Story 34.3 — TERMINAL dispositions" below for the per-item disposition. Their `Owner` column still reads `34.3`; ownership passes to the next epic that opens (Epic 34 has only its retrospective remaining), and Rule #37's burn-down trigger fires at 3 consecutive carries.

| # | Finding | Severity | Owner | Deferral rationale | Suggested resolution |
|---|---------|----------|-------|--------------------|----------------------|
| 34-2-R1 | A capture `<MAXSTRING>` that the TARGET's own `Try/Catch` swallows produces a silent partial: `output` stops dead at the ceiling, `truncated` stays `0`, response is success | MEDIUM | 34.3 | The `<MAXSTRING>` surfaces as an ordinary catchable error at the target's own `Write` statement, so a target shaped `Try { Write big } Catch {}` absorbs it before `InvokeWithArgs` ever sees it. Requires >2M chars of output AND an exception-swallowing target, so it is narrow — but it is the one remaining shape of the "silent partial" AC 34.2.6/R2 forbids. Not fixed in review because the correct fix changes `Command.cls`'s `Redirects()` labels, which carries Constraint C-2 re-verification and forces rework of the `TargetWriteHuge` fixture (whose doubling loop would then overflow in the target instead). | Make truncation observable from the BUFFER rather than the exception: wrap each concatenating label's `Set` in `Try { … } Catch { Set %ExecuteMCPTruncated = 1 }`, then OR that flag into `pTruncated`. This also improves the primary path — the target completes instead of aborting. Rework `TargetWriteHuge` to write a fixed-size chunk repeatedly so it exercises capture overflow only. |
| 34-2-R2 | No size cap on `output` or `byRefValues` — the partial-success path deliberately returns a 2–4 MB response body | MEDIUM | 34.3 | Rule #38 shape: work and payload scale with what the target produced, with no caller-supplied cap. The realistic client outcome at the ceiling is a transport failure, so the `truncated` flag is least visible exactly where it was designed to matter. Left as a product decision rather than a review patch — picking a ceiling changes the tool's advertised contract, which is Story 34.3's surface. | Truncate `output` to a documented ceiling (e.g. 64–256 KB) with an explicit elision marker; add a node/byte budget to `BuildByRefNode` with a `byRefTruncated` indicator; document both in the tool description. |
| 34-2-R3 | `RenderResponseBody`'s returned `%Status` is discarded; a mid-stream serialization failure ships a truncated, invalid JSON body with HTTP 200 and no error signal | MEDIUM | 34.3 | `%Atelier.REST.RenderResponseBody` streams (`Write` … `%ToJSON()` … `Write`) inside its own Try/Catch and returns the failure as a status the handler drops. The known trigger was fixed at its root in this review (the `$Data=11` OREF guard), so no reachable path remains today — but the pattern is shared by every handler in `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.*` and this story enlarged the payload surface most. | Capture the status (`Set tRenderSC = ..RenderResponseBody(...)`) and, when it errors, emit a minimal pre-validated error envelope; or pre-flight `tResult.%ToJSON()` into a discard inside the Try. Apply across handlers, not just `Command.cls`. |
| 34-2-R4 | `BuildByRefNode` deep-copies with `Merge` at every level (O(nodes × depth)) and has no recursion-depth bound | MEDIUM | 34.3 | The `Merge`-into-a-fresh-local descent is a correct workaround for the hard ObjectScript constraint that a subscripted node cannot be dot-passed, but it was adopted without a budget. A large idiomatic `Output` array (thousands of nodes) or a pathological depth burns memory/wall-clock or risks `<FRAMESTACK>`. Same budget decision as 34-2-R2. | Cap total nodes and depth with an explicit indicator; consider `$Query`-based subscript-path walking to avoid repeated subtree copies. |
| 34-2-R5 | `Open tNull:::1`'s `$Test` is unchecked AND `tRedirected` is only set AFTER the `Use`, so a failed `Open`/`Use` leaks the null device (now at TWO call sites) | MEDIUM | 34.3 | Carries forward 34-1-R15 and 34-1-R8 exactly as predicted: Story 34.2 duplicated the pattern into `ClassMethod()`. The failure mode stays benign for the response itself (`Use tNull` throws while `tRedirected` is 0, so the outer Catch renders ONE clean error envelope), but the scratch device is never closed. Pre-existing in `Execute()`; not introduced here. | Track `tOpened` separately, set it immediately after the `Open`, and gate `Close tNull` on it; check `$Test` after the timed `Open` and fail with a clear message. Apply to both `Execute()` and `ClassMethod()`. |
| 34-2-R6 | Success-path I/O teardown (`ReDirectIO(0)` / `Use tInitIO` / `Close tNull`) is unguarded | LOW | 34.3 | If an R3-class target closed or re-`Open`ed the null device, `Close tNull` throws, the outer Catch fires, and a target call that SUCCEEDED is reported as an error with its output and by-ref values discarded. Narrow, and the same shape exists in `Execute()`. | Wrap the three teardown statements in `Try {} Catch {}`, exactly as the outer Catch's own cleanup block already does. |
| 34-2-R7 | `args: ""` and `args: null` bypass the R12 non-array rejection and are accepted as zero arguments | LOW | 34.3 | `tBody.%Get("args")` renders both as `""`, which `InvokeWithArgs`'s `ElseIf (pArgs '= "")` treats as "absent". Benign (a 0-arg call), and the alias preserves back-compat for callers that send an empty `args`. Fixing it requires `%GetTypeOf` at the BODY level in `Command.cls`, which the review kept out of scope to avoid Constraint C-2 churn. | Distinguish absent from present-but-empty via `tBody.%GetTypeOf("args")` in `Command.cls` before delegating, or state the alias explicitly in the AC and tool docs. |
| 34-2-R8 | `byRef:false` provides no by-value protection — every position is dot-passed regardless, so the flag only gates READ-BACK | LOW | 34.3 | Deliberate and documented in the story's Dev Notes (34.1 Finding 1/E established that unconditional dot-passing is safe), but the name invites the opposite reading: a caller writing `byRef:false` to PREVENT mutation gets no such guarantee, and a mutation that does occur is silently discarded. No code defect; a naming/documentation risk on a surface 34.3 exposes to users. | State plainly in 34.3's tool description that the flag means "report this position's post-call value", not "bind by value"; consider surfacing it as a `readBack` alias. |
| 34-2-R9 | No endpoint-level regression for `ClassMethod()`'s own response shape — all 48 tests drive `Utils.InvokeWithArgs` directly | MEDIUM | 34.3 | Nothing in the committed suite exercises the handler's redirect setup/teardown ordering, its outer-Catch cleanup block, or the assembly of `returnValue`/`argCount`/`output`/`byRefValues`/`truncated` into the envelope. Covered today only by live HTTP runs recorded in the story (dev, QA, and this review). `%UnitTest` cannot easily fabricate `%request`, so a genuine fix means an HTTP-driving smoke, which is Story 34.3's deliverable. | Add the endpoint-level assertions to 34.3's live smoke set (AC 34.3.4), including a back-compat pin that `returnValue`/`argCount` are byte-identical for a plain-scalar call. |
| 34-2-R10 | `InvokeWithArgs` runs the by-ref read-back in whatever namespace the target `ZN`'d into | LOW | 34.3 | `BuildByRefNode` and its recursion resolve `ExecuteMCPv2.Utils` in the target's post-`ZN` namespace. Safe today because the `ExecuteMCPv2` package is `%All`-mapped (Constraint C-1), which also covers namespaces created later — so this extends an already-documented dependency rather than adding a new failure class. Verified live at review (`namespace:"USER"` + `ZN`-ing target read back correctly). | Fold into 34-1-R14's missing-mapping detection: annotate a `<NOROUTINE>`/`<CLASS DOES NOT EXIST>` from this path as an infrastructure gap rather than surfacing it as the caller's error. |

## Story 34.3 — TERMINAL dispositions for the 34-2-R batch (2026-08-14)

AC 34.3.5 required item `34-2-R1` to reach a TERMINAL disposition (implement, or close-by-decision with the condition made observable — "leave it silent" was explicitly disallowed) and the remaining `34-2-R2..R10` to each receive an explicit resolve-or-re-defer disposition.

| # | Disposition | Evidence |
|---|-------------|----------|
| 34-2-R1 | **RESOLVED** | `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Command`'s `Redirects()` labels now wrap each concatenating `Set` in its own `Try/Catch`, recording a capture-buffer `<MAXSTRING>` in the process-private `%ExecuteMCPTruncated` flag instead of letting the exception propagate — so a target's OWN `Try/Catch` around `Write` can never swallow it first, and the target completes instead of aborting (exactly the ledger's suggested design). `Utils.InvokeWithArgs` ORs the flag into `pTruncated` immediately after dispatch (both the success and the exTarget-caught-MAXSTRING paths) and kills it; `Execute()` gained the identical treatment plus an ADDITIVE `truncated` field on `/command`'s response (Rule #19 — without this, fixing the shared `Redirects()` mechanism would have turned `Execute()`'s previous genuine-error behavior at the MAXSTRING boundary into a NEW silent partial, which the fix must not introduce). `TargetWriteHuge` reworked to a fixed-size-chunk repeated-write shape (never doubles a local) per the ledger's suggested resolution, since the old doubling design would, under the new mechanism, run to completion and then hit the LOCAL's own independent `<MAXSTRING>` instead of exercising capture overflow. New coverage: `TestMaxStringSwallowedByTargetTryCatchStillObservable` (the exact reported shape — `TargetWriteHugeSwallowsOwnErrors`, wraps every `Write` in the target's own swallowing `Try/Catch`; proves `truncated:true` and the target's own return value both surface) and `TestRedirectsSetsTruncatedFlagOnCaptureOverflow` (drives `Redirects()` directly, isolated from `InvokeWithArgs`). `iris_execute_tests` on `ClassMethodArgsTest` → 49/49 (was 48; Rule #35 matches the mechanical `Method Test` count) and `CommandTest` → 17/17 (was 16); Constraint C-2 re-verified (`iris_doc_list(filter="ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Command", generated=true)` → exactly one `.1.int`, no `.2.int`) and the `TestCommandCompilesToSingleGeneratedRoutine` mechanical pin stayed green throughout. `pnpm run gen:bootstrap` re-run: `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` `b514009cf654` → `b1c1415b4870` (same 28 classes, same order; `bootstrap.test.ts` 44/44). |
| 34-2-R8 | **RESOLVED** | Story 34.3's `iris_execute_classmethod` tool description now states plainly, per the ledger's suggested wording, that `byRef` "controls only whether the position's post-call value is reported back in `byRefValues` — every position is bound by reference to the target regardless of the flag, so `byRef: false` does not protect a plain scalar argument from being mutated by a target that happens to assign it; it only means that mutation is not read back." No code change (none was needed — the naming/documentation risk is the entire finding). |
| 34-2-R9 | **RESOLVED** | Added endpoint-level regression driving the REAL `/classmethod` REST handler (`Command.cls`'s `ClassMethod()` — its redirect setup/teardown ordering and its own `returnValue`/`argCount`/`output`/`byRefValues`/`truncated` envelope assembly) over real HTTP in `packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/__tests__/custom-rest.integration.test.ts`: a Rule #19 back-compat pin (`%SYSTEM.Encryption.Base64Encode`, asserts `returnValue`/`argCount` unchanged plus additive fields present-but-empty), a Write-narrating no-wrapper call, an `Output`-param byRefValues call, a 20-argument call, a `ZN`-mid-execution call, a rejected-object-arg error surface, and a second-namespace (`USER`) call — 12/12 passed live against `HSCUSTOM`/`USER` on IRIS 2026.1. This is exactly the "HTTP-driving smoke" the ledger item asked for (`%UnitTest` cannot fabricate `%request`). |
| 34-2-R2, R3, R4, R5, R6, R7, R10 | **RE-DEFERRED** (Rule #37 count **1**) | None bind Story 34.3's docs/schema/live-smoke deliverable. R2 (output/byRefValues size cap) and R4 (BuildByRefNode node/depth budget) are the SAME product decision — picking a ceiling changes the tool's advertised contract, and Story 34.3's schema work deliberately did not add one (the description documents `truncated` as the existing safety valve; a byte/node cap is new scope, not a formalization of existing behavior). R3 (RenderResponseBody status discarded) is a cross-cutting concern spanning every `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.*` handler, not just this endpoint — too broad for a docs/smoke story. R5 (Open tNull `$Test`/`tRedirected` ordering) and R6 (unguarded success-path teardown) are narrow resource-leak/robustness items, pre-existing in `Execute()`, orthogonal to the fields and docs this story delivers. R7 (`args:""`/`args:null` accepted as zero args) requires a `Command.cls`-level `%GetTypeOf` change on `args` itself — the 34.2 review deliberately kept this out to avoid Constraint C-2 churn, and this story's own `Redirects()` change already carried that risk once for `34-2-R1`; stacking a second `Command.cls`-shape change in the same story was avoided. R10 (by-ref read-back namespace) folds into `34-1-R14`'s missing-mapping detection, a separate infrastructure-gap item never owned by this story. |

**Disposition tally:** **3 resolved** (`34-2-R1`, `R8`, `R9`) · 0 closed-with-evidence · 0 closed-by-decision · **7 re-deferred** (`R2, R3, R4, R5, R6, R7, R10`). (10 items; mechanically recounted from the `#` column, Rule #51.)

## Story 34.3 code review — deferred items (2026-08-14)

Raised by the three review layers (Blind Hunter / Edge Case Hunter / Acceptance Auditor) plus lead verification. Items PATCHED during the review are recorded in the story's Review Findings section, not here. Re-deferral count 0 (first carry) for every row below.

| # | Finding | Severity | Owner | Deferral rationale | Suggested resolution |
|---|---------|----------|-------|--------------------|----------------------|
| 34-3-R1 | The `truncated` flag is computed and then discarded on every ERROR response path | MEDIUM | next epic | `InvokeWithArgs` ORs `%ExecuteMCPTruncated` into `pTruncated` and then `If $$$ISERR(tSC) Quit`s; `ClassMethod()`'s error branch renders only `SanitizeError(tSC)`, and `Execute()`'s `tCmdErrored` branch `Kill`s the flag without reading it. So a target that overflowed capture AND then failed for an unrelated reason reports the error with no truncation signal. Not treated as a silent partial (the caller receives an ERROR, not a success carrying partial output, so nothing misrepresents completeness) — but it is a real gap in the "truncation is never invisible" property. | Add `truncated` to the error envelope on both endpoints (render it alongside the sanitized error), or document explicitly that the flag is only meaningful on the success path. |
| 34-3-R2 | The unbounded ~3.6 MB success response is newly reachable on `/command` | MEDIUM | next epic | Duplicate-in-spirit of the already-re-deferred `34-2-R2`/`R4` size-cap product decision, but with a new reachability note: before Story 34.3, `Execute()` surfaced a capture `<MAXSTRING>` as a bounded ERROR; it now completes and ships the full capture through `%ToJSON` and the CSP gateway. (For `/classmethod` this is NOT new — Story 34.2 already returned success + `truncated` at the ceiling.) | Resolve together with `34-2-R2`/`R4`: one documented output ceiling with an elision marker, applied to both endpoints. |
| 34-3-R3 | Re-entrancy: invoking a target that itself drives `%UnitTest`/`ExecuteMCPv2.Tests` through the endpoint wipes the outer request's capture buffer and truncation flag | MEDIUM | next epic | `%ExecuteMCPOutput`/`%ExecuteMCPTruncated` are single process-wide slots with no save/restore or nesting depth. `ExecuteMCPv2.Tests.*` hooks `Kill` both and call `ReDirectIO(0)`, so `iris_execute_classmethod("%UnitTest.Manager","RunTest",[":ExecuteMCPv2.Tests…"])` would tear down the outer capture mid-request. Same documented-limitation family as the existing nested-`%SYS.Capture` gap (`34-2` R3 note). The epic gate deliberately targets `UtilsTest`, which has no such hooks. | Save/restore both process-private slots around the dispatch (or use a depth-suffixed slot), and document the nesting limitation in the tool description alongside the existing nested-redirect note. |
| 34-3-R4 | AC 34.3.4 (b) `Output`-param, (c) 20-arg and (d) second-namespace coverage lives only in `custom-rest.integration.test.ts`, which no gate runs | MEDIUM | next epic | `vitest.config.ts` excludes `src/**/*.integration.test.ts`, `turbo.json`'s `test` task never invokes `test:integration`, and the repo has no `.github/workflows`. The review moved the three epic-gate reproductions into the default suite, but the (b)/(c)/(d) legs remain hand-run only. Ledger item `34-2-R9` was closed on that file's evidence; the endpoint coverage is real, but nothing enforces it on every run. | Wire a `test:integration` turbo task (opt-in, `IRIS_REQUIRE_LIVE`-gated) or promote the (b)/(c)/(d) assertions into the default-suite gate file alongside the three reproductions. |
| 34-3-R5 | `toStructured()`'s array and scalar branches cannot be produced by the real `/classmethod` endpoint and are pinned by no test (Rule #54 shape) | LOW | next epic | The helper's own docblock is honest that no array-shaped response has been observed; it mirrors the established per-file pattern in `iris-data-mcp/docdb.ts`. Reverting `structuredContent: toStructured(result)` to `structuredContent: result` leaves the suite green, so the helper is defensive code with no failing witness. | Either pin both transform branches with direct unit tests on the exported helper, or drop the branches and assert the object invariant. Also decide the `toStructured(undefined)` case: it now yields `{value: undefined}` where the field was previously omitted. |
| 34-3-R6 | `Execute()` and `ClassMethod()`'s outer `Catch` blocks each `Kill` only one of the two process-private slots | LOW | next epic | `Execute()`'s catch kills `%ExecuteMCPTruncated` but not `%ExecuteMCPOutput`; `ClassMethod()`'s kills `%ExecuteMCPOutput` but not `%ExecuteMCPTruncated` (and never initializes the flag — it relies on `InvokeWithArgs`). Latent only: both endpoints re-initialize defensively at entry, so no stale value is observable today. Adjacent to the `Kill` this story added on each side. | Make both catch blocks kill both slots; initialize `%ExecuteMCPTruncated` in `ClassMethod()` for symmetry with `Execute()`. |
| 34-3-R7 | `TestTwentyArgsAllMutated`'s read-back asserts positions 0 and 19 only | LOW | next epic | A ladder just widened 10 → 20 (whose rung-10 gap was itself a Story 34.2 HIGH) is exactly where an interior-position omission hides; the endpoint-level 20-arg test asserts the same two positions. | Assert every position's read-back value in a loop. |
| 34-3-R8 | `args: z.array(z.any()).max(20)` is looser than the contract its own description states | LOW | next epic | The description forbids JSON `null`, objects and arrays as marker values, but `z.any()` accepts all of them, so client-side JSON-Schema validation cannot reject them — they fail server-side instead. The project constraint forbidding Zod `.refine()` on these schemas (JSON-Schema conversion) is why this was not tightened. | Express the union structurally (scalar union OR a marker object schema) so the generated JSON Schema carries the constraint without `.refine()`. |
| 34-3-R9 | `TestRedirectsSetsTruncatedFlagOnCaptureOverflow`'s `> 1000000` length assertion is coupled to instance long-string configuration | LOW | next epic | With long strings disabled (`MAXSTRING` 32767) the first 5000-char write overflows and the partial capture never approaches 1 MB, so the test goes red for a configuration reason with a message pointing at the wrong cause. (The review's `<MAXSTRING>` discrimination patch removes the companion concern that the flag could be set by an unrelated exception.) | Derive the floor from `$SYSTEM.SYS.MaxLocalLength()` (or equivalent) instead of hard-coding 1 MB. |
