## Terminal disposition — Story 34.5 escalated items (2026-08-15)

Closes the "in-flight, not deferred" state recorded above. Both items reached TERMINAL disposition when Story 34.5 closed (Rule SC-5 / Rule #37 — re-deferral was not an allowed outcome for these).

| Item | Disposition | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `34-4-R4` | **RESOLVED** | `IrisApiError` (`packages/shared/src/errors.ts`) gained a 5th, optional, additive `result` constructor parameter; `http-client.ts`'s two envelope-bearing throw sites now pass `envelope.result`/`envelope?.result` through. `iris_execute_command`/`iris_execute_classmethod` surface it as `structuredContent.truncated` on the error path. Verified live: a real `Set x = 1/0` call through the built dist against HSCUSTOM returned `{"structuredContent":{"truncated":false},"isError":true}` — the flag that was previously discarded now reaches the tool caller. Mutation-verified (revert `this.result = result` → 3 tests red in `errors.test.ts`/`http-client.test.ts`; revert `extractTruncated()` call → 2 tests red in `execute.test.ts`; both restored byte-identically → green). Back-compat proven mechanically: every pre-existing 3-/4-argument `IrisApiError` call site across the repo is unaffected (mechanically recounted at review: 2 non-test source files CONSTRUCT the type - `atelier.ts`, `http-client.ts` - and 52 further non-test source files reference it via catch/`instanceof`/property reads only, 54 referencing files in total), and an error with no envelope `result` gets no `structuredContent` at all (not `{truncated: undefined}`). README/changeset wording restored to the stronger claim per AC 34.5.2. |
| `34-4-R10` | **RESOLVED** | `iris_execute_tests` (`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/execute.ts`) gained a zero-result guard on the `class` and `method` levels, mirroring the pre-existing `package`-level guard's shape exactly (content-only JSON with an `error` field; no `isError`/`structuredContent`). Verified live against the real Atelier `/work` unittest endpoint on HSCUSTOM: a nonexistent class, a nonexistent method on a real class (`ExecuteMCPv2.Tests.AdvisorDataTest:NoSuchMethod34_5`), and a genuine 13-method class-level run and a genuine 1-method method-level run all behaved correctly (guard fires only on the true zero-match cases; real counts report normally otherwise). Mutation-verified (guard disabled → 3 tests red; restored byte-identically → green, 48/48). Fixture diversity per Rule #58 pinned in `execute.test.ts`: class exists/method doesn't, class doesn't exist, a genuinely-passing method, and both named `ClassName:MethodName` parse edges (missing colon, trailing empty method part). **QA follow-up fix (same story, same day, Rule #52 seam closed rather than re-deferred):** the dev's own "genuine 1-method method-level run" verification above used the Atelier endpoint's *unprefixed* form (discovered live during the dev's probe and flagged as an out-of-scope incidental finding). QA confirmed live that the endpoint's `methods` request filter — and every result row it returns, at all three levels — matches ONLY the `Test`-prefix-STRIPPED form, even though the real OS method name, this tool's own schema, and the README all use the prefixed form. Left as found, the guard just added would have turned a silent false-negative into a misleading "No tests found" for a genuine, correctly-documented `ClassName:TestMethodName` call — the exact defect this ledger item exists to close, one layer deeper. Fixed in the same file: `toAtelierMethodFilter`/`fromAtelierMethodName` strip the prefix on the way out and restore it on every result row, so the documented form now works end-to-end. Verified live (HSCUSTOM): `ExecuteMCPv2.Tests.AdvisorDataTest:TestExtractTablesSingleTable` (documented form) went from `total:0` (silent, pre-34.5) / a misleading guard error (post-guard, pre-this-fix) to `total:1, passed:1` with `details[0].method:"TestExtractTablesSingleTable"`; a class-level run's 13/13 result rows all carry the restored prefix; a genuine typo and a nonexistent class still correctly trip the guard. Mutation-verified (both helpers no-op'd → 5 tests red → restored byte-identically → 50/50 green). 3 new tests + 2 assertions added to existing tests; all pre-existing raw-Atelier-response mock fixtures in `execute.test.ts` corrected from the (unrealistic, `Test`-prefixed) mocked shape to the (real, stripped) shape per Rule #54. |

## Deferred from: code review of 34-5-tool-layer-truncated-and-test-runner-guard (2026-08-15)

Raised by the three adversarial review layers, verified by the lead, and judged
pre-existing or scope-external to Story 34.5's ACs. All are in
`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/execute.ts` unless noted.

| Item | Severity | Summary | Rationale for deferral | Suggested resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `34-5-R1` | MEDIUM | The zero-result guard is invisible to `isError` and `structuredContent` consumers: it returns content-only JSON with no `isError` and no `structuredContent`, so an agent reading `structuredContent.total` moves from `0` (pre-34.5) to `undefined`, and an agent branching on `isError` records the call as a success. | AC 34.5.3 explicitly required mirroring the pre-existing `package`-level guard's shape, which is exactly what shipped — changing it is a deliberate product decision about the tool's machine-detectability contract, not a defect fix, and it would diverge the two guard paths mid-story. Raised independently by all three review layers. | Lead decision: either set `isError: true` on the guard path, or keep the content-only shape but add `structuredContent` carrying the same `{total:0,…,error}` object so structured consumers see the error field. Apply to the package discovery-time guard at the same time so all guard paths stay identical. |
| `34-5-R2` | LOW | `target.split(":")` at `level: "method"` silently discards everything after the second segment and does not trim whitespace: `"Class:TestFoo:TestBar"` drops `TestBar`; `"Class : TestFoo"` ships a class of `" Class"` and a filter of `" TestFoo"`, which drains zero rows and now reports "No tests found" for a correctly-spelled method. Empty and leading-colon targets (`""`, `":TestFoo"`) reach the endpoint as `{class: ""}`. | Pre-existing parse behavior, untouched by this story; Story 34.5 deliberately PINNED the missing-colon and trailing-colon edges rather than changing them (Rule #58 fixture diversity), and widening target validation is a separate input-contract change. | Add a `target` format validation step for `level: "method"` (trim segments, reject more than one colon and an empty class segment) returning an explicit malformed-target error distinct from the zero-result guard. |
| `34-5-R3` | LOW | A test method named exactly `Test` is unrunnable and misreported. `toAtelierMethodFilter("Test")` returns `""`, so `methods: [""]` ships on the wire (confirmed live: matches nothing); and on the return leg the endpoint's `$EXTRACT(name,5,*)` yields `""`, which is falsy at the `if (r.method)` check, so the row is dropped from the counts and collides with the class-summary key in the poll accumulator. Net effect: the guard reports "No tests found" for a method that exists. | Requires a `%UnitTest.TestCase` method named literally `Test` — legal but vanishingly rare, and the correct fix touches the pre-existing truthiness check and accumulator key rather than this story's normalization helpers. | Change the row filter from truthiness to `r.method !== undefined`, and key the accumulator on the presence of the `method` property rather than its truthiness. |
| `34-5-R4` | LOW | Prefix aliasing is many-to-one and returned rows are never matched against the requested method: `Class:Validate` and `Class:TestValidate` both send the filter `Validate`, so a caller naming a method that does not exist can silently execute a differently-named one and get `total: 1, passed: 1`. The zero-result guard cannot see this because rows WERE returned. | Inherent to the Atelier endpoint's wire contract (it hard-prepends `Test` to the filter — `%Api.Atelier.v8.cls`), not introduced by this story; rejecting the unprefixed form would break the workaround callers may already be using. | Optionally verify at `level: "method"` that the drained rows' restored names match the requested method name, and report a mismatch explicitly. |
| `34-5-R5` | LOW | The `cycle-log-epic-34.md` `dev_complete` entry records `tests_added=18`, which is the raw added-`it(` count of the FINAL diff (including the later QA and review work) and could not have been known at dev-complete time; the story's own dev-stage figure is 14 (+8 dev / +6 shared). Rule #51. | The cycle log is the lead's artifact — the review stage is explicitly barred from writing to it. | Lead to correct the `tests_added` figure at commit time, deriving it mechanically for the dev stage only. |

A release built with IRIS unreachable passes with all six legs skipped, suite green. | AC 34.6.3 |
| 34-5-R1 | MEDIUM | The zero-result guard is invisible to `isError`/`structuredContent` consumers — AI agents are this product's audience, so the guard is partly blind to exactly the consumer class it protects. ## Terminal disposition — Story 34.6 escalated items (2026-08-15)

Closes the "in-flight, not deferred" state recorded above. All five items reached TERMINAL disposition when Story 34.6 closed (Rule SC-5 / Rule #37 — re-deferral was not an allowed outcome for these).

| Item | Disposition | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `34-2-R2` | **RESOLVED** | `ExecuteMCPv2.Utils.ApplyOutputCeiling` bounds `output` to a documented `OUTPUTCEILING` (32768 characters) on both `/command` (`Execute()`) and `/classmethod` (`ClassMethod()`), appending a structured, machine-detectable elision marker (`[IRIS-MCP-TRUNCATED ceiling=32768chars]`) rather than a bare `...`. Only ever SETS the `truncated` flag (never clears an already-true value), so it ORs cleanly with the pre-existing capture-overflow signal. Ceiling picked from live 2026-08-15 measurement (Rule #36), not by feel: the CSP Gateway/HTTP transport was found to tolerate multi-megabyte `/command` responses cleanly (tested clean to 3,000,000 characters, zero corruption/errors/timeouts), so the ceiling is explicitly documented as NOT protection against a Web Gateway timeout or the target's own execution cost (Rule #38) — it exists to keep the payload within what a real MCP client comfortably consumes: Claude Code CLI (this repo's own dev environment) was observed diverting a rendered tool result to a file once it reached 50,031 characters; 32768 stays safely under that measured boundary. Verified live via a disposable HTTP probe and permanently pinned in `%UnitTest` (`UtilsTest.TestApplyOutputCeiling*`, 6 tests) and `CommandTest.TestCommandOutputCeilingAppliedAfterCapture`. |
| `34-3-R2` | **RESOLVED** | Same fix as `34-2-R2` (resolved together, per the ledger's own suggested resolution — one ceiling, both endpoints): `Execute()`'s `/command` success path now applies `ApplyOutputCeiling` before rendering, closing the "newly reachable unbounded ~3.6 MB response" gap. Rule #19 back-compat: under-ceiling responses are byte-identical on both endpoints (mechanically proven via live HTTP + `%UnitTest`). |
| `34-4-R1` | **RESOLVED** | Epic-gate leg (d) (`execute-classmethod-epic-gate.test.ts`) and its `custom-rest.integration.test.ts` original both replaced `%SYSTEM.Version.GetVersion()` with a new fixture target, `ExecuteMCPv2.Tests.ClassMethodArgsFixture.TargetReturnNamespace` (`Quit $NAMESPACE`), asserting the returned value both equals the requested `"USER"` and differs from the configured default namespace — genuinely namespace-sensitive (Rules #34/#40). Mutation-verified (Rule #48): omitting the `namespace` argument drove the gate leg RED (`expected 'HSCUSTOM' to be 'USER'`); restored byte-identical (md5sum-confirmed) and re-run GREEN. |
| `34-4-R3` | **RESOLVED** | `fixtureIsDeployed`'s catch block now re-throws anything that is not an `IrisApiError` with `statusCode === 404` (the same discriminator this repo already uses for the identical existence check in `doc.ts`'s `iris_doc_get` and every package's `integration-setup.ts`), so `beforeAll` reports the real cause instead of a misleading "fixtures not deployed". Mutation-verified: reverting to the old catch-all drove both new RE-THROWS discrimination tests RED; restored byte-identical, re-run GREEN. `IRIS_REQUIRE_LIVE=1` is now armed on `@iris-mcp/dev`'s own `prepublishOnly` npm/pnpm lifecycle script (`scripts/prepublish-gate.mjs`) — mechanically confirmed to be the packaging enforcement surface via `pnpm publish --dry-run --no-git-checks`: with IRIS reachable the hook runs the gate live and the dry-run proceeds to packing; with a simulated credential fault (`IRIS_PASSWORD` overridden) the hook fails, `pnpm publish` itself exits 1 with `ELIFECYCLE Command failed`, and NO tarball is ever produced (confirmed absent from the dry-run output) — the release genuinely fails closed before packaging, not just before upload. |
| `34-5-R1` | **RESOLVED** | The zero-result guard (`packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/tools/execute.ts`) now carries both `isError: true` and `structuredContent` (the same `{total,passed,failed,skipped,details,error}` object as the text content) via one shared `zeroResultGuardResponse()` helper, applied identically to both guard call sites (the package discovery-time check and the shared post-run check covering all three levels) so they cannot diverge. Recorded Lead decision honored verbatim (both halves, not an either/or). All pre-existing guard-shape tests updated to assert the new envelope; back-compat preserved for the message TEXT itself (unchanged wording). |

**Disposition tally:** **5 resolved** (all of `34-2-R2`, `34-3-R2`, `34-4-R1`, `34-4-R3`, `34-5-R1`) · 0 closed-with-evidence · 0 closed-by-decision. (5 items; mechanically recounted from the `#` column, Rule #51.)

## QA-pass findings — Story 34.6 (2026-08-15, bmad-qa-generate-e2e-tests)

Two additional items found and resolved during the Story 34.6 QA pass, neither part of the five items escalated above. Both fixed within the QA pass (not deferred); recorded here per Rule #48 (mutation/live evidence) and so neither is later mistaken for an outstanding gap from a prose-only mention. Full detail in the story file's own QA Results section.

| Item | Severity | Finding | Disposition | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `34-6-QA-1` | HIGH | AC 34.6.3's `prepublishOnly` gate was wired only into `@iris-mcp/dev`. `.changeset/config.json` groups every `@iris-mcp/*` package as `"fixed"`, so a real release always version-bumps and publishes `@iris-mcp/shared`, `@iris-mcp/admin`, `@iris-mcp/data`, `@iris-mcp/interop`, `@iris-mcp/ops`, and `@iris-mcp/all` alongside `@iris-mcp/dev` — none of which had any live-IRIS check. Confirmed live (dry-run only, no registry write): `pnpm -r publish` processes packages in dependency-topological order, so `@iris-mcp/shared` (a dependency of every other package) packaged and "published" COMPLETELY before `@iris-mcp/dev`'s gate ever ran and correctly failed closed on a simulated credential fault — in a real release `@iris-mcp/shared` would already be on the registry by the time that failure is observed. | **RESOLVED** | New `scripts/verify-iris-reachable.mjs` (a minimal, unconditional IRIS-reachability check — not a substitute for `@iris-mcp/dev`'s own deeper epic-done regression suite) wired as `prepublishOnly` on `@iris-mcp/shared`, `@iris-mcp/admin`, `@iris-mcp/data`, `@iris-mcp/interop`, `@iris-mcp/ops`, and `@iris-mcp/all`. Deliberately NOT wired into `@iris-mcp/client-config` (no IRIS dependency at all — a local client-config-file manager). Mutation-verified: the identical bad-credential recursive dry-run that previously produced a complete `@iris-mcp/shared` tarball now produces zero tarballs (grep-confirmed) before `@iris-mcp/dev`'s own gate is even reached; a good-credential recursive run still publishes both packages cleanly (2 tarballs). Spot-checked independently on `@iris-mcp/ops`. |
| `34-6-QA-2` | LOW | `packages/iris-dev-mcp/src/__tests__/tools.integration.test.ts`'s `"iris_doc_list with category=CLS includes the test class"` assertion checks only page 1 of an UNFILTERED `iris_doc_list` call (`{category:"CLS"}`, no `filter`) for the test's own created class — `iris_doc_list`'s own tool description documents exactly this footgun ("WARNING: Without a filter, this returns ALL documents including system classes"). As the namespace's system-class count grew, the created class (alphabetically late) was pushed past the first (paginated) page, failing the assertion. Confirmed via `git stash` to reproduce identically on the unmodified pre-Story-34.6 baseline — genuinely pre-existing, unrelated to this story's changes. Not part of any required gate (`*.integration.test.ts` is excluded from `vitest.config.ts`'s default include glob and never invoked by `pnpm turbo run test`), so it was previously mentioned only in Dev Agent Record prose rather than fixed or ledgered — the exact "normalizing known test failures" anti-pattern this note exists to close. | **RESOLVED** | Added `filter: "Test.MCPIntegration"` to the `docListTool.handler` call, matching the tool's own documented usage guidance and scoping the assertion to what it actually means to test. Verified: 19/19 tests pass (was 18/19). |

## Deferred from: code review of 34-6-pre-publish-release-hardening (2026-08-15)

The 16 deferred items from this review are recorded in full — with severity, rationale and suggested resolution — in the table below (`34-6-CR-5` through `34-6-CR-20`). They are not duplicated here; this heading exists so the standard "Deferred from: code review" lookup finds them.

## Code-review findings — Story 34.6 (2026-08-15, bmad-code-review, 3 adversarial layers)

Three-layer review (Blind Hunter · Edge Case Hunter · Acceptance Auditor) of the pre-publish hardening story, run as a **release gate**. All three layers DELIVERED an explicit findings payload within the Rule #57 20-minute bound, so the close is **NORMAL, not degraded** (`failed_layers` empty). Every item below was independently verified live by the reviewing lead against real IRIS (2026.1 Build 235U, `HSCUSTOM`/`USER`) — dry-run publishes only, no registry write; all live mutations restored byte-identically (`md5sum`-confirmed) and recompiled.

### Resolved during the review

| Item | Severity | Finding | Disposition | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `34-6-CR-1` | HIGH | **`@iris-mcp/client-config` was publishable, ungated, and FIRST in publish order** — the exact partial-release failure mode `34-6-QA-1` was raised to close, reproduced one package over. It matches `.changeset/config.json`'s `"fixed": [["@iris-mcp/*"]]` lockstep group (so a release always bumps and publishes it) and has NO workspace dependencies, so `pnpm -r publish` processes it before every gated package. QA's exclusion rationale ("no IRIS dependency, gating it would be a mismatched requirement") answers a different question than the gate exists to answer: not "does this package need IRIS?" but "may it land on the registry when its lockstep siblings cannot?". npm versions are immutable, so a partial publish of a version-locked group cannot be undone. | **RESOLVED** | Reproduced live with a deliberately wrong `IRIS_PASSWORD`: `pnpm -r publish --dry-run --no-git-checks` produced a **complete 278.3 kB `@iris-mcp/client-config@0.0.1` tarball** and reported `+ @iris-mcp/client-config@0.0.1` with **zero gate output**, and only THEN did `@iris-mcp/shared`'s gate fail closed. Fixed by wiring `scripts/verify-iris-reachable.mjs` as `prepublishOnly` on `packages/client-config/package.json`. Mutation-verified: the identical bad-credential run now produces **0 tarballs** (was 1); a good-credential run still publishes all 8 packages cleanly, exit 0. |
| `34-6-CR-2` | HIGH | **The story's headline feature had ZERO regression coverage at its integration point.** Every ceiling test either called `ExecuteMCPv2.Utils.ApplyOutputCeiling` directly or re-implemented `Execute()`'s sequence ("Mirror Execute()'s post-capture ceiling application exactly"), and the TS tests fed a MOCKED over-ceiling envelope. The two lines that actually ship the feature — the `ApplyOutputCeiling` calls in `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Command`'s `Execute()` and `ClassMethod()` — were asserted by nothing, so deleting the feature left every suite green. | **RESOLVED** | Proven by mutation: both calls deleted, class recompiled → live `/command` returned **50,000 untruncated characters** with `truncated:false`, while `ExecuteMCPv2.Tests.CommandTest` (18/18), the full `@iris-mcp/dev` vitest suite (**643/643**) and `custom-rest.integration.test.ts` (12/12) all stayed **GREEN**. Fixed by adding **leg (e)** to `execute-classmethod-epic-gate.test.ts` — drives BOTH deployed endpoints through the real tool handlers and asserts over-ceiling (length exactly 32768 + marker + `truncated:true`) and under-ceiling byte-identity (AC 34.6.5 / Rule #19) on each. Placed in that file deliberately: it is in the DEFAULT suite *and* is the file `scripts/prepublish-gate.mjs` runs with `IRIS_REQUIRE_LIVE=1`, so the pin is armed on the packaging path. Mutation-verified: with the wiring deleted the new leg goes **RED** (`expected 50000 to be 32768`); restored byte-identical (`feb4a1c99480b404ab3ad58b2e8df2f6`), recompiled, 10/10 green. |
| `34-6-CR-3` | MEDIUM | **Epic-gate leg (d) had two false-RED modes that would BLOCK the release**, now that `prepublishOnly` runs that file. (a) The second namespace was hard-coded to `"USER"` while the assertion also required `!== configuredNamespace` — but `packages/iris-dev-mcp/README.md:33` documents `IRIS_NAMESPACE` default as `USER` and all three example client configs set it, making the two assertions mutually unsatisfiable on a perfectly working system. (b) The leg called `ExecuteMCPv2.Tests.ClassMethodArgsFixture` in a namespace the `beforeAll` fixture probe never checks; the fixture is excluded from the bootstrap manifest (Rule #39) and here resolves in `USER` only via this instance's DB mapping (`db: HSCUSTOM`), so a clean instance loaded per the repo's own instructions would go RED as "fixtures missing" — the exact misdiagnosis AC 34.6.3 exists to eliminate. | **RESOLVED** | (a) reproduced live: `IRIS_NAMESPACE=USER … vitest run execute-classmethod-epic-gate.test.ts` → **`expected 'USER' not to be 'USER'`**. Fixed in BOTH files (gate + `custom-rest.integration.test.ts` original, per AC 34.6.2's "both" requirement): the second namespace is now DERIVED (`configured === "USER" ? "%SYS" : "USER"`) and the target is `%SYSTEM.SYS.NameSpace`, a system class present in every namespace (verified live returning `"USER"`/`"%SYS"`/`"HSCUSTOM"`), removing the fixture precondition entirely. AC 34.6.2's mutation property is **preserved and strengthened** — dropping `namespace` is now RED under BOTH configurations (`expected 'HSCUSTOM' to be 'USER'` and `expected 'USER' to be '%SYS'`), where previously it was meaningless under the `USER` config because the leg was permanently RED there. |
| `34-6-CR-4` | LOW | **Two TS test fixtures encoded shapes the real server cannot produce (Rule #54).** `execute.test.ts`'s `overCeilingOutput` used `"A".repeat(32728)` + a 41-char marker = 32769 characters, one over a ceiling the server can never exceed; and its byRef fixture used `"B".repeat(20)` alongside a `ceiling=20` marker, but at a remaining budget of 20 the real code computes `tKeep = 20 - 38 < 0 → 0` and emits the **marker alone**. Both encoded the same wrong mental model ("content is retained under small ceilings") that produces `34-6-CR-8`. | **RESOLVED** | Marker length pinned live as `36 + digits(ceiling)` (41 at the default ceiling; ceiling=20 → 38 chars, marker only). Fixtures corrected to the real shapes, with the ceiling arithmetic derived in-test and a `expect(overCeilingOutput.length).toBe(CEILING)` self-check so the fixture can no longer drift off the server's real invariant. |

| Item | Severity | Finding | Rationale for deferring | Suggested resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `34-6-CR-5` | **HIGH** | **`returnValue` is completely unbounded on `/classmethod`** — the ceiling covers `output` (both endpoints) and `byRefValues` leaf values, but not the primary payload field. Verified live: a call returning a 1,000,000-character string shipped a **1,000,166-byte HTTP body with `truncated:false`** and no flag of any kind; the TS layer is pure pass-through. This defeats the story's own stated purpose ("the first public release cannot ship an unbounded payload") and contradicts the `Utils.cls` banner's claim that the ceiling "exists purely to keep the RESPONSE PAYLOAD a bounded, genuinely consumable size". Trivially reachable — any method returning a file read, a serialized JSON blob, or a report. | The ledger items this story closed (`34-2-R2`/`34-3-R2`) named `output` and `byRefValues` only, so AC 34.6.1 was satisfied literally. Fixing it is a public-contract change (a new response field + descriptions ×2 + README ×2 + CHANGELOG + changeset + `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` regen) and a product decision the story reserves to the Project Lead. **Escalated in the review report as a publish precondition**, because applying a cap AFTER publish is a breaking change — this is the last moment it is free. | Apply `ApplyOutputCeiling` to `tReturnStr` in `ClassMethod()` and surface a `returnValueTruncated` boolean, symmetric with `truncated`/`byRefTruncated` (the story's own established "one flag per response field" pattern). Document at all five ceiling-adjacent doc sites and extend gate leg (e) with a returning-target case. |
| `34-6-CR-6` | **HIGH** | **byRef byte-budget exhaustion DESTROYS every subsequent position's small value and replaces it with a LARGER marker.** `InvokeWithArgs`' 20 top-level `BuildByRefNode` calls are unconditional — unlike the recursion's child loop, there is no `pByteBudget <= 0` check between positions. So after one large `Output` value exhausts the shared budget, every later position's value is replaced by a ~37-char elision marker. Verified live with `Target20`: position 0 = 32768 chars, positions 1–3 = 37-char `ceiling=0chars` markers that **destroyed** the real values `"42-m"`, `"hello-m"`, `"x-m"`. Total `byRefValues` content = **33,471 chars, exceeding the documented 32,768 budget** — the response GREW while losing data. `truncated` stays `false`, so an `output`-focused consumer sees nothing wrong, and `byRefTruncated` does not say which positions were lost. | Ordinary, realistic call shape (one large report `Output` plus several small status/count `Output` params) silently loses data — but the fix is ObjectScript in `ExecuteMCPv2.Utils`, triggering a `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` regen and a re-verification cascade this review should not perform unilaterally on the eve of publish. Not covered by any test: `TestByRefValueByteBudgetTruncatesHugeLeaf` uses a SINGLE byRef position, so it only ever exercises the first-leaf case at full budget. | Guard the 20 top-level calls with the same budget check the child loop uses, and prefer OMITTING an over-budget position (marking it in `byRefTruncated`) over replacing real data with a larger marker. Add a test with TWO+ marked positions — the "budget shared across every marked position" property currently has no coverage at all (see `34-6-CR-17`). |
| `34-6-CR-7` | **HIGH** | **Non-ASCII JSON request bodies are silently mis-decoded across all 14 REST handler classes** that share `ExecuteMCPv2.Utils.ReadRequestBody` (46 call sites, both counts mechanically derived per Rule #51). UTF-8 is read as Latin-1 on the way IN: verified live that `é` (U+00E9) returns as 2 characters with code points `[195,169]`, `世界` as 6 code points `[228,184,150,231,149,140]`, and `😀` as 4 code points `[240,159,152,128]` — in every case the raw UTF-8 bytes. Server-ORIGINATED Unicode round-trips OUT correctly (control: `TargetWriteMetachars` returns `héllo-世界-😀` intact), so the defect is strictly on the request-body input path. Silent — HTTP 200, no error. Affects `iris_execute_command`'s `command`, `iris_execute_classmethod`'s `args`, and `iris_global_set`'s `value`, so non-ASCII data written through these tools is **corrupted at rest in the database**. | Pre-existing across every epic and untouched by Story 34.6 (Rule #52 scope discipline); a correct fix requires researching IRIS CSP request-body charset negotiation, with its own risk of breaking the currently-working ASCII path. **Recorded here because it was previously captured ONLY in story prose** — the documented "deferred findings only in story files" anti-pattern, which would have made it invisible at Epic 35's retro-review triage gate. In the same QA pass, the smaller `34-6-QA-2` was ledgered precisely for that reason while this larger, user-facing finding was not. | Investigate `%request.CharSet` / the CSP request-body character-encoding negotiation for these `%CSP.REST` handlers and fix centrally in `ReadRequestBody` (one fix covers all 14 handlers). Pin with a round-trip test per Rule #58 fixture diversity (BMP accented, CJK, astral/surrogate-pair) submitted through the request body, not just server-originated literals. **Until fixed, document the limitation prominently in the user-facing READMEs** — shipping silent data corruption undocumented is the unacceptable option (see the review report's explicit ship/no-ship call). |
| `34-6-CR-8` | MEDIUM | **`ApplyOutputCeiling` returns a string LONGER than the ceiling it was given, for every ceiling in 0..37**, contradicting its own banner ("The returned string's length never exceeds `OUTPUTCEILING`"). The marker is `36 + digits(ceiling)` characters, so `tKeep` clamps to 0 and the marker alone is returned. Verified live through the ordinary `/classmethod` path at ceilings 0, 5, 20, 36 and 37 (e.g. ceiling=5 → 37 chars returned). Reachable in production because `BuildByRefNode` passes the live REMAINING budget as the ceiling on every leaf. | Bounded overshoot (~740 chars worst case across 20 positions), no data-integrity impact beyond `34-6-CR-6`, and the fix touches `ExecuteMCPv2.Utils` → `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` regen. | Either honor small ceilings (return a bare truncation with no marker, or the first `tCeiling` characters) or correct the banner and the tool descriptions to state the real invariant. Rule #58: the existing tests deliberately step around the broken band — `TestApplyOutputCeilingCustomSmallerCeiling` uses 100 with an explicit comment choosing it over "a tiny value like 5", and no test asserts `$Length(result) <= ceiling` anywhere in 1..37. |
| `34-6-CR-9` | MEDIUM | **The advertised elision-marker literal never appears for any truncated byRef leaf.** The marker embeds the EFFECTIVE ceiling (`ceiling=0chars`, `ceiling=26chars`, …), but `execute.ts` ×2, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` and the changeset all advertise the single literal `[IRIS-MCP-TRUNCATED ceiling=32768chars]` as *the* machine-detectable marker, and the classmethod description says byRef values are "cut short at **the same** elision marker". A consumer implementing the documented detection — which AC 34.6.1's "machine-detectable, not a bare `...`" contract invites — finds zero matches in truncated byRef positions. | Documentation/contract accuracy rather than a runtime fault; the fix spans the `Utils.cls` banner (bootstrap regen) plus four TS/markdown surfaces and is best done alongside `34-6-CR-6`/`34-6-CR-8`, which change the same behavior. | Document the stable PREFIX `[IRIS-MCP-TRUNCATED` as the detection token at every doc site (and state that the `ceiling=<N>chars` suffix varies with the effective budget), or make the marker constant. |
| `34-6-CR-10` | MEDIUM | **A truncation cut through a surrogate pair emits invalid UTF-8 on the wire.** `$Extract` cuts at a UTF-16 code-unit index with no surrogate-boundary guard. Verified live: with the cut landing on a high surrogate, the raw HTTP body is **not valid UTF-8** (IRIS emits the lone `D83D` as WTF-8 `ED A0 BD`); Node's lenient decoder substitutes 3 × U+FFFD, making the client-visible string **32,770 characters** and breaking the "capped at 32768 characters" claim. `JSON.parse` survives, but a strict decoder (`TextDecoder({fatal:true})`, Go/Rust/Python strict, a validating proxy) errors. Story 34.6 introduces this — before the ceiling, `output` passed through verbatim. | Not the epic's original corrupt-envelope class (the envelope still parses in Node), and the fix touches `ExecuteMCPv2.Utils` → bootstrap regen. Notably, QA's pinning test gives false assurance here: `TestApplyOutputCeilingMidSurrogatePairStaysValidJSON` validates `%ToJSON` → `%FromJSON` **inside IRIS**, a closed loop over IRIS's own WTF-8 that is structurally blind to the wire encoding and to the real JS consumer (Rule #36 oracle gap). | Back the cut off by one code unit when `$Extract`'s last retained character is a high surrogate (`$ZHEX`/`$Ascii` range `55296..56319`). Re-pin with an oracle on the CLIENT side — assert the raw response body is valid UTF-8 and that `JSON.parse`'s output length equals the advertised ceiling — rather than round-tripping inside IRIS. |
| `34-6-CR-11` | MEDIUM | **No prepublish gate verifies that the package being published was actually built.** `prepublishOnly` runs BEFORE `prepack`; `verify-iris-reachable.mjs` imports only `@iris-mcp/shared`'s dist (never the caller's own), and `prepublish-gate.mjs` runs vitest over TypeScript SOURCE. Mechanically confirmed: no package in the workspace declares a `prepare` or `prepack` script, and all publishable server packages declare `files: ["dist"]`. So with a stale or missing `dist`, both gates pass green and npm packs the tarball without erroring. | Distinct from the IRIS-reachability property the gates were written for, and closing it is a design addition (build-freshness verification) rather than a review patch. For a first public publish the consequence is severe — an empty or stale tarball on the registry cannot be un-published. | Add a `prepack` script running the package's own build, or have each gate assert its own `dist/index.js` exists and is newer than `src/`. Rule #22's "smoke the BUILT dist as a real consumer would" is currently inverted at the one moment it matters most. |
| `34-6-CR-12` | MEDIUM | **Per-package `prepublishOnly` hooks structurally cannot gate a multi-package release.** Publish order established live: `client-config` → `shared` → `admin` → `data` → **`dev`** → `interop` → `ops` → `all`. `@iris-mcp/dev` carries the ONLY gate that can detect an actual `iris_execute_classmethod` regression, and it runs FIFTH — so if that gate fails for the reason it exists (a real regression, IRIS being up), four packages are already on the registry. `34-6-QA-1` and `34-6-CR-1` narrow this to the "IRIS is down" trigger only; the regression trigger remains. The changeset's claim that "the release aborts before packaging even begins" is true only for the specific package whose own gate fires. | Fixing it properly means introducing a release entry point and changing the documented release procedure — a Project Lead decision about how the first publish is actually run, not a review patch. | Add a root-level pre-flight release script that runs the full gate set ONCE (reachability + the epic-done gate) and only then invokes `pnpm -r publish`, and document it as the sole supported release command. Per-package hooks stay as defense-in-depth. |
| `34-6-CR-13` | MEDIUM | **`verify-iris-reachable.mjs` silently falls back to hard-coded local dev credentials** (`localhost:52773`, `_SYSTEM`/`SYS`, `HSCUSTOM`) when the corresponding env vars are unset, so on a maintainer's own machine — where a local IRIS is always up — the gate passes automatically regardless of the intended release target, degrading to a rubber stamp. Its banner calls it "a minimal, unconditional, always-required reachability check", and it cites `loadConfig()`'s throw-on-missing-credentials as a design virtue while defeating it one line later. | Fail-closed direction is preserved (verified live: no path exits 0 with IRIS unreachable — unreachable port and unresolvable host both exit 1 cleanly), so this is a gate-strength issue, not a safety hole. | Require `IRIS_HOST`/`IRIS_USERNAME`/`IRIS_PASSWORD` to be explicitly set at the publish boundary and fail closed when they are not, rather than defaulting to a dev instance. Also note the reachability probe (`HEAD /api/atelier/`) only proves whatever auth that web application enforces, so the "credentials are wrong" claim is weaker than stated. |

The repo holds **9** non-private publishable manifests, not the 8 the story's enumeration considered. `pnpm-workspace.yaml` globs only `packages/*`, so `pnpm -r publish` cannot reach it. | Realistic trigger is a manual `npm publish` from that directory, not the release command in use; and `private: true` interacts with the VS Code `vsce` packaging path in ways not verified live, so patching it blind would violate the same Rule #54 discipline this review is enforcing. | Add `"private": true` to `extensions/iris-mcp-launcher/package.json` after confirming `vsce package`/`vsce publish` still work (they do not use `npm publish`), and replace the `TBD-at-publish-time` publisher placeholder before any marketplace release. |

Reliably non-zero, so `pnpm` still aborts the publish. | Documented as benign teardown noise (Rule #22). | The residual risk is not cosmetic: `abort()` (not `exit()`) can trigger Windows Error Reporting or a configured `AeDebug` JIT debugger, which would hang the publish on a modal dialog rather than returning any code. ## Terminal disposition — Story 34.6 review-continuation items (2026-08-15)

The review returned Story 34.6 to `in-progress` over its 3 open HIGH findings (`34-6-CR-5`, `34-6-CR-6`, `34-6-CR-7`); the other 13 deferred MEDIUM/LOW items (`34-6-CR-8` through `-20`) were explicitly OUT of this pass's scope (Rule #52) and stay open at Rule #37 re-deferral count 0. All three HIGHs reach TERMINAL disposition here.

| Item | Disposition | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `34-6-CR-5` | **RESOLVED** | `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Command`'s `ClassMethod()` now applies `ApplyOutputCeiling` to `returnValue` — the primary payload field — with the same documented ceiling/marker as `output`, surfaced via a new additive `returnValueTruncated` boolean (present, defaulted false, on both error envelopes too, for shape symmetry with `truncated`/`byRefTruncated`). Pinned at the REAL deployed `/classmethod` endpoint via a new leg in `execute-classmethod-epic-gate.test.ts` (not a re-implementation of the handler's own sequence — the exact gap the review's own CR-2 finding closed for `output`). Mutation-verified: reverting the `ApplyOutputCeiling` call on `tReturnStr` turns the new leg RED (`returnValue` length exceeds 32768); restored byte-identically and recompiled, green. Rule #19 back-compat: an under-ceiling `returnValue` is byte-identical, and `returnValueTruncated` is additive/absent from a legacy envelope. |
| `34-6-CR-6` | **RESOLVED** | Two-part ObjectScript fix in `ExecuteMCPv2.Utils`, both necessary: (1) `ApplyOutputCeiling` now falls back to a plain, marker-less hard cut whenever the elision marker itself would not fit in the remaining ceiling — a result is now PROVABLY never longer than the ceiling it was given, nor longer than the original value it replaces, for every ceiling from 0 upward (pinned by a new sweeping `%UnitTest`, `TestApplyOutputCeilingNeverExceedsCeilingOrOriginalLength`). (2) A new `AddByRefPosition` helper wraps all 20 top-level `byRefValues` positions with the SAME budget pre-check the recursive subscript walk already had, OMITTING a position from `byRefValues` entirely (never as a misleading empty-string placeholder) once the shared budget is already exhausted. Reproduced the exact code-review scenario (`Target20`, one huge value then `"42"`/`"hello"`/`"x"`) at both the `%UnitTest` level and the REAL deployed `/classmethod` endpoint (a new leg in `execute-classmethod-epic-gate.test.ts`): positions 1-3 are now omitted, not destroyed, and the total content across `byRefValues` never exceeds 32768. Mutation-verified independently: reverting the `ApplyOutputCeiling` marker-vs-ceiling guard alone reproduces the original overshoot; reverting `AddByRefPosition` alone (falling back to unconditional `BuildByRefNode` calls) restores the pre-fix omission gap; both restored byte-identically and recompiled, green. |
| `34-6-CR-7` | **CLOSED-BY-DECISION** | Per the code review's own explicit instruction, NOT code-fixed in this pass — pre-existing, cross-cutting across 14 REST handler classes / 46 call sites spanning all five servers, genuinely out of this story's scope (Rule #52). Instead documented prominently rather than shipped silently: a new "Known Limitations" section in the suite root `README.md` (full detail: affected fields, the Latin-1-read-as-UTF-8 mechanism, the at-rest data-corruption consequence for `iris_global_set`, the workaround) and a matching, cross-referenced note in `packages/iris-dev-mcp/README.md` (the package owning the three concretely-named affected tools). Both reference this ledger item by ID. This is a decision to close via documentation, not a code resolution — the underlying defect remains open as a future investigation (IRIS CSP request-body charset negotiation). |

**Disposition tally:** **2 resolved** (`34-6-CR-5`, `34-6-CR-6`) · 0 closed-with-evidence · **1 closed-by-decision** (`34-6-CR-7`). (3 items; mechanically recounted from the Disposition column, Rule #51.)

## Code-review findings — Story 34.6, re-review pass (cycle_iteration=2, 2026-08-15)

Re-review of the review-continuation pass, run as a RELEASE GATE ahead of the suite's first npm publish. All three adversarial layers (Blind Hunter · Edge Case Hunter · Acceptance Auditor) DELIVERED an explicit findings payload against a frozen diff snapshot, inside the Rule #57 20-minute hard bound. **`failed_layers` is empty — this close is NORMAL, not degraded.**

**Verification of the three HIGH closures (own evidence, Rule #48 — not the dev's report).** Each mutation was applied to the working tree, uploaded AND recompiled via `iris_doc_load` (never `iris_doc_compile` alone, which would leave the mutated server copy in place), then restored from a byte-exact scratchpad snapshot and re-uploaded:

- `34-6-CR-5` — removed the `ApplyOutputCeiling` call on `tReturnStr` → gate leg (e-2) RED at the REAL deployed `/classmethod` endpoint (`expected 50000 to be 32768`). Restored (`cb9cc4904448d5600ef725e32937a4f1`), recompiled, green. **Closure CONFIRMED.**
- `34-6-CR-6` part 1 — reverted `ApplyOutputCeiling`'s marker-vs-ceiling guard → RED at the unit level (`UtilsTest.TestApplyOutputCeilingNeverExceedsCeilingOrOriginalLength`, 38 assertions across ceilings 0–37) AND at the endpoint (leg (e-4): `expected 38 to be 20`); leg (e-3) stayed green, independently confirming the two legs isolate different halves. **Closure CONFIRMED.**
- `34-6-CR-6` part 2 — reverted `AddByRefPosition`'s budget pre-check → RED at the unit level (`TestByRefValueBudgetExhaustionOmitsRatherThanDestroys`, all 3 omission assertions) AND at the endpoint (leg (e-3): `expected true to be false`); leg (e-4) stayed green. Restored (`21c4d88beb0bf2f6b4ca54183d715080`), recompiled, green. **Closure CONFIRMED.**
- `34-6-CR-7` — documentation verified in place at both READMEs; the `../../README.md#known-limitations` anchor resolves to a real `## Known Limitations` heading. **Closure CONFIRMED as closed-by-decision.**

Prior-fix non-regression re-verified: gate 13/13 green under BOTH the default namespace and `IRIS_NAMESPACE=USER` (the previously release-blocking false-RED configuration); all 8 workspace manifests including `@iris-mcp/client-config` carry a `prepublishOnly` gate.

### Patched during this review (applied and verified)

| Item | Severity | Finding | Disposition | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `34-6-CR2-1` | **HIGH** | **The `byRefValues` "32768-character budget" did not bound `byRefValues`.** The shared byte budget was decremented only by each leaf VALUE's length — subscript KEYS and `<Object:...>` OREF placeholders were emitted entirely free. Found independently by all three layers and by the reviewing lead's own live probes. Measured live on the deployed code: 400 positions keyed by 900-character subscripts emitted **363,908 characters** with 32,368 of the budget still reported unspent and `byRefTruncated` still **false**; 20 positions keyed by 30,000-character subscripts emitted **600,187 characters**; 400 OREF leaves emitted ~16,000 characters without touching the budget at all. A realistic file-path-keyed array (1200 keys, ~70 chars each) emitted 92,067 characters, 2.8x the stated budget. A long-keyed `Output` array is an entirely idiomatic ObjectScript shape, so ordinary callers reached this — the same unbounded-payload class as `34-6-CR-5`, one field over, on the field whose bounding is AC 34.6.1's headline. | **RESOLVED** | `BuildByRefNode` now charges every emitted piece of content: the OREF placeholder, and each subscript key (charged BEFORE descending, with a short-circuit when the key alone would consume the remainder, so `pByteBudget` stays >= 1 at every recursive entry and `ApplyOutputCeiling` is never called with a zero ceiling). The 363,908-character case now emits **32,797** characters with `byRefTruncated: true`; the 600,187-character case emits 30,051. Pinned by two new `%UnitTest` methods (`TestByRefKeysAreChargedAgainstTheByteBudget`, `TestOrefPlaceholdersAreChargedAgainstTheByteBudget`), both deliberately keeping every VALUE tiny so only key/placeholder charging can bound the result. Mutation-verified: reverting the key charge turns the new test RED reproducing the exact `363908` figure. Per-node JSON scaffolding (~25-30 chars/node) remains uncharged and is bounded by `BYREFNODECEILING` instead — now stated explicitly at the class banner, both tool descriptions, the README, CHANGELOG and changeset rather than implied away. |
| `34-6-CR2-2` | MEDIUM | **The published byRef elision contract was a false dichotomy.** The `iris_execute_classmethod` tool description and `packages/iris-dev-mcp/README.md` both stated a value that no longer fits is "either cut short with an elision marker … when there is room for the marker itself, or OMITTED from `byRefValues` entirely when there is not". The code has a third outcome: at a positive remaining budget smaller than the ~40-character marker it emits a plain UNMARKED prefix — and the story's own new gate leg (e-4) pins exactly that (`expect(pos1).not.toContain("[IRIS-MCP-TRUNCATED")`). Omission happens only when the budget was already exhausted BEFORE the position. So the shipped description and the shipped test asserted different contracts, and a consumer implementing the documented marker detection would read an unmarked truncated value as complete. | **RESOLVED** | Both surfaces now describe all three outcomes explicitly (omitted / marker / unmarked prefix), and both state that in the unmarked case `byRefTruncated` is the ONLY signal — "the absence of a marker is not proof a byRef value is complete". |
| `34-6-CR2-3` | MEDIUM | **Two shipping artifacts still asserted the `@iris-mcp/client-config` exclusion that `34-6-CR-1` had already reversed.** `scripts/verify-iris-reachable.mjs`'s own banner and the changeset's final paragraph both read "Deliberately NOT wired into `@iris-mcp/client-config`", while `packages/client-config/package.json` in the same working tree carries the gate. The changeset becomes the public release notes, and the script is what a blocked maintainer would read first. The changeset frontmatter also listed 7 packages, omitting `@iris-mcp/client-config` whose manifest changed. | **RESOLVED** | Both texts rewritten to state the wiring AND the reason it was reversed (the `"fixed"` changeset group publishes all `@iris-mcp/*` as one event and `client-config` sorts first in dependency-topological order, so ungated it was the one package that could reach the registry before any gate fired). `@iris-mcp/client-config` added to the changeset frontmatter. |
| `34-6-CR2-4` | MEDIUM | **Ledger row `34-6-CR-8` was STALE-OPEN.** Its finding — "`ApplyOutputCeiling` returns a string LONGER than the ceiling it was given, for every ceiling in 0..37" — was fully fixed by the `34-6-CR-6` part-1 rework, which implemented CR-8's own suggested resolution verbatim ("return the first `tCeiling` characters"), and its test-coverage sub-claim was closed by `TestApplyOutputCeilingNeverExceedsCeilingOrOriginalLength`. Verified live across ceilings 0, 1, 5, 20, 36, 37, 38, 40, 41, 42, 60: the result is `<= ceiling` and `<= $Length(original)` at every one. It nevertheless sat in "Deferred — open" as a MEDIUM and was counted in the post-pass "11 MEDIUM / 61 open" tally — a resolved defect recorded as an outstanding release risk, and a Rule #51 tally defect. | **RESOLVED** | Row superseded below; tally corrected in this section's closing count. |
| `34-6-CR2-5` | MEDIUM | **`TestByRefValueBudgetExhaustionOmitsRatherThanDestroys`'s "total across ALL surviving positions" assertion measured exactly one position** and was a strictly weaker duplicate of the `AssertEquals` two lines above it — it could not fail. A regression letting position 1 survive with 5 KB of content kept it green. (Its TS sibling, gate leg (e-3), does sum, but filters `typeof v === "string"`, so any subscripted node's content is silently excluded — the same blindness one layer up.) | **RESOLVED** | The assertion now sums the WHOLE serialized `byRefValues` structure (`%ToJSON()` length) against the documented budget plus an explicit scaffolding allowance, so it observes the property its message claims. |

### Superseded rows

- **`34-6-CR-8` — now RESOLVED**, not open. See `34-6-CR2-4`. Removed from the open MEDIUM count.
- **`34-6-CR-9` — PARTIALLY superseded.** Its rationale ("documented nowhere") is no longer true at the two consumer-facing surfaces: both the tool description and `packages/iris-dev-mcp/README.md` now state that the marker's `ceiling=<N>chars` suffix varies with the remaining budget, and (this pass) that a sub-marker budget emits no marker at all. Its headline ("the advertised literal never appears for ANY truncated byRef leaf") is also factually wrong — position 0 always receives the full 32768 budget, so an over-budget first leaf emits the exact advertised marker. What legitimately remains open is the narrow half: `CHANGELOG.md` and the changeset still advertise only the fixed literal, and no doc site names the stable `[IRIS-MCP-TRUNCATED` PREFIX as the detection token. Stays open at that reduced scope.

| `34-6-CR2-6` | MEDIUM | **The per-field ceilings do not bound the response.** `output` (32768) + `returnValue` (32768) + `byRefValues` (32768) are three independent budgets, so a fully-compliant, fully-flagged `/classmethod` response can carry ~98 KB — and JSON escaping inflates it further (live: two 32768-character fields of escape-heavy content serialize to 131,102 characters). The documented rationale is that 32768 "stays safely under" the 50,031-character threshold at which a real MCP client diverted output to a file "with headroom for the rest of the JSON envelope" — which the worst case exceeds by ~2.6x. Rule #19 back-compat proven mechanically: `/command` (no returnValue/byRefValues) is untouched, and a small-returnValue response is byte-identical to pre-34.7. |

## QA pass on Story 34.7 (2026-08-16) — one new residual-risk item

The `34-6-CR2-6` RESOLVED disposition above is accurate for its own literal scope (three independent budgets replaced by one shared budget). QA's independent live re-measurement — a raw `fetch` against the real `/classmethod` endpoint, deliberately not trusting the dev's own reported number — confirms the dev's 98,410-character figure but surfaces a narrower, adjacent gap the resolution does not close.

| Item | Severity | Finding | Rationale for deferring | Suggested resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `34-7-QA-1` | MEDIUM | **The shared budget reduces but does not restore the 50,031-character safety margin for metacharacter-heavy content.** The dev's cited 98,410-character worst case (single maxed escape-heavy `returnValue`, leg (e-7)) is confirmed accurate — but is not itself the true ceiling. A realistic, idiomatic `byRefValues` shape (many short escape-heavy entries in a keyed Output array — the exact pattern `BuildByRefNode`'s own banner cites as idiomatic: "file paths, URLs, composite keys") measured live at **98,749 raw HTTP bytes**, and — more importantly — **106,405 characters in the pretty-printed `content[0].text`** that an MCP client actually renders (`JSON.stringify(result, null, 2)`, per `execute.ts`'s own tool handler), both above the documented 98,410 figure. The client-rendered number is over 2.1x past the 50,031-character threshold that was the entire rationale for `OUTPUTCEILING`. Docs are now honest about this residual risk (patched during this QA pass: `Utils.cls`'s `ApplyOutputCeiling` banner, `packages/iris-dev-mcp/README.md` x2, `execute.ts` x2 tool descriptions all now state the figure plainly and connect it back to the 50,031 threshold), but the underlying safety property this ceiling exists to protect (an escape-heavy response won't get diverted to a file by a real MCP client) is only improved — from a pre-34.7 ~131,102 chars / ~2.6x, to a post-34.7 ~98,410-106,405 chars / ~2-2.1x — never restored. | Fully closing this requires measuring/truncating against SERIALIZED size rather than raw characters (a re-serialize-and-check pass, or a conservative per-character escape-cost estimate applied at truncation time) — a larger design change than this pre-publish-blocker story's scope, and the raw-character ceiling plus honest disclosure is still a meaningful, tested improvement over the pre-34.7 shape. Not a publish blocker given the disclosure fix landed in this same pass. | Bound `ApplyOutputCeiling` (and the byRefValues byte budget in `BuildByRefNode`) against actual JSON-serialized length instead of raw `$Length`, or apply a conservative worst-case multiplier (every character can cost up to 6 serialized characters via `\uXXXX`) when computing the ceiling for content that cannot be cheaply pre-scanned for its true escape cost. |

So MEDIUM 9 + 1 = 10; open 67 + 1 = 68; distinct 132 + 1 = 133; terminal unchanged at 65.

## Code review on Story 34.7 (2026-08-16) — nine new LOW residual items

Three adversarial layers plus the reviewer's own live mutation/measurement pass. Four defects were FIXED IN THIS PASS and are therefore not ledgered: (a) the documented serialized "worst case" was wrong by 2x at all six doc sites — the reviewer's own live measurement on `/command` recorded **196,495** characters for 32768 raw C0 control characters (6.0x inflation, ~3.9x past the 50,031 client-divert threshold) versus the 98,410 previously published, and every site plus the changeset/CHANGELOG now carries the full escaping-neutral / mixed / worst-case range; (b) `execute.test.ts`'s new scenario mocked a 539-character `output` stamped `ceiling=500` — a shape the server's own never-exceeds-the-ceiling invariant forbids (Rule #54) — now derived arithmetically from the shared-budget model with a length assertion; (c) the story's Rule #24 BOOTSTRAP_VERSION record named a hash present nowhere in the tree, now recording the full four-hop chain; (d) the newly-reachable marker-less `output` band is now documented at both the tool description and the README.

| Item | Severity | Finding | Rationale for deferring | Suggested resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `34-7-CR-1` | LOW | `Utils.SurrogateSafeCutLength` backs off exactly ONE code unit with no re-check. On MALFORMED UTF-16 (two adjacent high surrogates, e.g. `Write $Char(55296)_$Char(55296)`, which IRIS `%String` permits), a cut landing on the second backs off onto the first — still a lone high surrogate, still WTF-8 on the wire. The banner justifies safety only for the preceding-unit-is-a-LOW-surrogate case. | The lone surrogate pre-exists in the source data either way, so this bounds the AC's claim rather than regressing behavior: well-formed input (every real astral character) is fully correct, verified live. | Convert the single `If` to a `While` (or re-test after the decrement); add a fixture with adjacent high surrogates to `TestSurrogateSafeCutLengthCases`. |
| `34-7-CR-2` | LOW | `checkDistFresh` skips (passes) unless `files` contains the EXACT string `"dist"`. `files:["dist/"]`, `["dist/**"]`, `["./dist"]`, or an absent `files` field all silently skip while printing a benign "declares no dist/ build output of its own" line — fail-OPEN exactly where the gate matters. The available independent signal (presence of a `build` script) is never consulted (Rule #44). | Mechanically verified not present today: all 7 dist-declaring packages use the exact string, and the only skipped package (`@iris-mcp/all`) genuinely has no `build` script and no `dist`. Structural, latent. | Skip only when no `files` entry starts with `dist` AND the package declares no `build` script; fail closed when `dist/` exists on disk but is undeclared. |
| `34-7-CR-3` | LOW | `checkDistFresh`'s JSDoc promises it "never throws … callers fail closed with a clear message instead of an uncaught stack trace", but `JSON.parse(readFileSync(...))` and `readdirSync` throw on a malformed/mid-write `package.json`, a `dist` that is a FILE not a directory (`ENOTDIR`), or an unreadable entry (`EPERM`/`EBUSY`, common on Windows). Neither caller wraps the call. | Exit code is still non-zero so publish does abort — the safety property holds; only the promised message shape is false. | Wrap the body in try/catch returning `{ok:false, reason}`, or correct the JSDoc; pin both branches. |
| `34-7-CR-4` | LOW | `newestMtimeMs` handles only `isDirectory()`/`isFile()` with no `else`. Node `Dirent` uses lstat semantics, so a SYMLINKED file or subdirectory under `src/` is silently skipped and its mtime never enters the comparison — a genuinely stale `dist/` can pass. | No symlinks in the repo today; the mirror case (`dist/` of only symlinks) reports "contains no files", which is fail-closed and harmless. | Add an `else` branch that `statSync`s (follows) symlinks, or fail closed on an unexpected entry type. |
| `34-7-CR-5` | LOW | No clock-skew guard: only `srcNewest > distNewest` fails. A `dist/` file with a FUTURE mtime (restored cache, extracted archive, VM clock jump, copied from another machine) makes every subsequent `src` edit compare older, so the gate passes indefinitely on an arbitrarily stale build. | Requires an unusual filesystem/clock state; the common staleness direction is caught. | Add a `distNewest > Date.now() + tolerance` check that fails closed with a clock-skew message. |
| `34-7-CR-6` | LOW | The gate verifies only that `dist/` exists, is non-empty, and is newer than `src/` — never that the package's DECLARED entry points (`main`/`module`/`exports`/`bin`) actually exist inside it. One stray recent file (e.g. a leftover chunk, or `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` written into `dist`) satisfies all three checks, and an entry-point-less tarball reaches an immutable registry. | The fully-empty-dist case — the one that actually occurred during this story via a stale `tsbuildinfo` — IS caught, verified live by the reviewer (tsc exit 0, zero dist output, gate exit 1). | Resolve each declared entry point relative to `pkgDir` and `existsSync` it; fail closed on any missing one. |
| `34-7-CR-7` | LOW | Freshness is per-package `src/` vs own `dist/` only, so CROSS-package staleness is invisible: if `@iris-mcp/shared` is rebuilt (e.g. a `bootstrap-classes.ts` regen) and `@iris-mcp/dev` is not, dev's own `src/` is untouched and the gate passes while shipping a stale embedded `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION`. `package.json`/`tsconfig.json` edits are likewise invisible build inputs. | A full `pnpm turbo run build` before publish (already the documented flow, and turbo's own dependency graph handles ordering) avoids it. | Include workspace-dependency dist mtimes and non-`src` build inputs in the comparison, or delegate freshness to `turbo run build` and assert its cache state. |
| `34-7-CR-8` | LOW | Epic-gate leg (e-5)'s effectiveness depends on cut-position PARITY: it works only because `32768 - 41` = 32727 is odd and odd positions in its generated run are high surrogates. Its comment claims it holds "regardless of the ceiling/marker's own exact digit-length arithmetic", which is false — a one-character marker/ceiling change flips parity, strict decode passes, and the leg stays green while proving nothing. The ObjectScript sweep guards this with `tSawWouldHaveCutMidPairUnguarded`; the TS leg has no equivalent. | Currently genuinely effective — the reviewer's own mutation run reproduced the defect through this exact leg (strict decode threw, 32,770 chars), and post-fix `output.length` = 32,767 confirms the back-off fires. | Assert the cut position lands on a high surrogate (or drive both parities), mirroring the ObjectScript sweep's would-have-cut guard; correct the comment. |
| `34-7-CR-9` | LOW | The "ONE 32768-character shared budget" is not a hard bound on total RAW response content: `BuildByRefNode` deliberately does not charge per-node JSON scaffolding (~25-30 chars x up to `BYREFNODECEILING`=1000 nodes ≈ 30 KB), and the `<Object:...>` OREF placeholder is charged AFTER emission then clamped, so one placeholder can overshoot by up to ~220 chars. Documented in `BuildByRefNode`'s banner as `OUTPUTCEILING + (BYREFNODECEILING x 30)`, but the tool descriptions and README state the shared budget without that caveat. | Pre-existing Story 34.6 behavior, unchanged by 34.7 and already documented at the method banner; the node ceiling bounds it deterministically. | Either charge scaffolding against the byte budget, or state the `+ ~30 KB` structural residue at the tool-description/README level alongside the 1000-node ceiling. |

**Terminal disposition for the escalated item.**

| Item | Disposition | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `34-6-CR-7` | **RESOLVED** (supersedes the Story 34.6 `CLOSED-BY-DECISION`, which closed it by documentation only and explicitly left "the underlying defect ... open as a future investigation") | Fixed centrally in `ExecuteMCPv2.Utils.ReadRequestBody` via the new `DecodeUtf8Stream` / `IncompleteUtf8TailLength` / `SanitizeUnpairedSurrogates` methods. Reviewer-independent live evidence on HSCUSTOM, not re-trusted from the story: (a) **Rule #19 / AC 34.8.5 byte-identity proven mechanically across 12 DISTINCT handler classes** — the pre-fix `Utils.cls` was pushed to the server via a mirrored scratch path, raw HTTP responses were captured from 23 requests spanning `Command`, `Global`, `Security`, `Config`, `Task`, `Analytics`, `EnvSync`, `SystemConfig`, `SqlAdvisor`, `Health`, `Monitor`, `Interop`, `MessageResend`, and `UnitTest`, then re-captured after restoring the fix; **all 16 ASCII responses were SHA-256-identical** across the mutation (the sole exception carried only a varying `duration` field, which also differed between two consecutive post-fix runs, confirming inherent nondeterminism rather than a fix-induced change), while **4 non-ASCII responses genuinely CHANGED**, proving the comparison was sensitive and not vacuous; (b) `SanitizeUnpairedSurrogates` independently attacked on 9 adversarial shapes (valid pair at start/end, lone high, lone low, adjacent highs, pair-then-orphan, empty, and the exact boundary code units 55295 / 56319 / 57343 / 57344) — all correct, and a genuine astral emoji provably survives untouched through both the sanitizer and the full decode path; (c) large-body integrity re-verified through the shipped method at 4,500,000 and 11,250,000 raw bytes — decoded size matched the mechanically-computed expectation EXACTLY with an intact tail in both cases; (d) mutation-verify of the sanitizer wiring reproduced QA's result exactly (2 RED / 31 GREEN, restored byte-identical). Both READMEs, the CHANGELOG, and the changeset now describe the FIXED behavior. |

**Fixed in this review pass (deliberately NOT ledgered).** Two HIGH defects were found and fixed rather than deferred: (a) **`TestDecodeUtf8StreamLargeBodyNoMaxString` could not detect the silent truncation AC 34.8.4 exists to prevent** — the reviewer proved live that a decode regressed to a single `.Read(stream.Size)` still satisfied all three of its assertions (no throw, object returned, correct FIRST slice) while silently losing **801,596 characters**; a mechanically-derived total-size assertion and a sequential tail check were added and mutation-verified (both new assertions go RED on that exact regression, and the test stays under half a second); (b) **`packages/iris-dev-mcp/scripts/prepublish-gate.mjs` armed only `execute-classmethod-epic-gate.test.ts` with `IRIS_REQUIRE_LIVE=1`**, so the new `request-body-utf8-decode.test.ts` — the ONLY end-to-end proof of this fix — would silently `ctx.skip()` and let a publish ship the fix unexecuted (the same defect class already recorded as `34-4-R3` / AC 34.6.3); the gate now takes a named roster of live-IRIS gate files. Also fixed in-pass: the `pChunkSize = 0` non-terminating read loop (live-confirmed that `.Read(0)` returns `""` WITHOUT setting `AtEnd`), a discarded `%Stream.Write` status that could silently truncate the decoded body, a decode-stage fault misreported to callers as "Invalid JSON in request body", the file-wide fixture skip that masked 8 fixture-independent tests, an untested `IncompleteUtf8TailLength` branch, the RFC-3629-inaccurate lead-byte banner, a wrong hand-authored fixture size (`~3,900` vs the actual 3,300), the stale `BOOTSTRAP_VERSION` in `sprint-status.yaml`, and the undocumented malformed-input dispositions (now published in both READMEs with a filter-bypass security note).

| Item | Severity | Finding | Rationale for deferring | Suggested resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `34-8-CR-1` | MEDIUM | **The decode is unconditional and never consults `%request.CharSet`.** `grep -i charset src/` finds `CharSet` only in comments, never in code, while `%CSP.Request.UpdateURL` genuinely populates it from a Content-Type charset parameter. A third-party client posting `Content-Type: application/json; charset=ISO-8859-1` with a real Latin-1 body decoded CORRECTLY before this story (the old behavior was effectively Latin-1) and now fails: `0xE9` is read as a 3-byte lead, swallows the following bytes, and the request errors. AC 34.8.5's Rule #19 proof covers ASCII only, so this regression is outside the proof's reach. | These are open REST routes, but no client in this suite sends a charset parameter (the probe confirmed `%request.CharSet` is empty for every real call), so nothing in the product regresses today. Implementing charset negotiation hours before the first publish would add an untested code path whose IRIS `$ZCONVERT` encoding names do not map 1:1 to HTTP charset labels — a materially riskier change than the narrow gap it closes. | When `%request.CharSet` is non-empty and names something other than UTF-8, translate using that charset (mapping HTTP charset labels to IRIS table names) instead of decoding as UTF-8; pin with a fixture posting a declared-Latin-1 body. |
| `34-8-CR-2` | MEDIUM | **No end-to-end test crosses the production chunk boundary.** The largest body any of the 12 HTTP tests submits is `"café-世界-😀-".repeat(300)` = 3,300 characters / 5,400 raw bytes — about 0.5% of `UTF8CHUNKSIZE` (1,000,000). Every end-to-end fixture therefore decodes in a SINGLE chunk, so the carry/hold-back machinery — the most intricate part of the fix — is exercised only by ObjectScript unit tests at artificial 1-7 byte chunk sizes, never through `ReadRequestBody` at the value production actually uses. A >1MB body is realistic for `iris_global_set`'s `value`. | The reviewer independently exercised the SHIPPED method at 4,500,000 and 11,250,000 raw bytes with multi-byte content throughout — i.e. across 4 and 11 real 1,000,000-byte boundaries — and decoded size and tail were both exact, so the machinery IS verified at the production chunk size; what is missing is a PERMANENT pin, and specifically one driven over real HTTP. A multi-megabyte HTTP fixture in the default suite is slow and a plausible flake source. | Add one gate-suite test posting a >1,000,000-byte non-ASCII body through `/command` or `/global` and asserting exact round-trip, or an ObjectScript test that calls `DecodeUtf8Stream` with the DEFAULT chunk size on a >1MB fixture and asserts size + tail. |
| `34-8-CR-3` | LOW | **The `GetMimeData("BODY")` branch is decoded unconditionally although the story's own probe proves it is never reached, and a multipart part carries its own `CharSet`.** `ReadRequestBody`'s banner concedes the branch "is never populated for this project's plain `application/json` POSTs" and then asserts both branches "neither applies charset translation of its own" — a claim about a path never observed live. `%CSP.Stream` exposes a per-part `CharSet` property; if the branch ever goes live with an already-translated character stream, `DecodeUtf8Stream` would DOUBLE-DECODE exactly the content this story fixed. QA's own Rule #54 enumeration omits this branch. | Genuinely unreachable today for this project's traffic (probe-confirmed `mimeIsObject=0` on every call), so there is no live defect — it is an unverified assertion about a dormant path, which is precisely the Rule #54 shape worth recording rather than silently trusting. | Either drop the decode from that branch and let it fall through, or consult the part's own `CharSet` before decoding; state in the banner that the claim is unverified for multipart. |
| `34-8-CR-4` | LOW | **A CESU-8/WTF-8 surrogate PAIR decodes differently depending on chunk alignment.** Live-confirmed by the reviewer: raw bytes `ED A0 BD ED B8 80` (CESU-8 for U+1F600) decode to `??` at chunk size 3 but to a genuine 😀 at chunk size 6 and at the production 1,000,000. `IncompleteUtf8TailLength` treats each 3-byte group as complete, so the boundary may fall between them; when both land in one `$ZConvert` call the two lone surrogates form a valid pair that `SanitizeUnpairedSurrogates` correctly leaves alone. So the same malformed input yields two different outputs by byte offset, and malformed CESU-8 is silently laundered into a real character at production chunk size. | Malformed input only — well-formed UTF-8 is unaffected, and BOTH outcomes are valid UTF-8 on the wire, so nothing invalid escapes and no valid data is corrupted. The `DecodeUtf8Stream` banner's "identical to a single-shot decode" property holds for all conformant input. | Decide and document one disposition for a CESU-8 pair (accept as the astral character, or substitute); if the former, note in the banner that the chunk-independence property is stated for well-formed UTF-8 only, and pin the CESU-8 pair case at two chunk alignments. |
| `34-8-CR-5` | LOW | **The request body is now materialized a SECOND time, in IRISTEMP, with no size guard.** `%FromJSON` previously streamed straight from `%request.Content`; every request now writes a fully decoded copy into a `%Stream.TmpCharacter` first, so peak temp usage roughly doubles per request across all 46 call sites, and `ReadRequestBody` never inspects `tStream.Size` before starting. | Inherent to the chosen decode design and the direct cause of the fix working at all; measured cost is modest (11.25 MB decoded in 0.785s) and the temp stream is released with the request. A ceiling is a product decision, not a defect. | If a bound is wanted, check `tStream.Size` against a documented maximum in `ReadRequestBody` and return a clear error, rather than discovering the limit as an IRISTEMP fault. |
| `34-8-CR-6` | LOW | **The malformed-byte candidate set is closed by example, not stated exhaustive (Rule #56).** The sanitizer addresses exactly one class of `$ZConvert` output (unpaired surrogates); the tests pin overlong, orphan continuation, and WTF-8 surrogates, but no AC states the enumeration of "byte sequences whose decode could re-emerge as invalid UTF-8" is complete, and no fixture covered `F5`-`FF` or an above-U+10FFFF sequence. | The reviewer probed exactly this gap live and found it CLEAN: an `F5` lead, an `F8` 5-byte-style lead, a bare `FF`, `F4 90 80 80` (above U+10FFFF), and `ED BF BF` all decode to a literal `?` and leak NO surrogate or other code unit that would be invalid UTF-8 on the wire. So there is no known reachable gap — only an unstated completeness claim. | State in the ACs (or the `SanitizeUnpairedSurrogates` banner) that the invalid-output candidate set is exhaustive as of the named probe date with its evidence, and pin one `F5`-`FF` and one above-U+10FFFF fixture so the property cannot drift silently. |
| `34-8-CR-7` | LOW | **`cycle-log-epic-34.md` line 90 carries an impossible timestamp, `2026-08-16T25:00:00Z` (hour 25), in a machine-parsed TSV log.** Any consumer parsing this column strictly will reject or mis-order the row. | Not touched by this review pass: the reviewer was explicitly instructed not to write cycle-log entries, so the correction is left to the Project Lead who owns that artifact. Pure metadata — no product impact. | Correct the hour to the intended value (the adjacent `spawn_at=2026-08-16T23:05:00Z` suggests a next-day `2026-08-17T01:00:00Z`), and consider a format check on the log's timestamp column. |

**Disposition tally (Rule #51 — mechanically counted from the Disposition/Severity columns above):** 8 items handled — **1 resolved** (`34-6-CR-7`, re-dispositioned from `CLOSED-BY-DECISION` to `RESOLVED`), **7 newly deferred open** (0 HIGH / 2 MEDIUM / 5 LOW). All 7 enter at Rule #37 re-deferral count 0. Eleven further defects were fixed in-pass and are deliberately NOT ledgered (see the paragraph above).

**Ledger state after this pass:** **0 HIGH / 12 MEDIUM / 72 LOW = 84 open** across **149 distinct items** (65 terminal), derived mechanically per Rule #51 from the prior recount (77 open / 142 distinct / 65 terminal): +2 MEDIUM, +5 LOW, all open. So MEDIUM 10 + 2 = 12; LOW 67 + 5 = 72; open 77 + 7 = 84; distinct 142 + 7 = 149. Terminal is unchanged at 65 — `34-6-CR-7` was ALREADY terminal (closed-by-decision) and is re-dispositioned in place, not newly added. Check: 0 + 12 + 72 = 84.
