## Post-Epic-34 full-surface verification sweep (2026-08-17) — nine findings, all escalated to Epic 35

**Source:** Project-Lead-requested manual exercise of **all 109 tools across the 5 MCP servers** against a live IRIS 2026.1 instance, immediately after Epic 34's retrospective close and before the SC-4 merge. Build and self-install verified clean (`BOOTSTRAP_VERSION 01dc15bb27df` deployed == embedded, `IsConfigured` true, 15/15 turbo tasks, 2,878 tests green).

**Critical framing:** every finding below is a **PRE-EXISTING defect in code Epic 34 did not author**. Epic 34's own deliverables all passed with discriminating oracles. These were invisible to a green suite because no prior activity drove the complete tool surface live — the same blindness class Rule #59 addresses, one layer out: not a dead gate, but an *absent* one.

**Disposition:** all nine escalated to **Epic 35** (Sprint Change Proposal `sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-17.md`, approved by the Project Lead 2026-08-17). Sequencing: SC-4 merge → Epic 35 → beta hand-off. None is re-deferred; all enter at **Rule #37 re-deferral count 0**.

| Item | Severity | Finding | Why not fixed in Epic 34 | Suggested fix (Epic 35 story) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `35-SWEEP-1` | **HIGH** | **`iris_production_item:set` can never succeed.** `Interop.cls:473` calls one-arg `Ens.Config.Item.NameExists(tItemName, .tID)`, but that index is composite `(Production, Name)` — so `tItemName` binds to the *Production* slot and the undefined ByRef ID binds to *Name*. Proven live via `iris_execute_classmethod`: two-key call → `returnValue 1`, id `835`; one-key call → `returnValue 0`. `get` (SQL over the extent, line 429), `enable`/`disable` (`Ens.Director.EnableConfigItem`) are unaffected — so `get` resolves an item that `set` then reports as "not found". | Outside Epic 34's scope (classmethod fidelity); `Interop.cls` was touched only by Story 34.8's UTF-8 decode. This is exactly the trap **Rule #27 already codifies** — the rule existed, this call site never applied it. | Resolve by the FULL composite key, consistent with the `add`/`remove` paths' existing `FindItemByConfigName` usage (lines 609/693). Probe first (#16). **Story 35.1.** |
| `35-SWEEP-2` | **HIGH** | **`iris_analytics_cubes:build` returns unparseable non-JSON.** `%DeepSee.Utils.%BuildCube()` at `Analytics.cls:219` writes to the current device and **no `ReDirectIO` appears anywhere in `Analytics.cls`**. Raw wire body: `\nERROR #20013: Cube 'NOSUCHCUBE' does not exist{"status":…}` — a correct envelope with prepended device text. `sync` (`%SynchronizeCube`, line 231) parses cleanly, isolating the cause. | Same class as Rule #7 (I/O redirect + single-response dispatch), which Epic 34 applied to `Command.cls` only. `Base.cls`'s pre-flight guards *serialization* failure, not a target writing to the device before the envelope renders — a gap the epic never claimed to cover. | Apply the proven `Command.cls` null-device redirect discipline. Prove RED from the RAW wire body, not an internal round-trip (Rule #59). Success path still unverified — zero cubes on this instance. **Story 35.2.** |
| `35-SWEEP-3` | MEDIUM | **Localized error prefixes come from an unpinned request locale.** `Accept-Language` is never set anywhere in the suite (`http-client.ts` ~L183 builds headers without it); IRIS resolves the effective `*` to the alphabetically-first locale (`araw`), localizing every `%Status` prefix. Verified by curl on one endpoint: `*` → `خطأ #5001`, `en-US,en;q=0.9` → `ERROR #5001`, no header → `ERROR #5001`. Instance locale is `enuw`. | The suite accommodated the *symptom* rather than the cause: `Utils.cls:181` strips both prefixes (Rule #8) and `Loader.cls:168-214` normalizes locale words. Both were correct responses to a **second, independent** mechanism — `GetErrorText` picks the message table PER WORKER PROCESS (Rule #13, 2026-07-03 SADEMO smoke) — which the header does not control. | Pin `Accept-Language` (operator-overridable, English default). **PRESERVE all existing stripping** — it guards the other mechanism. Keep `http-client.test.ts:1084-1129` unchanged. **Story 35.3.** |
| `35-SWEEP-4` | MEDIUM | **`iris_resource_manage:listPrivileges` is unbounded.** Grantee `_SYSTEM` (holds `%All`) returns **15,341 rows / 2,900,478 characters**, exceeding the client limit and diverting to a file. No `maxRows`, no `cursor`, no output ceiling. Handler `Security.cls:3083`; tool `packages/iris-admin-mcp/src/tools/resource.ts`. | Predates the Rule #38 codification being applied to this surface; the action was added as a read and reads were not swept for output bounds. | Add `maxRows` + cursor, prefer a SQL `TOP` so the SCAN is genuinely bounded (Rule #38 — an output cap is not a scan cap). Consider the `%All` short-circuit (#6) instead of enumerating derived rows. **Story 35.4.** |
| `35-SWEEP-5` | MEDIUM | **DocDB tool family (4 tools) is dead on a default IRIS install.** `%Service_DocDB` is disabled by IRIS default; `iris_docdb_manage`/`_document`/`_find`/`_property` all fail with a bare `ERROR #822: Access Denied` from `/api/docdb/v1/{ns}`. The `/api/docdb` webapp exists and IS enabled, and `_SYSTEM` holds `%All` — so it is purely the service gate. The remedy (`iris_service_manage:enable`) is itself governance-default-disabled, leaving no path forward from inside the suite. | Never exercised live: no prior story or smoke drove the DocDB family against a default instance, so the prerequisite was never discovered. | Actionable error naming BOTH routes (Management Portal or `IRIS_GOVERNANCE` override) + document the prerequisite at point of use (#30/#43). **Story 35.7.** |
| `35-SWEEP-6` | MEDIUM | **`iris_oauth_manage` client-create leaks a raw `<PARAMETER>`.** `Security.cls:1792` calls `%SYS.OAuth2.Registration.RegisterClient(tServerName, .tProps)` having validated only that `serverName` is non-empty (lines 1770-1776) — never that it names a real server definition. `OAuth2.ServerDefinition` does not exist on this instance (`COUNT(*) = 0`). Client registration additionally requires a prior successful `discover`, which requires an IRIS SSL/TLS config — verified: `discover` fails with *"Although Https property is enabled no SSLConfiguration is specified"*. None documented. | Server-create works and was the tested path; the client path requires an environment (TLS + a discovered server definition) that no prior smoke stood up. | Pre-validate `serverName` (probe first — do not assume the backing class); error names the real precondition; document both prerequisites. **Story 35.5.** |
| `35-SWEEP-7` | MEDIUM | **`iris_interop_rest` rejects its own documented example.** `rest.ts:32` describes `name` as `"REST application name (e.g., '/myapi')"`. Passing `/claudesmokeapi` fails: *"Application name is not a valid package name: /claudesmokeapi"*. A package name (`ClaudeSmokeApi`) succeeds. | A description-vs-validator mismatch: no test asserts that a documented example is accepted, so the wrong example survived every green run. | Correct the description to the package-name contract with a working example; audit other `e.g.` descriptions against their validators (#56). **Story 35.5.** |
| `35-SWEEP-8` | MEDIUM | **`iris_doc_xml_export:import` reports success but does not compile.** `format.ts:163` POSTs Atelier `action/xml/load`, which loads the definition without compiling. Response `{"imported":["…XmlProbe.cls"]}` is followed by `<CLASS DOES NOT EXIST>` on invocation; a separate `iris_doc_compile` fixes it. No `compile` option (unlike `iris_doc_load`), no warning. | The import path was verified by response shape, never by *using* the imported document — a success-signal test rather than an outcome test. | Additive `compile` PARAMETER mirroring `iris_doc_load` (NOT a new action — Constraint E-1); probe whether Atelier accepts a native qualifier. **Story 35.6.** |
| `35-SWEEP-9` | MEDIUM | **`iris_rest_manage:get` fails misleadingly on legacy apps.** `list` with `scope:"all"` returns `/api/executemcp/v2`, but `get` on that exact name 404s via the Mgmnt API with *"Check the IRIS web server configuration."* Per Rule #12 the Mgmnt API knows spec-first apps only — but `get` neither routes legacy names to the legacy path nor says so. Spec-first `get` works correctly. | Story 12.5 added `scope:"legacy"`/`"all"` to `list` only; `get` was not swept for the same dual-mode requirement — an enumeration-completeness gap (#56). | Route legacy names to the legacy path, or return an accurate explanation. A name `list` returns must never fail implying server misconfiguration. **Story 35.6.** |

**Disposition tally (Rule #51 — mechanically counted from the Severity column above):** 9 items, **all newly deferred open** — **2 HIGH / 7 MEDIUM / 0 LOW**. All 9 enter at Rule #37 re-deferral count 0 and are assigned to a named Epic 35 story (35.1 ×1, 35.2 ×1, 35.3 ×1, 35.4 ×1, 35.5 ×2, 35.6 ×2, 35.7 ×1). Zero items are unassigned.

**Note on the reported count:** the verbal report to the Project Lead said "2 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM"; the mechanical count over the findings table is **2 HIGH / 7 MEDIUM = 9**. The heading had been written separately from the list and under-counted by one — the Rule #51 failure mode exactly. The table above is authoritative.

**Ledger state after this pass:** **2 HIGH / 19 MEDIUM / 72 LOW = 93 open** across **158 distinct items** (65 terminal), derived mechanically per Rule #51 from the prior state (84 open / 149 distinct / 65 terminal): +2 HIGH, +7 MEDIUM, +0 LOW, all open. So HIGH 0 + 2 = 2; MEDIUM 12 + 7 = 19; LOW 72 + 0 = 72; open 84 + 9 = 93; distinct 149 + 9 = 158. Terminal unchanged at 65 — nothing was dispositioned in this pass, only added. Check: 2 + 19 + 72 = 93.

**This is the first time the ledger has carried an open HIGH since Epic 34 closed at 0 HIGH.** Both HIGHs are assigned to Epic 35 stories that must close before the beta hand-off, per the approved sequencing.

AC 35.0.2's merge precondition was re-verified live: `git merge-base --is-ancestor 6fe1763 origin/feature/feature-wave-4-classmethod-fidelity` exits **0** — Epic 34's commits are reachable from the wave-4 feature branch; the retro §6 branch-reachability risk is closed. Rule #57 machinery (bounded-close, frozen-diff snapshot, delivery receipts) was confirmed still present and unmodified in `.claude/skills/bmad-code-review/steps/` (installed by Story 34.0) — AC 35.0.4 satisfied by verification, not re-implementation.

## Story 35.1 code review (2026-08-17) — deferred findings

Eight findings from the three adversarial review layers were triaged to DEFER; the rest were patched in-story (see the story's Review Findings section). `35-1-CR-1` is a **HIGH carried out of Story 35.1** and the lead should decide whether Epic 35 needs a dedicated story for it before beta, since it is the same user-visible `get`/`set` contradiction class that Story 35.1 exists to eliminate, reached by a different route.

| # | Finding | Severity | Origin story | Deferral rationale | Suggested resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `35-1-CR-1` | **A config item whose host `ClassName` no longer resolves to a business type is UNREACHABLE through `set` and `remove`, which report it as "not found" while `get` returns it happily.** `Ens.Config.Production:findItemByName` -> `findItemIdByName` (`irislib/Ens/Config/Production.cls:739`) does `Continue:$$$eHostTypeUnknown=##class(Ens.Config.Item).GetBusinessType(tClassname,$$$NULLOREF)` — items with a deleted, renamed, or uncompiled host class are skipped entirely, so `FindItemByConfigName` returns `$$$NULLOREF`. **Empirically proven live during this review**: with the className guard reverted, a `set` persisted a non-`Ens.Host` className and the fixture item immediately became unresolvable to `FindItemByConfigName` — the test-class restore hook failed with "fixture item 'FixtureOp' not found", and every later test cascaded. The broken item is then unrepairable through the tool, and the error text says it does not exist. | HIGH | 35.1 (code review, Edge Case Hunter; confirmed at irislib source AND reproduced live) | Not introduced by Story 35.1 and not `set`-specific — `remove` (line ~800) uses the same `FindItemByConfigName` idiom, so a fix scoped to `set` would leave the handler internally inconsistent. AC 35.1.2 explicitly directed the story to prefer the sibling paths' API, which is what it did. The safe fix also needs live probing of the `tProd.Items` swizzle interaction (see below), which is more than a code-review drive-by. Story 35.1's own `ValidateHostClassName` guard now prevents the tool from *creating* this state, which bounds the exposure to items broken by other means. Rule #37 re-deferral count **0** (first carry). | Add a composite-key fallback in BOTH `set` and `remove`: when `FindItemByConfigName` returns null, resolve via the two-arg `##class(Ens.Config.Item).NameExists(tProdName, tItemName, .tID)` (the other API probed and verified under AC 35.1.2) and then take the item **from `tProd.Items`** via `tProd.Items.GetAt(tProd.Items.FindObjectId(tID))` — **not** via `%OpenId(tID)`. Live-probe this first: `SaveToClass` serializes `tProd`'s in-memory `Items` collection, so mutating a separately-opened object risks the XData write silently persisting stale values. Regression-pin with a fixture production whose XData names a non-host `ClassName`, and mutation-verify RED. |
| `35-1-CR-2` | **`FindItemByConfigName`'s Output `%Status` is captured into `tFindStatus` and never read** (`Interop.cls` `set` branch), flattening an SQL failure (`"SQL Error finding config item ... SQLCODE="`) and a cross-production qualifier (`"Can't find item from Production 'A' in Production 'B'"`, reachable because `Ens.Director.ParseConfigItemName` splits `itemName` on `\|\|` and `\|`) into a generic "not found in production 'X'". | MEDIUM | 35.1 (code review, Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter) | Rule #9 violation, but the library also returns an error status (`$$$EnsErrConfigItemNotFound`) for the ORDINARY absence case, so naively propagating it would replace the branch's pinned AC 35.1.3 contract text on the common path. Correctly discriminating the actionable causes needs error-code inspection plus Rule #8 single-prefix stripping — deliberately not done as a drive-by; an in-code NOTE at the call site records the reasoning. | Inspect the status code (`$System.Status.GetErrorCodes`) and keep the pinned production-naming text for `$$$EnsErrConfigItemNotFound`; for any OTHER code, attach the library status with `$System.Status.AppendStatus` (avoids Rule #8 hand-splicing). Pin both branches with tests. |
| `35-1-CR-3` | **`set` is governance-grandfathered / always-enabled, but Story 35.1 escalated it from a single SQL-extent row update to a durable rewrite of the production CLASS source** (`SaveToClass` regenerates the whole `ProductionDefinition` XData and `%Save()`s the `%Dictionary.ClassDefinition`). `add`/`remove` do the same class of write and are `mutates:"write"`, default-DISABLED; `set` still carries `destructiveHint: false`. | MEDIUM | 35.1 (code review, Blind Hunter + Acceptance Auditor) | Constraint E-1 (lead-binding for this story) states no new tool/action key and no `mutates`/preset classification is needed, and `set`'s *intent* was always a durable settings update — the extent-only write was the bug, not the contract. Re-classifying a grandfathered always-available action is a product decision above a code review's authority (and would be a breaking change for existing users, against the project's additive-only release gate). | Lead decision: either ratify the current classification explicitly (documenting that `set` writes production class source — the README now states this), or introduce a separate opt-in gate for the XData-writing half. Revisit alongside `35-1-CR-4`. |
| `35-1-CR-4` | **Whole-production read-modify-write with no lock: concurrent `set`/`add`/`remove` silently lose updates.** Each call does `LoadFromClass(prod)` -> `%OpenId(prod)` -> mutate one item -> `SaveToClass` (full XData rewrite) with no `LOCK`. Request B, holding a snapshot taken before A wrote, overwrites A's already-acknowledged change. | MEDIUM | 35.1 (code review, Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter) | Pre-existing shape inherited from `add`/`remove`, which Story 35.1 was explicitly instructed to copy. The window is materially wider now that the ungated, higher-frequency `set` shares it, but closing it is a handler-wide concurrency design change, not a scoped fix. | Take `Ens.Director`'s runtime lock (or a dedicated `LOCK ^Ens.Config.Production(prod)`) around the `LoadFromClass`->`SaveToClass` span in all three branches, with a bounded timeout and a clear "production is being modified" error. Regression-pin with two concurrent jobs. |
| `35-1-CR-5` | **Intra-production duplicate item names resolve silently to a preference-ranked first match, and `\|`/`\|\|` qualifiers in `itemName` are neither rejected nor documented (Rule #29).** `findItemIdByName` selects `WHERE Production = ? AND Name = ?` (not unique), ranks candidates and returns `$O(tFoundItems(""))` with no ambiguity error; multiple same-named items in one production is a supported IRIS state (`Ens.Director.EnableConfigItem`'s own doc says so), disambiguated by `\|comment`. AC 35.1.3 closed only the CROSS-production dimension. | MEDIUM | 35.1 (code review, Edge Case Hunter) | AC 35.1.3 as written targets the cross-production dimension, which the fix does close. The intra-production dimension needs a product decision (reject as ambiguous vs. expose the `\|comment` qualifier as a first-class parameter) plus a schema/doc change, and it is a pre-existing characteristic of the shared lookup, not a Story 35.1 regression. | Decide the contract, then either return a candidate-listing ambiguity error (matching AC 35.1.3's original wording) or add an explicit `comment`/`qualifier` parameter. Either way, document how `itemName` is parsed and validate the delimiter in the user-supplied slot per Rule #29. |
| `35-1-CR-6` | **`get` with `production` omitted still returns an arbitrary row and the response never echoes the resolved production**, so a `get`/`set` pair with `production` omitted on both can still silently describe different items (`get` = first extent row, `set` = active production). | MEDIUM | 35.1 (code review, all three layers) | The omitted-`production` path was deliberately left byte-identical under Rule #19, and the asymmetry is now disclosed in the tool description and README. Making `get` default to the active production would break `get` when no production is running; echoing `production` in the `get` response would break the Rule #19 byte-identity the story committed to. Both are product decisions. | Preferred: add `production` to the `get` response envelope (additive, lets a caller detect the mismatch) and re-baseline the Rule #19 claim as "query unchanged, response additively extended". Optionally warn when more than one row matches an unqualified `get`. |
| `35-1-CR-7` | **No gate asserts the fixture production classes are byte-identical to their checked-in `.cls` after a suite run** (Rule #58). `RestoreFixtureBaseline` restores property VALUES, then `SaveToClass` re-serializes the XData through `XMLExportToStream`, which need not reproduce the hand-written attribute order/whitespace of the on-disk fixture. | LOW | 35.1 (code review, Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter) | Verified live at review time that both fixtures ARE currently byte-identical to their on-disk `.cls` (via `iris_doc_get`) and that the extent matches, so there is no active drift — and the review made the restore status assert rather than swallow, which catches the value-level half. A byte-parity gate is a genuine but additive test-infrastructure improvement. | Add a test (or a suite-level `OnAfterAllTests`) that compares `iris_doc_get` output for both fixtures against the on-disk `.cls` and fails on any difference. |
| `35-1-CR-8` | **`get` and `set` may disagree on production-name matching semantics for a non-exact-case name**: `get` compares via SQL (`AND Production = ?`, subject to the column's collation) while `set` resolves via `%Dictionary.ClassDefinition.%ExistsId` (dictionary ID lookup). Nothing pins the comparison on either side and no test uses a differently-cased production name. | LOW | 35.1 (code review, Blind Hunter) | Same `get`/`set` disagreement family as `35-1-CR-6` and best fixed with it. `set`'s check matches the idiom `add`/`remove` already use (verified identical at `Interop.cls` lines ~691/778), so changing it in isolation would make `set` inconsistent with its siblings. | Pin the intended semantics with a test using a differently-cased production name, then normalize on whichever side diverges — across `set`, `get`, `add` and `remove` together. |

## Story 35.2 dev pass (2026-08-17/18) — AC 35.2.4 audit finding: `UnitTest.cls`'s device redirect binds the mnemonic on `$IO`, not a null device

Found while auditing every `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.*` handler for the same device-output-isolation defect Story 35.2 fixes in `Analytics.cls` (AC 35.2.4, Rule #56 — reported even though it did not block this story). `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.UnitTest.cls` already redirects `%UnitTest.Manager.RunTest`'s device output (it is NOT an instance of Story 35.2's "zero `ReDirectIO` calls" defect class), but via a structurally different and more fragile pattern than the one this story's Dev Notes document as proven: it binds the mnemonic directly on `tInitIO` (`$IO`, the live HTTP response stream) — `Set tWasRedirected = ##class(%Library.Device).ReDirectIO()` / `Use tInitIO::("^"_$ZNAME)` — rather than opening a throw-away null device first, the way `Command.cls` (and now `Analytics.cls`) do.

| # | Finding | Severity | Origin story | Deferral rationale | Suggested resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `35-2-DEV-1` | **`ExecuteMCPv2.REST.UnitTest.RunTests()` binds its I/O-redirect mnemonic on `$IO` itself instead of a throw-away null device**, the exact pattern `CommentTest.cls`'s `TestLargeOutputCaptureOver8KB` banner documents as a REAL, previously-shipped bug in `Command.cls` (fixed 2026-04-23): "Binding the mnemonic onto `$IO` ... leaves device parameters altered even after `ReDirectIO(0)` + bare `Use tInitIO`, ... causing CSP's response-buffer flush to silently drop the first ~8KB of the JSON envelope." `UnitTest.cls`'s own restore line (`Use tInitIO::($Select(tOldMnemonic=""\|\|(tOldMnemonic="%X364"):"", 1:"^"_tOldMnemonic))`) is more careful about clearing/restoring the PRIOR mnemonic than `Command.cls`'s pre-fix bare `Use tInitIO` was, but that extra care does not address the root cause — the corruption Command.cls's historical bug describes happens to `$IO`'s own device/buffer state DURING the redirect, before any restore line runs, and `%UnitTest.Manager.RunTest` is exactly the kind of API (like `%BuildCube`) that can emit substantial narration for a large/verbose test run. Past `<MAXSTRING>` (~3.6 MB) each further write re-copies the whole buffer and throws, and `wstr` re-throws any non-`<MAXSTRING>` exception (e.g. `<STORE>`) back INTO `%BuildCube`, so a sufficiently verbose build could abort a legitimate cube build. The one-token fix two review layers independently proposed — `%BuildCube(tCube, 0, 0)` — is **forbidden**: this review probed it live and BOTH the narration AND the error text are `pVerbose`-gated (`%BuildCube("NOSUCH",0,1)` writes `ERROR #20013: ... does not exist` to the device; `%BuildCube("NOSUCH",0,0)` writes nothing), so silencing the source would leave `analytics-cubes-wire-gate.test.ts` structurally incapable of failing — deleting the entire redirect would still yield a green gate (Rule #59, "an oracle that cannot vary with the property it claims"). A warning to this effect is now in `Analytics.cls`'s own comment. | MEDIUM | 35.2 (code review) | The runtime cost is real but bounded and, on observed builds, negligible (a stock cube build emits a handful of lines; the `<MAXSTRING>` path needs an extraordinarily verbose build this instance cannot currently produce). Fixing it properly requires a design that reduces capture volume WITHOUT destroying the gate's oracle — a design question larger than this defect-fix story. Rule #37 re-deferral count **0** (first carry). | Options, in preference order: (a) bound the capture at the sink — have `wstr`/`wchr` stop appending once a small ceiling is reached when the caller has declared the text discardable, keeping `pVerbose=1` so the device still receives bytes and the gate still has something to isolate; (b) pass `pVerbose=0` ONLY together with a replacement oracle that can still detect redirect removal (e.g. a gate leg that drives a genuinely narrating target through the same branch). Do NOT take (b) without the replacement oracle. Live-verify either against the raw wire per Rule #48/#59. |
| `35-2-CR-5` | **The redirect-restore sequence has two failure shapes, in BOTH `Command.cls` and `Analytics.cls`.** (i) The outer `Catch`'s cleanup puts `ReDirectIO(0)`, `Use tInitIO` and `Close tNull` in ONE `Try` under a bare `Catch {}`, so a throw at the first or second step skips the rest — leaving the null device current when `RenderResponseBody` runs two lines later, i.e. an HTTP 200 with an empty body, the same defect class this story fixed. (ii) `Set tRedirected = 1` is executed AFTER the device-mutating `Use tNull::(...)`, so a throwing `Use` (or a timed-out `Open tNull:::1`, which does not raise — it sets `$TEST`, which is never read) leaves `tRedirected = 0` and the outer catch's `If tRedirected` guard skips cleanup entirely, leaking an open null device on a pooled CSP worker. | MEDIUM | 35.2 (code review) | Both shapes are inherited VERBATIM from `Command.cls`, which AC 35.2.2 explicitly required this story to copy without inventing a new mechanism ("Do not invent a mechanism"). Fixing them in `Analytics.cls` alone would leave the repo with two divergent redirect disciplines — strictly worse than one consistent, well-understood one. Neither shape was reproduced live in this pass; both need a device-exhaustion or client-disconnect scenario. ## Terminal disposition — Story 35.1 (re-opened, cycle_iteration=3, Task 7) closes `35-1-CR-1` (2026-08-18)

Story 35.1 was re-opened under Rule SC-5 solely to close this ledger HIGH — the last open HIGH in the ledger and, per the story's re-open note, "the SAME `get`/`set` contradiction this story exists to kill, reached by a second route." Scope was AC 35.1.7 / Task 7 only; Tasks 1-6 (closed `35-SWEEP-1`, above) were not re-touched.

**Probe first (#16), live on HSCUSTOM, disposable `ExecuteMCPv2.Temp.Story351dProbeProd`/`Story351dProbe` (deleted before finishing this pass):**

```
ProbeBasics:
LoadFromClass:OK;get_sql_found:1,className=%Stream.GlobalCharacter,comment=probe-baseline;
GetBusinessType(%Stream.GlobalCharacter)=0,eHostTypeUnknown=0;
ProdOpened:1;
FindItemByConfigName_isObject:0,status:خطأ <Ens>ErrConfigItemNotFound: Item BadHostOp not found in Production ExecuteMCPv2.Temp.Story351dProbeProd;
TwoArgNameExists:1,id=1562;
```

Confirms the ledger's finding exactly: (a) `get` (raw SQL) resolves the item with its unresolvable `%Stream.GlobalCharacter` className; `GetBusinessType` for that class DOES equal `$$$eHostTypeUnknown` (0=0), confirming `findItemIdByName`'s skip guard fires; (b) `FindItemByConfigName` returns `$$$NULLOREF`, `$$$EnsErrConfigItemNotFound`; (c) two-arg `NameExists(prod, name, .id)` DOES resolve it (id 1562).

**Swizzle-interaction probe (the trap the ledger flagged) — `ProbeOpenIdSwizzle` vs `ProbeItemsCollectionSwizzle`:**

```
ProbeOpenIdSwizzle:    id=1563;OpenIdOref:188@Ens.Config.Item;CollectionOref:188@Ens.Config.Item;sameOref:1;ItemSave:OK;SaveToClass:OK;afterReload_id=1564,comment=openid-swizzle-probe;
ProbeItemsCollectionSwizzle: id=1565;CollectionFound:1;ItemSave:OK;SaveToClass:OK;afterReload_id=1566,comment=itemscollection-swizzle-probe;
```

`%OpenId(tID)` returned the **SAME OREF** (`188@Ens.Config.Item`, `sameOref:1`) as `tProd.Items.GetAt(tProd.Items.FindObjectId(tID))` — IRIS's in-process object cache dedupes both paths to the identical swizzled instance here, so mutating via either path durably persisted (both reload checks show the new comment surviving a `LoadFromClass` resync). Per the story's own instruction ("if `%OpenId` actually works fine, say so with evidence and still prefer the `tProd.Items` idiom for consistency with the library"), the fix uses `tProd.Items.GetAt(tProd.Items.FindObjectId(tID))` — matching `irislib/Ens/Config/Production.cls:725-727` — not because `%OpenId` was shown unsafe, but for consistency with the library's own idiom.

**Fix:** a composite-key fallback added in BOTH the `set` and `remove` branches of `ExecuteMCPv2.REST.Interop:ItemManage` (`src/ExecuteMCPv2/REST/Interop.cls`, ~line 548 for `set`, ~line 843 for `remove`): when `FindItemByConfigName` returns `$$$NULLOREF`, resolve via two-arg `##class(Ens.Config.Item).NameExists(tProdName, tItemName, .tFallbackID)` and, if found, take the item from `tProd.Items.GetAt(tProd.Items.FindObjectId(tFallbackID))` before falling through to the existing not-found error. The fallback is scoped to the SAME already-resolved production (AC 35.1.3's cross-production guarantee is unaffected — no new ambiguity is introduced).

**Regression test**, `ExecuteMCPv2.Tests.InteropUnresolvableHostItemTest` (new class, 3 methods; fixture `ExecuteMCPv2.Tests.InteropUnresolvableHostFixtureProd`, two items both carrying `ClassName="%Stream.GlobalCharacter"`, never started, deliberately OUT of the bootstrap manifest per Rule #39):
- `TestGetResolvesItemWithUnresolvableHostClassName` — baseline sanity, automates probe (a).
- `TestSetReachesUnresolvableHostItemAndRepairPersists` — the core AC 35.1.7 test: asserts `FindItemByConfigName` genuinely cannot resolve the item pre-repair, then `set`s a VALID host `className` over real HTTP, asserts success + the `get` reflects it, and directly re-checks `FindItemByConfigName` now resolves the item WITHOUT the fallback — proving the repair restores normal resolution (the user-visible point of the fix, not merely that the fallback keeps working).
- `TestRemoveReachesUnresolvableHostItem` — `remove` succeeds on the unresolvable item; a follow-up `get` confirms it is genuinely gone. Teardown re-creates the item (mirrors `ItemManage`'s own `add` idiom).

**Mutation-verify RED/GREEN on the REAL HTTP endpoint (Rule #48/#59)** — the fallback was reverted via `git stash` (isolating only the 40-line `Interop.cls` diff), redeployed, and the new test class re-run:

## Terminal disposition — Story 35.3 closes `35-SWEEP-3` (2026-08-18, code-review gate)

Recorded at the code-review gate rather than the dev pass: Story 35.3's dev pass did not touch this file, which the Acceptance Auditor flagged as a recurrence of exactly the missed-row problem the Story 35.1 gate had to fix by hand (and which the Story 35.2 review had explicitly named as a recurrence risk). Recorded here so the next retro-review gate does not re-triage an already-closed item.

| Item | Severity | Disposition |
|---|------|-------------|
| `35-SWEEP-3` | MEDIUM | **RESOLVED** — `IrisHttpClient.executeFetch`'s single header-construction site now sends an explicit, operator-overridable `Accept-Language` (`IRIS_ACCEPT_LANGUAGE`, default `en-US,en;q=0.9`) on **every** request; verified at review that exactly ONE `fetch(` exists in the whole repo's runtime source, so no request path bypasses it. **The ledger item's own causal claim is confirmed, but its stated evidence was misleading and is corrected here:** the item (and AC 35.3.2) recorded "no header → `ERROR #` (English)" from a `curl` capture, which reads as contradicting the wildcard mechanism. `curl` genuinely omits the header; **Node's `fetch`/undici injects `Accept-Language: *` whenever the caller sets none**, so the suite's client was never in the "absent" state — it was sending `*`, which IRIS resolves to the alphabetically-first locale. Established by QA with a real local HTTP listener capturing the wire pre- and post-fix, and independently re-confirmed at review (Node v24.16.0, same endpoint, header the only variable, 3 repeats each): caller-sets-nothing → `خطأ #5001`; explicit `en-US,en;q=0.9` → `ERROR #5001`; explicit `*` → `خطأ #5001`. That A/B is the before/after delta on the guarded path (Rule #59) that the dev pass's own live table lacked, and it also attributes the lead's Story 35.2 Arabic-through-MCP observation to **mechanism 1** rather than leaving it unexplained. Post-patch live re-verification through the BUILT client on two namespaces (HSCUSTOM + SADEMO, Rule #34): default → `ERROR #5001` on both; `IRIS_ACCEPT_LANGUAGE=*` → `خطأ #5001` on both (the knob is not inert). **Mechanism 2 is untouched and still required**: `ExecuteMCPv2.Utils.cls` and `ExecuteMCPv2.Diagram/Loader.cls` are absent from the diff (verified mechanically), the mocked Arabic UTF-8 test is byte-identical, and no surface claims localized prefixes are eliminated wholesale. Permanent default-suite regression coverage: 4 tests from the dev pass + 12 added at the review gate, all mutation-verified RED against the pre-fix/pre-patch code. |

**Disposition tally (Rule #51 — mechanically counted from the Disposition column above):** 1 item — **1 RESOLVED** · 0 closed-with-evidence · 0 closed-by-decision · 0 re-deferred.

## Story 35.3 code review (2026-08-18) — defer bucket

Story 35.3's code review closed **CLEAN** (3/3 layers delivered, `review_degraded=false`). **14 patched in-story · 4 deferred (all LOW, below) · 7 dismissed with recorded reasons · 0 decision-needed.** No HIGH survives and **this pass adds no open HIGH to the ledger** — two layers opened at HIGH, but both were about the story's RECORD and EVIDENCE rather than the shipped code, and both were closed in-story by supplying the missing live A/B and correcting the write-up (details in the story's Review Findings).

**Patched in-story (NOT carried), for context on why the carried set is small:** four genuine code defects — (1) `IRIS_ACCEPT_LANGUAGE=""` or whitespace-only put an EMPTY `Accept-Language:` header on the wire (verified live against a local listener), silently un-pinning the very locale the variable exists to pin, and breaking the `!== ""` convention both optional siblings 20 lines above already follow; fixed at BOTH the loader and the client so hand-built config literals get the same guarantee; (2) a CR/LF- or non-ASCII-bearing value made undici throw a `TypeError` that `executeFetch` maps to `NETWORK_ERROR` ("Failed to connect to IRIS ... verify the host and port"), so **every** request failed with a diagnostic pointing at the wrong subsystem — now a named fail-fast error at config load, matching every sibling `IRIS_*` var; (3) the four-verb regression test drove only `post()` despite its name and asserted inside a `for` over `mock.calls` with no call-count guard, so it could pass vacuously; (4) `mergeProfile`'s new `acceptLanguage` inheritance had zero coverage and is invisible by construction, because the client's fallback yields exactly the value a naive test would assert — the new test uses a NON-default value so inheritance is distinguishable from fallback. Plus the Rule #56 docs gap (a sixth published README carrying the identical env-var table) and eight smaller record/comment corrections.

A caller summing `superUserPrivilegesOmitted` across pages gets a nonsense total, and the fields on an empty page read as though that page omitted something. | A direct consequence of the signal-preserving spread this story deliberately chose over `audit.ts`'s field-dropping rebuild — the spread is the right call and the alternative loses the signal entirely. Which per-page fields are page-scoped vs response-scoped is a pagination-contract question affecting every sibling list tool, not just this one. Rule #37 re-deferral count **0** (first carry). | Define response-scoped vs page-scoped fields once in the shared pagination helper, and emit response-scoped signals on the FIRST page only (or nest them under a `summary` object), applying it across the sibling list tools together. |
| `35-4-CR-7` | LOW | **Rule #34's second-namespace smoke was neither performed nor recorded as a residual risk.** The story's Testing section explicitly requires a second, genuinely-different namespace or a written residual-risk note for this namespace-scoped surface; the Dev Agent Record contains neither. All live proof — dev, QA, and this code review — ran against HSCUSTOM only. | Recording the residual risk is the option Rule #34 itself permits, and the risk is genuinely low here: the namespace-dependent part is the SQL privilege query, which the story did not change, while both new guards (`maxRows` cap, `%All` short-circuit) are namespace-independent — `%All` membership is instance-wide and resolved in `%SYS`. Rule #37 re-deferral count **0** (first carry). | On the next touch of this handler, run the same three probes (`_SYSTEM` uncapped, `_SYSTEM` capped, non-`%All` control) against a second namespace with a genuinely different SQL object population, and confirm the counts differ while the response SHAPES match. |

**Rework iteration 1 (2026-08-18) — `35-4-CR-1` RESOLVED, never carried.** The MEDIUM originally listed here (the unconditional `%SYS` excursion adding a privilege requirement, plus a swallowed `Security.Users.Get` status that could silently disable the `%All` guard) was resolved rather than deferred, on the lead's policy call: the excursion was DELETED outright instead of choosing between degrading and failing. The handler no longer resolves the grantee's roles at all — it omits per-ROW on the `GRANTED_VIA` value the query already computes. Both halves of the finding dissolve (no `%SYS` switch ⇒ no added privilege requirement; no lookup ⇒ no status to swallow), roughly 45 lines of security-sensitive code are gone, and the Finding B defect class becomes structurally impossible at any role-chain depth. Validated by an instance-wide sweep of all 46 users and roles (`SuperUser` rows emitted **iff** the principal effectively holds `%All`, zero counterexamples, `Admin` control at 0 derived rows) and by confirming the column-level query genuinely emits `SuperUser` (224 rows for `_SYSTEM`, 0 for `Admin`) so its filter is not inert. Re-mutation-verified RED on all three guarded paths against the NEW implementation. The row is therefore removed from the table above rather than left standing.

**Disposition tally (Rule #51 — mechanically counted from the Severity column above):** 6 deferred items — **0 HIGH · 0 MEDIUM · 6 LOW**. The five HIGH defects this review found were FIXED in-story, and `35-4-CR-1` was resolved in rework iteration 1, so none of them add anything here.

**Ledger state after this pass:** **0 HIGH / 30 MEDIUM / 92 LOW = 122 open** across **192 distinct items** (70 terminal), derived mechanically per Rule #51 from the prior state (0 HIGH / 30 MEDIUM / 86 LOW = 116 open / 186 distinct / 70 terminal): +6 LOW added, all open, all new; no item moved to terminal, and no MEDIUM was added because `35-4-CR-1` was resolved before it could be carried. So HIGH unchanged at 0; MEDIUM unchanged at 30; LOW 86 + 6 = 92; open 116 + 6 = 122; distinct 186 + 6 = 192; terminal unchanged at 70. Check: 0 + 30 + 92 = 122.

**Still no open HIGHs.** Both HIGH-opened findings from this review were closed in-story rather than ledgered, so the HIGH tier of Epic 35's beta charter remains satisfied.

**Lead smoke observation (2026-08-18) — `35-4-DEV-1` gates a whole guarded path, not just a parameter.** The rework note above cites the column-level query emitting 224 `SuperUser` rows for `_SYSTEM` as proof that the second loop's filter is not inert. That is correct, but it is only reachable with `system=1`. Driving the deployed route directly, `?grantee=_SYSTEM&target=%Dictionary.ClassDefinition` returns `count=0` with **no** `superUserPrivilegesOmitted` field at all, while the same call plus `&system=1` returns `count=0, superUserPrivilegesOmitted=224`. Since `resource.ts` never forwards `system` (which is precisely `35-4-DEV-1`), **the column-level `%All` guard cannot be exercised through the MCP tool at all** — only through the raw REST path. This does not make it dead code under Rule #54 (the REST route is a real, supported surface, and the guard demonstrably fires there), and it does not affect this story's ACs, all of which are met on both paths. But it means `35-4-DEV-1` is not merely "an optional filter is unavailable": it renders one of the two guards this story shipped unreachable from the tool layer, which is the surface nearly every caller uses. Re-weigh its LOW severity when it is next picked up, and note that the suggested fix (add and forward an optional `system` field) is what unblocks the column-level guard as well. Rule #37 re-deferral count unchanged at **0** — this is an annotation to an existing item, not a new one.

## Terminal disposition — Story 35.5 closes `35-SWEEP-6` and `35-SWEEP-7` (2026-08-18, dev pass)

| `35-SWEEP-6` | MEDIUM | **RESOLVED** — the sweep's causal story was wrong (premise correction, Rule #16/#42): `%SYS.OAuth2.Registration.RegisterClient` is **1-arg** on this instance, so the 2-arg call at the old `Security.cls:1792` died on ARITY (`<PARAMETER>`) before any server-definition lookup could run — client-create had never worked. The client-create branch was reworked to the real 2026.1 flow: resolve `serverName` as the exact issuer URL of an existing `OAuth2.ServerDefinition` via `OpenByIssuer` (clean precondition error naming BOTH prerequisites when absent — run `discover` first; discover of an https issuer needs an SSL/TLS config, inspectable via `iris_ssl_list` / creatable via `iris_ssl_manage`), create the `OAuth2.Client` record (ClientType defaults to `confidential`, SSLConfiguration inherited from the server definition, RedirectionEndpoint parsed via the in-file `%Net.URLParser` idiom), then call the 1-arg `RegisterClient(clientName)` and harvest `ClientId` on reopen (never `ClientSecret`, NFR6). The adjacent mis-shaped **discover** branch (2-arg call against the 3-arg signature, so `sslConfiguration` was always dropped and the mapped `configuration` was unreachable) was fixed with an additive `sslConfiguration` parameter (Constraint E-1 — no new tool/action key) bound as Discover's 2nd argument, the discovered server via the 3rd (Output) argument, and validate-before-delegating guidance for https-without-SSL. Mutation evidence (Rule #48/#59): old code re-deployed → raw `<PARAMETER>` on the wire for client-create and bare ERROR #6159 for discover-with-SSL-supplied (RED); fixed code re-deployed → clean precondition/guidance errors and a live successful discover of `https://accounts.google.com` returning the full documented configuration shape (GREEN). Fixture-backed leg: against a throwaway `OAuth2.ServerDefinition`, client-create saved the record and surfaced the clean `OAuth2NoRegistrationEndpoint`-class error (ERROR #8881) with a "record remains saved" note — never `<PARAMETER>`. All fixtures deleted; `OAuth2.ServerDefinition`/`OAuth2.Client` baselines verified 0/0 after every leg. Permanent coverage: `ExecuteMCPv2.Tests.OAuthClientValidationTest` (6 tests over the deployed route, total==6 Rule #35 — count corrected at code review: 4 dev + 1 QA + 1 review-added) + 6 new `oauth.test.ts` TS tests. |
| `35-SWEEP-7` | MEDIUM | **RESOLVED** — `iris_interop_rest`'s `name` description now states the real contract: an ObjectScript **package name** (e.g. `'MyApi'`, `'MyCompany.MyApi'`), validated by `%REST.API.CreateApplication` via `$ZNAME(name,4)`, with `'/myapi'` documented AS the rejected form. The interop README create example was wrong on three counts and is fixed on all three: the rejected `/myapi` name (now `MyApi`), the output shape (`"action":"create"` + `"status":"created"` → the handler's actual `{"action":"created","name":…}`), and a note naming the package-name contract. TS fixtures in `rest.test.ts` replaced with values the real server accepts (Rule #54). The AC 35.5.4 audit re-enumerated every `e.g.` in both touched packages mechanically (grep, 51 hits across 19 files — count corrected at code review from the dev-recorded 50/18; the story's starting list missed `audit.ts`, `ldap.ts`, `service.ts`, `message-resend.ts`, which is exactly why re-running rather than trusting the list matters): every other documented example is a value its validator accepts or pure prose; `SigningAlgorithm` example `RS256` verified against the live `VALUELIST`; no further defects found. |

The Rule #59 oracle held at BOTH layers: broken-syntax XML with `compile: true` surfaces the compile error in the per-file `status` (`ERROR #5475`/`#1001`), which the tool now surfaces as a `Per-file status (…)` note. | `35-SWEEP-9` | MEDIUM | **RESOLVED** — `iris_rest_manage:get` on a Mgmnt API 404 (matched by HTTP STATUS, never message text — #8/#13) falls back to the legacy webapp list via the existing `fetchLegacyApps` helper (#47): a legacy name resolves to `{name, dispatchClass, namespace, swaggerSpec: null, explanation}` (fullSpec deliberately has no effect); a name in neither scope fails with an accurate not-found naming BOTH scopes — the misleading "Check the IRIS web server configuration" text is never surfaced. Proven LIVE through the built dist against the REAL `/api/executemcp/v2` legacy app in HSCUSTOM (which the raw probe showed 404s at the Mgmnt API with a text/html gateway page), with `HS.FHIRServer.MFE.V1` as the unchanged spec-first control. `delete` stays spec-first-only by design: a legacy name fails with an explanation pointing at `iris_webapp_manage:delete` (verified live — the app survived the refused delete). |

## Terminal disposition — Story 35.7 closes `35-SWEEP-5` (2026-08-18, dev pass)

| Item | Severity | Disposition |
|---|------|-------------|
| `35-SWEEP-5` | MEDIUM | **RESOLVED** — All four DocDB tools now translate the disabled-`%Service_DocDB` failure into an actionable error naming the cause and BOTH remedy routes (Management Portal path, and `iris_service_manage` `action="enable"` with its governance catch + example `IRIS_GOVERNANCE` override). The translation is a catch-path enrichment (no happy-path round-trip): on an `IrisApiError` carrying a numeric `code: 822` in `errors[]` (locale-stable code, never message text — #8/#13; the real shape is HTTP 200 with a code-822 envelope, re-captured live this pass), the tool makes ONE follow-up `GET /api/executemcp/v2/security/service?name=%25Service_DocDB` and enriches only when the live state reads `enabled: false` — the grounded discriminator, since 822 is shared with genuine privilege failures (#54; `_SYSTEM` holding `%All` still gets 822 with the service off, re-confirmed). Pass-through is total and test-pinned: service enabled / check failure / non-822 / string-form-822 all return today's byte-identical error (Rule #19). Proven LIVE through the built dist tool layer (fresh-Node smoke, 31/31 PASS): all four tools actionable while disabled; the raw REST leg showed the exact 200-with-822 envelope; the service was then enabled via the direct REST route (MCP-governance-free by design), `iris_docdb_manage:list` genuinely succeeded (`["HS.BulkFHIR.Log"]`), and the service was RESTORED to disabled — restore verified three ways (service route `enabled:false`, `iris_service_manage:get`, and a post-restore tool call returning the actionable error again). Docs corrected at every point of use: the four tool descriptions, `iris-data-mcp` README (prerequisites + DocDB table note + namespace note + the error-table row whose "DocDB service not available" text never matched reality), and `tool_support.md`'s factually-wrong "typically enabled by default" claim (Mgmt API typically is; `%Service_DocDB` is NOT). `iris-mcp-all` README and root README checked — no change needed (no default-state or error-text claims there). |
